# Decisions log

Append-only, newest first. One entry per judgment call on this project's
science/config setup — not process fixes (those go in the assistant's
lessons file) and not things `branko/realcase/README.md`,
`CHANGES.md`, or `OPEN_ISSUES.md` already document in depth.

---

**2026-09-26, 13:37 (clock) — CLOUD PLACEMENT CONFIRMED AND TRACED: HERO2 SPREADS SHORT-LIVED CUMULUS EVENLY OVER THE WHOLE REGION — TOO LITTLE CLOUD IN THE NORTH AND EAST, TOO MUCH OVER THE CLEAR INN VALLEY; THE VALLEY CUMULUS IS MADE LOCALLY AT THE BOUNDARY-LAYER TOP OVER THE SLOPES, IN A 2.5–3.3 km LAYER THAT WRF KEEPS NEARLY SATURATED FROM THE NIGHT WHILE ICON DRIES IT. ICON CARRIES THE OBSERVED PATTERN, SO IT IS LOST INSIDE WRF. SIDE FINDING (VERIFIED): THE 3D CLOSURE'S PBLH IS THE TERRAIN HEIGHT + ≈ 17 m (A37).** (Elias: "Do 1 and 2" = independent cloud map; advected vs local.) Agents F (map) and G (origin); files `exp/x16_judge/agentF_*`, `agentG_*`, data `data/inca/`, figures `plot_output/diagnostics/cloud_placement/`.
- *Independent map (agent F):* GeoSphere INCA global radiation GL (1 km; the value ending at the stamp, not an hour mean — hour means as ½(GL(h−1)+GL(h)); pinned to four of the stations, r = 0.87 against the 7 it does not use) and an independent MTG-I1 FCI 0.6 µm cloud mask (EUMETView, 10-min, brightness > background + 30 grey levels, parallax-corrected 5.1 km). Transmission k = SW / WRF clear-sky SW. Regions 10–13 UT, k INCA / HERO2 / ICON, cloud fraction FCI / HERO2: Inn W 0.99 / 0.75 / 0.83, 0.41 / 0.38; Inn E 0.69 / 0.76 / 0.73, 0.67 / 0.34; range/Karwendel 0.65 / 0.75 / 0.70, 0.77 / 0.37; foreland 0.56 / 0.71 / 0.60, 0.87 / 0.44; south 0.82 / 0.84 / 0.82; lower Inn east 0.52 / 0.68 / 0.63, 0.94 / 0.45; box 0.68 / 0.76 / 0.71, 0.65 / 0.38. Spatial correlation of k with INCA (500 m / 5 km / 10 km): HERO2 0.45 / 0.58 / 0.67, ICON 0.63 / 0.77 / 0.84. At the stations, observed k vs model k: HERO2 r = 0.10, ICON 0.52; HERO2 cloud fraction 0.47 at clear vs 0.41 at cumulus stations (no contrast), FCI 0.24 vs 0.83. HERO2's cloud map decorrelates within 30 min (0.24 vs FCI 0.52–0.59); cells in bands along 35–45°.
- *Origin (agent G, 30-min frames, zones 11.30–11.85 E):* Inn-valley cloud from 11 UT has its base at the local CBL top (coupled fraction 0.82–0.85; base 2.66–2.74 km ASL over 1.25–1.55 km slopes), top 3.35–3.46 km; field motion ≈ 0 from 11 UT (from 08–11 UT it drifts ESE with the cloud-layer wind); Inn-box condensate: gross condensation 52–57 t s⁻¹ vs north-face import 11.5–16.5 t s⁻¹ (almost all above 2.5 km — not the shallow northerly); budget residual ≈ 12 t s⁻¹ rms (only the gross comparison is robust) → local production dominates, import ≈ ¼–⅓. Cloud layer 2.5–3.3 km at 11–13 UT: WRF RH 0.91–0.94, total water 5.1–5.2 g kg⁻¹, ≈ 0.5 K colder vs ICON RH 0.85–0.87, 4.75–4.94 g kg⁻¹. Morning: real.exe passes no condensate, but ICON's 01 UT humidity is ≥ 0.98 RH at 2–4 km in 88 % of foreland columns → a raised layer cloud (base 2.3–3.3 km, decoupled from a 50–150 m BL) forms within 30 min, as in ICON; ICON then clears the valley at 04–07 UT (cloud fraction 0.03 at 08 UT), WRF keeps it (0.64).
- *Reading (inferred):* the misplacement is WRF keeping the night-time moist layer at 2.5–3.3 km and filling it with scattered resolved cumulus from every heated slope, instead of ICON's (and the observed) terrain-locked cloud mass in the north and east and a clear Inn-valley channel. For the LE question: the Inn-W shading (k 0.75 vs 0.99) is ≈ 220–270 W m⁻² of sunlight and ≈ 100 W m⁻² of LE at the valley stations. Open: why WRF does not dry the layer at 04–07 UT (subsidence, cloud-top radiative cooling, boundary inflow); whether the too-strong northerly organises the bands (agent F's orientation hint vs agent G's local-production budget).
- *A37 (verified in-session):* `Diagnose_pbl_height` (`dyn_em/module_pbl3d_my.F` ≈ 4115) receives `grid%z_at_w` (height ASL) and tests `z_at_w ≤ 200` as if AGL; above 200 m terrain the search collapses and PBLH = the ASL height of the first layer top. HERO2 at 03 and 12 UT: PBLH − HGT = 15.1 / 16.9 / 18.0 m (1st / 50th / 99th percentile), range 200–3702 m. It feeds `Calc_scale_aware` (dxdh = 2.5Δx / min(PBLH, 3000)), which is ON (`pbl3d_scale_aware = 1`): the Honnert factor is computed from terrain height instead of BL depth — ≈ 0.92 on 2000 m crests and ≈ 1.0 on the floor with the bug, against ≈ 1.0 at night and ≈ 0.98 by day with a true depth (estimate). E45's "stale PBLH" was this bug; the 2026-09-22 Psig_bl values (0.90–0.96) and the 09-21 PBLH figures (1500/2200/560 m) are terrain heights. Fix proposal: compare z_at_w − HGT (or the closure's own AGL wall heights) behind a default-off switch.
Rating 8/10 research (placement confirmed by two independent products; the origin traced to a moist layer and local convection), 6/10 model (cause of the moist layer open; A37 a real but small-effect bug).

Surface SW cloud effect per zone 10–12 UT −220…−330 W m⁻²; first-order LE removed = EF·(1 − α)·ΔSW: western floor −106…−117 W m⁻² at 10–13 UT, all zones −58…−89 W m⁻² as a 10–16 UT mean.

Rating 8/10 research (a measured, observation-based energy term of the size of the gap, found with eleven independent sensors), 6/10 model (the cause of the cloud misplacement is open).

**2026-09-26, 12:51 (clock) — A36 FIXED (OPT-IN) AND VERIFIED: `noah_mp:noahmp_tv_conv = 1` MAKES NOAH-MP'S CANOPY ITERATION ENERGY-CONSERVING; DEFAULT 0 IS BIT FOR BIT THE OLD BINARY. THE FIX REMOVES 17–37 W m⁻² OF SPURIOUS SENSIBLE HEAT OVER VEGETATION AT 13 UT AND LEAVES LE ALMOST UNCHANGED. THE TWO PARAMETER IDEAS (NITROGEN FACTOR 1, JARVIS STOMATA) RAISE LE BY 20–85 W m⁻² BUT PUSH THE KOLSASS BOWEN RATIO BELOW THE OBSERVED BAND — NOT ADOPTED.** (Elias: "fix this and feel free to try your ideas with runs that are either smoke or at max 2 hours".)
- *Behavioural footprint:* nothing changes by default. New namelist key `noahmp_tv_conv` (group `noah_mp`, default 0) → module variable `NoahmpCanTempConvOpt` in Noah-MP's `ConstantDefineMod`, set each step in `module_first_rk_step_part1.F`; with 1, `SurfaceEnergyFluxVegetatedMod.F90` subtracts from the Newton residual the heat already stored by the earlier iterations of the step and books the storage of the whole step. Branch `3dpbl_noahmp_tvconv` (WRF-3DPBL, commits 630f63a9b, efba543bf) with Noah-MP branch `tvconv_A36` (fork, 0981e6d); built in a separate worktree `branko_tvconv` so HERO2's binary stayed untouched; the main tree's `main/wrf.exe` is NOT replaced yet.
- *First attempt failed, and why (trap E78):* booking the storage as C·(TV − TV_start) tripped Noah-MP's 0.01 W m⁻² balance check on all 640 ranks in the first step: Noah-MP runs in single precision (`kind_noahmp = 4`), TV ≈ 300 K carries ≈ 3·10⁻⁵ K resolution, and C = FVEG·HCV/Δt ≈ 8000 W m⁻² K⁻¹ at Δt = 2 s turns that into ≈ 0.2 W m⁻². Accumulating the small increments instead of differencing temperatures fixes it.
- *Gates (measured):* new binary with the switch off vs old binary, 12-min restart smoke from HERO2's 17 July 13 UT state, 5 × 128, devel QOS: IDENTICAL on all 332 variables at 13:06 and 13:12, twice (first build and the corrected build).
- *Verification of the fix (measured, whole domain, vegetated classes, 13:06–13:12):* residual R = FSA − FIRA − HFX − LH − GRDFLX, median, original → fix: dry crop −14.6 → −0.5, crop/wood −17.2 → −1.3, shrub/grass −31.3 → −3.7, needleleaf −33.3 → −0.3, mixed forest −28.4 → −0.6 W m⁻²; the canopy's true storage FVEG·HCV·dTV/dt (6-min mean) has medians −3.1…+0.5, and the correlation of R with it rises from 0.00–0.07 to 0.59–0.66. The budget is closed; R is now the canopy storage.
- *Effect at 13:12 (land response; the air has barely moved in 12 min), zone means, fix − control:* floor H −17, LE −6 W m⁻² (Bowen 0.68 → 0.62); floor forest H −37, LE −2; lee needleleaf H −31, LE −4 (Bowen 1.27 → 1.17); dry crop H −19, LE −14. As predicted, the bug inflated H and does not explain the LE deficit.
- *Parameter ideas (measured, same smokes):* FOLNMX 1.5 → 1.0 (nitrogen factor 1): LE +19…+43, H −18…−46, sunlit stomatal resistance roughly halved; `opt_crs = 2` (Jarvis): LE +34…+84 (forest +84), H −33…−89, lee shrub/grass unchanged. At the Kolsass cells the Bowen ratio goes 0.39 (control) → 0.28 / 0.19, below the observed 0.36–0.43 (Bowen-preserving closure; higher if the residual goes to H). The one flux tower therefore favours the control over both; the forests, where the partition is most doubtful, have no tower. Neither is adopted.
- *Running:* 2-h coupled pair from the same restart, 13 → 15 UT, 3 × 128 on `zen3_1024`, both on the new binary: control LE2C (8672444, switch 0) and fix LE2T (8672445, switch 1) — to see the fix's effect on the valley air and the crest–lee contrast. Logs `exp/x16_judge/smoke_land_*.log`, `A36_fix_storage_check.log`; script `wrf3dpbl-diag/smoke_land_compare.py`.
- *Next (not done):* replace the main tree's binary once HERO2 has finished (no job may be linking it; E16); decide with Elias whether `noahmp_tv_conv = 1` goes into the production namelist; report A36 upstream (NCAR/noahmp).
Rating 8/10 model (a verified conservation fix with bit-for-bit default), 6/10 research (the fix is small for the LE question; both parameter ideas rejected by the one tower).

ICON IS NO TARGET FOR LE.** (Elias: "is this offset of LE caused by a wrong surface state or the wrong interaction with the atmosphere? … not just the city … land cover or initial state? look for a bug in WRF"; five read-only Opus agents, reports in `exp/x16_judge/agentA_*` … `agentE_*`.)
- *Coupling excluded (measured, two ways):* (1) offline HRLDAS (Noah-MP 5.1, same options and table, 15-min step) driven by ICON's own surface series gives the SAME LE as coupled HERO2 — valley floor 10/12/14 UT offline 178/208/220, HERO2 177/219/236, ICON 366/369/358 W m⁻²; 99–113 % of the floor gap is already offline (agent A). (2) In the 13 UT starts, while the atmosphere is still ICON's, the vegetated classes already evaporate like HERO2 does after 12 h: floor dry crop LE 326 (X17, VPD 10.6 hPa) vs 322 (HERO2, VPD 14.8 hPa), crop/wood 292 vs 299 W m⁻² — a 4 hPa drier air leaves LE unchanged; the floor evaporative-fraction deficit is deepest at 09–10 UT, before the air diverges (agent B). The dry 2 m air is a consequence of the low LE, not its cause.
- *Initial state excluded (measured):* HERO2 starts from exactly the offline state (soil moisture 0–1 m 0.298/0.296 floor, 0.332/0.329 lee needleleaf; LAI, FVEG equal); WRF's soil is wetter relative to its field capacity than ICON's (Noah-MP stress factor 0.89 floor / 0.99 lee vs ICON SMI 0.74 / 0.91; Kolsass 0.88 vs 0.55). The HRLDAS→wrfinput transfer carries all states that matter on day 1 (agent C).
- *Static land cover excluded for vegetation (measured, ICON native lead-0 fields):* plant cover 0.65/0.71 floor, 0.56/0.75 lee; LAI within 0–15 %; loam in both; WRF has deeper roots (1.14 vs 0.93 m) and more plant-available water (299 vs 215 mm) — every difference has the wrong sign (agent E). **Towns:** the sealed half of each town cell evaporates 0 (natural half 254–298 W m⁻², cell 127–149); unsealing all town cells would add 58 of the 189 W m⁻² floor gap (≈ 30 %). **RETRACTED (10:54 entry):** "sealed area plausibly overestimated about twofold" — LANDUSEF shows town cells are 80 % urban classes; CORINE sealing degrees give 0.18–0.36 of the floor sealed against 0.23 as run; Innsbruck city's observed summer Bowen ratio is ≥ 2.5 (Ward et al. 2022), so a high town-cell Bowen ratio is realistic.
- *Where the gap is (measured):* floor at 10 UT: evaporative fraction 0.50 vs ICON 0.79 (≈ 55 % of the gap) and available energy H + LE 356 vs 462 (≈ 45 %; net radiation 505 vs 579, more to ground/urban storage); lee: equal H + LE, Bowen 1.17 vs 0.65 — partition only.
- *BUG (verified in-session):* Noah-MP v5's canopy temperature iteration (`phys/noahmp/src/SurfaceEnergyFluxVegetatedMod.F90:218–331`) exits on the temperature increment (|δTV| ≤ 0.01 K, `:329`) while the per-step canopy heat-capacity term FVEG·HCV/Δt ≈ 8000 W m⁻² K⁻¹ at Δt = 2 s dwarfs the flux derivatives (≈ 250), and the first iteration of every step is neutral (`ResistanceAboveCanopyMostMod.F90:87–89`); the unconverged remainder is booked as canopy storage, so the balance check passes. HERO2, 11 UT, FSA − FIRA − HFX − LH − GRDFLX = −36 (crop), −38 (needleleaf), 0.0 (barren) W m⁻² (medians, domain sub-box; floor mean −17.5); the offline run at 900 s has 0 exactly; a toy of the loop gives −33 / −9 / −2 / 0 W m⁻² at 2 / 18 / 60 / 900 s. ≈ 75 % of the spurious flux goes to H (+25…+40 W m⁻² at noon over vegetation), ≈ 15 % to LE; ≈ 10 W m⁻² at night. It raises H and the Bowen ratio and adds a little to the warm night; it does NOT explain the LE gap. → OPEN_ISSUES A36.
- *Design/parameter leads (not bugs):* Ball–Berry uses the leaf-surface humidity relative to saturation at the (hot) canopy temperature and Vcmax falls above ≈ 35 °C → a hot canopy transpires less (self-limiting); the foliage-nitrogen factor FOLN/FOLNMX = 1/1.5 caps Vcmax at ⅔ although the driver comment says 'nitrogen saturation' (`drivers/wrf/BiochemVarInTransferMod.F90:49,52`) — +15…20 % transpiration if 1; CBIOM cut 0.02 → 0.001 m in the 4.8 table (canopy heat capacity ÷ 20). Upstream trackers have no report of this symptom (agent D).
- *ICON is no LE target (sources, agent D/E):* TERRA's Jarvis humidity factor is disabled in the code (`zf_sat = 1`), its stomatal and bare-soil resistances are tuned by data assimilation to 2 m T/q (Zängl 2023), BATS bare-soil evaporation is too high on wet soil (Schulz & Vogel 2020); ICON is too moist by day against the sondes. Observed Kolsass meadow Bowen 0.40 (Bowen-preserving closure) … 0.76–0.89 (residual to H) vs HERO2's Kolsass cells 0.45–0.60 — inside the band; no forest tower exists.
- *Traps (KNOWN_ISSUES E77):* before 09 UT 17 July the ICON surface series is the 16 July 12 UT cycle (floor LE 20–37 % below the 00 UT cycle at 07–08 UT) — my morning comparisons against the series before 09 UT used the wrong cycle; in town cells TR/ETRAN/EDIR/EVG in the history are natural-tile values; over forest T2/Q2 sit ≈ 15 m above ground (displacement height). *Open (agent B):* at 01 UT HERO2's level 1 is +0.73 K warmer and 0.25 g kg⁻¹ drier than ICON's lowest native level while T2 = ICON (suspect: real.exe surface interpolation with a fill-valued met_em surface TT).
- *Proposed (not approved), offline first:* HRLDAS at NOAH_TIMESTEP = 2 s to reproduce the bug; a residual-based convergence fix behind a default-off switch; FOLNMX 1.5 → 1.0; `opt_crs = 2` (Jarvis) as a structural sensitivity; judged against the Kolsass closure band, station 2 m q/RH and the 18 July sondes; then a day-2 restart twin pair.
Rating 8/10 research (coupling, initial state and static land cover excluded by independent tests; a real conservation bug verified), 7/10 model.

**2026-09-26, 10:42 (clock) — IS A BOWEN-RATIO CHANGE JUSTIFIED? NOT BY THE FLUX TOWERS: THE ENERGY BALANCE CLOSES TO 0.70–0.78 AT KOLSASS AND HERO2 LIES INSIDE THE CLOSURE BAND; AT THE SLOPE SITES CLOSURE CANNOT BE COMPUTED FROM THE FILES ON DISK. WHAT DOES POINT AT THE PARTITION IS THE AIR: THE MODEL'S MORNING VALLEY AIR IS 15–30 % RH TOO DRY (VPD 2–4× OBSERVED AT 10 UT), SOIL WATER IS NOT LIMITING, AND NOAH-MP'S BALL–BERRY STOMATA CLOSE WITH VPD. RETRACTED: "LEE SENSIBLE HEAT 1.5–3× THE SONICS" AS EVIDENCE OF A MODEL ERROR.** (Elias: "What is the justification of changing the Bowen ratio? … the surface energy balance is famously not closed, especially in the mountains … why might the Bowen ratio be wrong here and why does ICON have so different values?") Logs `exp/x16_judge/ebc_closure_17jul.log`, `lee_bowen_controls.log`, `rh_vpd_check_17jul.log`.
- *Closure (Kolsass, measured):* C = (H + LE)/(Rn − G) = 0.78 / 0.70 / 0.67 over 08–10:30 / 10:30–13 / 13–16 UT (Rn from the four components, G from the plate without storage, so C is if anything overestimated in the morning); observed Bowen 0.38–0.43. Distributing the residual: by the measured Bowen ratio → H = 117 / 201 / 124 W m⁻²; all to H (the buoyancy-flux-attribution view) → H = 186 / 340 / 238 and Bowen up to ≈ 0.8. HERO2 at Kolsass 09–12 UT: H 113–174, Bowen 0.60–0.70 — inside the band. Eggen and Stanser Joch: their RAW CNR4 columns are sensor voltages (≈ 0 W m⁻² at noon as read), so closure there needs the calibrated product or the sensor factors — not computed. Consequence: the 10:16 entry's "1.5–3× the three lee-side sonics" is not evidence of a model error beyond the closure uncertainty — withdrawn as such (the numbers stand as raw EC values).
- *What sets the model's Bowen ratio on the lee (measured, 10–11 UT):* root-zone soil water at field capacity in both models (HERO2 SMOIS 0–1 m 0.325–0.338 m³ m⁻³, loam, reference 0.329; ICON 0.30–0.33) — no soil-water stress; needleleaf cells: transpiration ≈ 136–146 W m⁻², understorey soil evaporation ≈ 83–99, sunlit stomatal resistance 319–323 s m⁻¹, canopy 2.9 K warmer than the 2 m air; shrub/grass cells: canopy 7.5–8 K warmer than the air. **2 m VPD on the lee: HERO2 0.67–0.84 kPa, ICON 0.38–0.48** (floor 0.97–1.16 vs 0.66–0.84).
- *Air humidity against stations (thermohygrometers, no closure issue; cells within 1.5 km and ±25/40 m):* 10 UT RH obs / HERO2 / ICON: Jenbach 73 / 54 / 77 %, Kolsass 77 / 55 / 67, Eggen 88 / 60 / 66, Innsbruck 60 / 48 / 65, Kufstein 78 / 69 / 76; VPD obs 0.20–0.53 kPa vs HERO2 0.61–1.10 vs ICON 0.45–0.79. Already at 08 UT HERO2 and ICON are drier than observed (Jenbach/Kolsass/Eggen RH 93–96 observed, HERO2 77–80, ICON 78–85); by 12–14 UT the difference shrinks at Innsbruck/Jenbach and stays −8…−18 % at Kolsass/Eggen. Stanser Joch (crest) is humid in all three.
- *Reading (inferred):* the model's partition is not wrong because of a parameter in isolation; it is a coupled state. Air that starts the morning too dry (inherited in part from ICON) raises the VPD, Noah-MP's Ball–Berry stomata close with VPD (at the leaf-surface humidity), more of the energy goes to H, the air warms and dries further. ICON differs structurally: its land model (TERRA) has no separate canopy energy balance (no canopy 3–8 K above the air) and a Jarvis-type stomatal resistance, and its morning air is moister; its Bowen ratio of 0.6 on the lee is therefore not a target either. A Bowen-ratio "change" is justified only as a physical correction: (a) the humidity of the morning valley air (initial and nocturnal moisture), (b) Noah-MP's canopy exchange / stomatal response (e.g. the Jarvis option `opt_crs = 2`, the canopy-air coupling), tested offline against the humidity and the closure-corrected fluxes — not as a tuning of β.
Rating 7/10 research (the closure caution holds and removes a weak argument; the humidity check is independent and points at the coupled cause), 5/10 model.

**2026-09-26, 09:30 (clock) — RETRACTION: THE EVENING "ADIABATIC WARMING BY THE PLUNGING FLOW, +7 → +22 K h⁻¹" (09-20 14:30 point 4, restated in the 09-21 02:20 chain) WAS A FRAME ERROR IN `lee_barrier_physical.py`. IN A CONSISTENT FRAME THE RESOLVED FLOW WARMS THE EVENING LEE SLOPE BY ONLY +1.1…+1.7 K h⁻¹ AND COOLS THE FLOOR (−0.3…−1.1 K h⁻¹). THE DAYTIME LEE READING KEEPS ITS SIGN BUT CHANGES ITS CAUSE: ALONG-SLOPE ADVECTION, NOT DESCENT.** Found while reading the script's evening output for HERO2 (Elias's evening-transition question): its terms summed to **+22…+29 K h⁻¹ on the lee slope while θ fell 0.2–0.3 K h⁻¹**, and to +2…+3.4 K h⁻¹ on the floor against −0.7…−1.0 — a sanity-check failure. **Cause (KNOWN_ISSUES E75):** the script samples at fixed heights ABOVE GROUND and takes ADV_H along those surfaces, but pairs it with the Cartesian −w ∂θ/∂z. On a constant-AGL surface the consistent vertical term is −(w − **u**·∇h) ∂θ/∂z; the mixed frame adds −(**u**·∇h)∂θ/∂z, i.e. counts flow that follows the slope as descent through the stratification. Test (`exp/x16_judge/lee_budget_frame_check.log`, HERO2 and X26, 0–300 m AGL zone means, tendency = centred 30-min frame difference; SGS and radiation not in the sum):

| K h⁻¹ | lee 18 / 19 / 20 / 21 UT | floor 18 / 19 / 20 / 21 UT | lee 14 / 15 / 16 UT |
|---|---|---|---|
| old script sum, HERO2 | +25.7 / +25.9 / +24.9 / +15.5 | +3.4 / +2.5 / 0.0 / +0.2 | −8.0 / −9.3 / −1.9 |
| consistent advection, HERO2 | +1.4 / +1.1 / +1.7 / +1.3 | −0.4 / −0.7 / −0.9 / −0.4 | −1.2 / −1.1 / −0.5 |
| actual dθ/dt, HERO2 | −0.3 / −0.3 / −0.3 / −0.3 | −0.8 / −1.0 / −1.0 / −0.7 | +0.4 / +0.2 / −0.1 |
| old script sum, X26 | +22.7 / +29.1 / +25.9 / +19.3 | +3.3 / +2.4 / +1.1 / +0.9 | −2.0 / −7.2 / −4.4 |
| consistent advection, X26 | +1.1 / +1.4 / +1.3 / +1.3 | −0.3 / −0.8 / −1.1 / −0.6 | −1.0 / −1.2 / −0.8 |
| actual dθ/dt, X26 | −0.3 / −0.3 / −0.2 / −0.2 | −0.7 / −0.7 / −0.9 / −0.9 | +0.6 / +0.4 / −0.2 |

Against the tendency the consistent sums miss by −1.4…−2.0 K h⁻¹ on the evening lee slope, ±0.4 on the floor and +1.6 on the daytime slope; the terms left out (closure SGS −0.4…−0.7 on the evening slope, +1.7…+2.8 on the daytime slope; radiation ≈ −0.2) close the floor and the daytime slope and leave −0.6…−1.2 K h⁻¹ unexplained on the evening slope (30-min sampling, the ±50 m ∂θ/∂z) — against a 20–30 K h⁻¹ miss before. **What is withdrawn:** (a) "the evening warm bias is adiabatic warming by the plunging flow, delivered to the floor by horizontal advection (+4.3 K h⁻¹)"; (b) the link "plunges to the valley floor → adiabatic warming at +7 → +22 K h⁻¹ after sunset → the evening and nocturnal warm bias" in the 09-21 02:20 chain; (c) the daytime numbers "ADV_Z = −3.8…−1.7 K h⁻¹, the resolved descent cools the slope". **What stands:** the barrier is removed from below (direct θ: floor +1.68 K vs slope +0.71 K, 14–16 UT, 09-20); the closure warms the slope more than the floor (SGS is computed independently); the resolved flow does cool the sunlit lee slope by day (−0.5…−1.2 K h⁻¹), but by along-slope advection of lower-θ air, with the terrain-relative vertical part slightly warming (+0.4…+1.7). **What replaces the evening link (measured, 09:25 entry):** the floor starts the night 1.2–1.5 K warmer than ICON (18–19 UT) — made in the afternoon — and then cools 0.4–0.6 K more than ICON overnight. `lee_barrier_physical.py` fixed (ADV_Z terrain-relative; the old Cartesian term still printed as `-w*thz`); the fixed script reproduces the check (HERO2 19 UT lee −0.55, floor −3.33 K h⁻¹). Other scripts: `x16w_slope_diag.py` splits a frame-robust WRFlux total by a Cartesian term (consistent); `northerly_snapshot_budget.py` already carries the E61 frame warning. Rating: 8/10 for the project (removes a mechanism that the evening story rested on), 9/10 confidence in the retraction (exact coordinate identity, closes against the tendency in two runs).

**2026-09-26, 09:25 (clock) — HERO2: THE INCURSION IS ONE CONTINUOUS EVENT, NOT A STOP-AND-RESTART — OVER THE RANGE FROM BEFORE 10 UT TO 21–22 UT, ON THE FLOOR 12:00 → 19:30 UT WITH ONE 30-min LULL AT 13:00; ITS LATE-AFTERNOON PEAK (17:00–17:30) IS THE SAME AS IN EVERY LINEAGE, IT ONLY STARTS 4 h EARLIER AND ENDS 1 h EARLIER ON THE FLOOR. Crest v is −3.1…−5.6 m s⁻¹ without a break from 10 to 18 UT (maximum 17 UT in all runs, −5.5…−5.6; ICON −3.4…−3.7), decays to ≈ 0 by 22 UT (older runs −1.2 at 22 UT). **A second, nocturnal crest northerly appears only in HERO2 at 01–03 UT (−2.0…−2.5 vs older runs −0.7…−1.3, ICON −1.1…−1.3) and never reaches the floor (fN = 0 from 23 UT on).** Along-valley wind: Innsbruck–Hall has **no up-valley wind at all by day in HERO2** (a −1.3…+0.7 m s⁻¹, 10–19 UT; ICON +2.8…+5.3; X17 +4…+5 to 15 UT; Innsbruck station observed 3.9–5.7 m s⁻¹ up-valley 12–16 UT); Hall–Jenbach loses it during the peak (a 3.4 → 0.5 at 15:30 → 16:30) and **regains 3.2–3.9 m s⁻¹ at 18:30–20:00** once the incursion withdraws (X17 2.2–2.4, ICON 00 UT cycle 2.6–3.4), reversing to down-valley only at ≈ 23:30 (X17 ≈ 23:30, ICON ≈ 23:00).

*Evening transition against observations:*
- Kolsass lidar 50–150 m: the up-valley wind ends at ≈ 20:30–21 UT observed (0.2 m s⁻¹ at 21:00) and in X17 (0.6 at 21:00); HERO2 keeps 5.4 at 21:00, 2.9 at 22:00 and turns down-valley at 23:00 — 2 h too long.
- Radfeld lidar (along-valley, 18–21 UT means, 190–400/400–700/700–1000/1000–1500 m AGL): observed +0.5/−1.4/−2.9/−4.6, HERO2 +2.9/+2.1/+0.7/−1.4, X17 +2.9/+0.6/−1.6/−3.4; reversal observed 22 UT at the bottom and ≤ 17 UT above 400 m, HERO2 00/23/22/17 UT, X17 23/21/17/17 UT. HERO2's evening at Radfeld is the worst of the lineages aloft; its afternoon is the best (13–17 UT 190–400 m +3.9 vs +3.3 observed, X17 +2.4).
- Downslope onset at the north-facing i-Box slope sonics (Weerberg, Hochhäuser): observed 17:00/17:30, HERO2 17:00/17:30, X17 17:00/18:00; all runs 1–1.5 m s⁻¹ too strong 18–22 UT. Rinn (south-side terrace): drainage 3.4–4.4 m s⁻¹ in all runs vs 1.0–1.2 observed.
- Kolsass HATPRO 0–100 m, model − obs: HERO2 +0.6/+1.4/+2.3 K at 19:30/20:00/20:30 vs X17 +2.4/+2.7/+3.1 — the earlier withdrawal buys ≈ 1.5 K for an hour — then +2.9 (21 UT) and +3.4 (22 UT) in both.
- Floor θ 0–300 m against ICON on the same zones: all WRF runs start the night 1.2–1.5 K warmer than ICON (18–19 UT), a legacy of the afternoon; WRF then cools 0.4–0.6 K more than ICON overnight (22→05 UT), ending 0.5–0.9 K above it. Lee–floor θ contrast (after sunset the valley inversion): HERO2 2.11 K at 20 UT vs X17 1.36, ICON 3.08; all within 0.6–0.9 K of ICON by 22 UT.

**Reading (inferred):** the incursion sets the floor's warm start into the night (+1.2…+1.5 K over ICON at 19 UT), but ending it an hour earlier (HERO2) changes the night only for an hour at Kolsass; the 22–05 UT bias against the radiometers is then carried mostly by what ICON also has (A34). The persistent evening up-valley wind in the Kolsass–Radfeld reach is a separate evening defect of the same size in HERO2 as the incursion's legacy; it restarts because the incursion that had suppressed it withdraws while the along-valley thermal force is still up-valley (Radfeld thermal force 18–21 UT HERO2 +1.9 vs observed +0.7 × 10⁻³ m s⁻², 190–400 m).
Rating 7/10 research (answers the question with every frame and on each run's own terrain; the persistence is measured, the causal reading inferred), 5/10 model.

**2026-09-25, 16:00 (clock) — LITERATURE CONTEXT OF THE ONE-MASTER-LENGTH RULE (Elias asked; reading only).** Kosović et al. (2020, J. Phys. Conf. Ser. 1452, 012080) and Juliano et al. (2022, MWR 150) both write MY82's proportionality — the flux and dissipation scales l₁, Λ₁, l₂, Λ₂ = l·(A₁, B₁, A₂, B₂) of one master l (Blackadar with l₀ = α∫qz/∫q, α = 0.1; Kosović prints l = ∫qz/∫q) — with the BouLac-fitted constants (0.3, 8.4, 0.33, 6.4, 0.08), and Juliano's second option (Bougeault–Lacarrère) uses a flux scale l_k and a separate dissipation scale l_e. Our rule (`db3b9176c`: any limit on l reaches the fluxes, the q² diffusion and ε = q³/(B₁l) alike) restores that proportionality in the code where the inherited limiter had broken it (the nocturnal runaway, q² 46 → 150 m² s⁻² in one minute). But Juliano et al. (their section 4) call the proportionality itself "one of the fundamental limitations" (LES: inappropriate in convective and stable conditions, worst in the entrainment zone) and propose to revise it. Reading: separate scales must be derived and energetically consistent, not a by-product of limiting; a dissipation scale longer than the transport scale (BouLac's √(l_up l_down) ≥ min(l_up, l_down), and A33 gap 1) has the sign that ran away at night. **Inferred link to A31:** Juliano et al. report that with the MY82 constants "numerical stability is not maintained when using the full 3D model" under convection — plausibly the explicit scalar-transport mode we measured, which they avoided by changing constants; we run MY82 and now remove it numerically. Longer or decoupled scales (their direction; our `pbl3d_l_opt = 2/3`) were blocked by the explicit diffusion limit (X14, D up to 1.2); the implicit θ/qv/q² solve lifts that for scalars and q², momentum stays explicit — implicit u, v is the prerequisite for a length-scale experiment. Rating 7/10 (reading solid; the A31 link inferred).

**2026-09-25, ~14:30 (clock) — THE "MISSING NON-THERMAL FORCE" OF ~13:30 WAS THE KOLSASS i-BOX BAROMETER (≈ −0.1 hPa K⁻¹, ~1 hPa day–night): RETRACTED. VALLEY-WIDE (Innsbruck–Kufstein, 70 km) THE OBSERVED NIGHT DOWN-VALLEY FORCE EQUALS THE MODEL'S; THE MODEL'S DEFICIT IS THE AFTERNOON UP-VALLEY FORCE (65 %). THE REMAINING RADFELD–KOLSASS FORCE ERROR IS THERMAL AND STARTS WITH THE NORTHERLY INTRUSION.** (`wrf3dpbl-diag/valley_force_tests.py`, logs `exp/x16_judge/valley_force_tests.log`, `ibox_barometer_check.log`.)
- **The barometer (measured):** i-Box `pact` minus the Kolsass sonde's launch pressure (first record, 545 m): day (10–15 UT) −0.69 ± 0.22 hPa (n = 4), night (20–05 UT) +0.37 ± 0.14 (n = 7), 18 and 19 July; slope −0.096 hPa K⁻¹ against the tower air temperature. Against the GeoSphere network interpolated to Kolsass (Innsbruck–Jenbach, 650 m ASL) the residual rises +0.41 hPa from afternoon to night; the model's same residual changes by −0.015 hPa. The drift fully explains the −1.3 × 10⁻³ m s⁻² "non-thermal" change and more; the ~13:30 non-thermal numbers and the Alpine-scale mountain–plain hypothesis are withdrawn. Its effect on the HATPRO θ conversion (E72) is ±0.05 K — negligible. (Radfeld's own residual against Jenbach–Kufstein falls −0.23 hPa; its two barometers agree, so that is real structure or the T2 reduction — not a sensor.)
- **Station-pressure force, 650 m ASL (TAWES barometers, each reduced with its own T2; model: floor-cell box means at 650 m, Jenbach's model floor 563 m for a 529 m station):** Innsbruck–Kufstein obs +2.03 (afternoon 13–17 UT) → −0.20 (night 22–05) × 10⁻³ m s⁻², X17 +1.29 → −0.29, X12m +1.30 → −0.26. The T2-reduction error measured inside the model is ≤ 0.24 × 10⁻³ for this pair, but ±0.6–1.0 × 10⁻³ for the 33–37 km sub-pairs (not used).
- **The intrusion (decomposition of the model − obs thermal force at 700 m ASL, radiometer columns):** afternoon −0.20, intrusion window 16–19 UT +0.15, evening 19–22 +0.78, night 22–05 +0.76 × 10⁻³ (model too up-valley). The Kolsass-minus-Radfeld column error (560–900 / 900–1500 / 1500–2200 m ASL): 16–19 UT +1.27 / +0.71 / −0.68 K — the Kolsass column warm low down and cold aloft, which nearly cancel in the force; 19–22 UT +0.64 / +0.90 / +0.09; night +0.64 / +0.87 / −0.01 — the upper cold part gone, the low warm part kept. At night the error comes from the Kolsass column (+1.14) more than from Radfeld's (−0.39). **Inferred:** the Kolsass-reach warm anomaly that appears with the intrusion and persists all night gives the model ≈ 0.8 × 10⁻³ m s⁻² too much up-valley (too little down-valley) thermal force between Kolsass and Radfeld — the intrusion is the most likely source of the remaining force error (timing and location; causality not tested).
- **Sondes above the valley at night (model − obs θ, 1000–2200 / 2200–3000 / 3000–4000 / 4000–5500 m ASL):** Innsbruck 02 UT X17 +0.51 / +0.23 / +0.98 / +0.15; Kolsass 05 UT +0.46 / +0.11 / −0.56 / −0.91; Oberschleißheim 23 UT −0.50 / −0.67 / +0.02 / −0.04 (X12m similar). Above ridge level the model is within ~0.2 K over the inner Alps; the night warm bias lives in the valley atmosphere below ~2200 m.
- **Footprint:** analysis and records; no code of the model or judges. `radfeld_profile_station.py`'s barometer section is marked invalid (E73). Ratings: barometer diagnosis 8/10 project (prevents a false mechanism), station-pressure force 7/10, intrusion decomposition 7/10, sondes aloft 6/10.

Obs thermal +1.91 → −0.48 (−2.39); X17 thermal +1.71 → +0.28 (−1.43, 60 %). Obs non-thermal +0.02 → −1.28 (**−1.30**); X17 non-thermal −0.45 → −0.57 (**−0.12**).

**2026-09-25, 09:30 (clock) — IMPLEMENTED (Elias: "no gate, but add the implicit solver"): `pbl3d_scalar_implicit`, BACKWARD-EULER VERTICAL TURBULENT TRANSPORT OF θ AND qv IN THE CLOSURE. DEFAULT 0 = BIT-FOR-BIT THE PREVIOUS CODE PATH. REBUILD STARTED.**
- **Design.** In `Vertical_turb_mix` the explicit coupled tendencies of θ and qv are computed exactly as before (`Vertical_mixing_s_with_flux`); with the switch on, `Vertical_mixing_s_implicit` turns each into a backward-Euler one. At every interior face the closure's flux F is split into a down-gradient part −K ∂φ/∂z with K = −F/(∂φ/∂z) where that is positive (capped at K Δt rdz² = 100) and an explicit remainder (counter-gradient part, the part above the cap, surface and top faces). The solve is for the increment δ: (I + b D_K) δ = Δt T_explicit / m with b = −g Δt/(Δη m), m = c1h μ + c2h, the same face densities, metrics and loop limits as the explicit operator; tendency = m δ/Δt. The right-hand side is the explicit increment, so the scheme returns the explicit tendency where K Δt rdz² ≪ 1, and solving for δ keeps single-precision round-off (≈ 3 × 10⁻⁵ K at 300 K) out of the tendency. φ = the closure's own temporary θ and qv (`th_tmp`, `qv_tmp`), whose gradients produced F.
- **Soundness.** (i) The matrix is diagonally dominant with non-positive off-diagonals (an M-matrix): the implicit part is unconditionally stable and cannot overshoot, so the 2Δt/2Δz mode of A31 (growth −1.3…−2 per step, effective D ≈ 0.6–0.75) is damped instead of amplified. (ii) Column integral conserved: the implicit fluxes telescope between interior faces; the surface flux and the top face are unchanged. (iii) Counter-gradient fluxes stay explicit, so a counter-gradient term the closure produces is not smeared by a negative K. (iv) Requires `pbl3d_nsteps = 1` (fatal otherwise; HERO and production use 1). (v) Not included: momentum (the mode is measured in θ/qv only; u, v are staggered and would need their own face K), hydrometeors and tracers (`pbl3d_scalar_mix`), and `q_sq` (its own `pbl3d_sq_implicit`, recommended on together). Cost: one tridiagonal solve per column and scalar, ≲ 1 % of a step (the q² analogue measured 1.540 vs 1.541 s/step).
- **Footprint.** New Registry `rconfig pbl3d_scalar_implicit` (default 0), optional arguments to `Vertical_turb_mix` passed from `module_first_rk_step_part2.F`, validator and template entries (template 0). Old binary kept as `main/wrf.exe.pre_scalar_implicit_20260925`. HERO segment b / link c stay held during the build (E16).
- **Gates.** (1) Bit-for-bit on defaults: SMOKEDEF (5 × 128, 13:00–13:10) against its 2026-09-22 archive 8659688 made by the current binary. (2) Devel smoke with the switch on: finite, changes confined to where K Δt rdz² is not small. (3) Science test: the crashed HERO configuration, cold start on its layout (2 × 128), with the switch on (and a twin with `pbl3d_sq_implicit = 1` too), judged by the odd–even mode counts against HDIAG and X26, not by surviving 03:50.

| 03:30 | 98 / 580 / 22.0 | 53 / 515 / 24.8 | 46 / 287 / 26.9 |
| 04:00 | crashed 03:50 | 55 / 717 / 16.8 | 139 / 459 / **194** |

  HERO a (only `t2_scalar = 1` added) passed 03:50 and is at 04:17+; HC2 (only the roughness removed) passed 03:50 and finished 04:10 — but with a 194 g kg⁻¹ m s⁻¹ spike at 04:00, four times the crashed run's worst. Survival past the marginal minute is mostly the changed trajectory. The scalar back-off halves the vapour side and leaves the heat side; the roughness lowers the heat side and not the vapour side. Neither removes the mechanism; the crest roughness stays (design, no reason to drop it).
- **Retraction/correction (E70):** "state is lost at the restart, not last-bit growth" (02:55) is wrong. HR0B (full 1-min stream): at the restart time every prognostic and surface field is identical (only the solver diagnostics start at zero; `PBL3D_T2_STEPS` is reset every call, so it carries no memory); one minute later nearly all cells differ but by tiny amounts (median |Δθ| 6 × 10⁻⁵ K, |Δqv| 2 × 10⁻⁹, |Δp| 0.26 Pa), with large local jumps only where the odd–even mode is active (θ 8.2 K, p 4.8 hPa at i 243, j 285, k 7). A small perturbation at the restart, spread within a minute and amplified by the mode. Its seed is not identified.
- **Where the loop stands.** Cause found in the sense that matters for a fix: an explicit-time instability of the closure's vertical scalar flux in thin (≈ 19 m), strongly turbulent (`q_sq` 15–18 m² s⁻²), saturated crest layers, whose effective diffusion number (≈ 0.6–0.75 from the growth factor) is 2–3 × the closure's own `q l Δt/(2Δz²)` estimate; production has it at 1/2–1/3 of HERO's amplitude. No existing switch removes it (`pbl3d_dn_max = 0.4` is inert because its proxy never reaches 0.4). Candidate fixes — for Elias, not implemented: (a) implicit (tridiagonal) vertical scalar transport in the closure, as already exists for q² (`pbl3d_sq_implicit`); (b) a limiter on the per-step scalar flux divergence; (c) `pbl3d_dn_max` evaluated with the effective scalar diffusivity instead of `q l/2`. Each needs the standing gates (soundness review, rebuild, bit-for-bit on defaults, devel smoke). Rating: 8/10 for the project, 7/10 for the model.

**2026-09-25, 02:00 (clock) — HERO'S CRASH REPRODUCED BIT FOR BIT AND THE MECHANISM MEASURED: A 2Δt / 2Δz ODD–EVEN MODE OF THE CLOSURE'S EXPLICIT VERTICAL SCALAR TRANSPORT, 100–140 m ABOVE A 2550 m CREST, GROWING ×1.3–2 PER STEP FOR 50 s; RADIATION ONLY MET THE NaN. FIVE RESTART TWINS QUEUED TO FIND WHICH SWITCH STOPS IT.**
- **Reproduction.** HDIAG (8667065, HERO's layout 2 × 128, HERO's crashed configuration) died at the same step (last completed 03:50:00, SIGSEGV in RRTMG-LW) on four of HERO's five ranks (35, 36, 51, 52; not 231 — HERO's fifth rank was collateral). Per-step frames: everything finite at 03:49:58; at 03:50:00, 181 columns (i 148–166, j 83–101, centroid i 157, j 92) are NaN in T, QVAPOR, P, PH, W, U, V and MU — born in one step, spread by the acoustic solve.
- **The mode (per-step frames at i 157, j 92, k 5–9, 100–160 m AGL, dz 19 m).** The level-7 vapour anomaly relative to its neighbours alternates sign every 2-s step and grows: +0.07, −0.10, +0.13, −0.19, +0.40, −0.74, +0.92 g kg⁻¹ in the first 12 s from 03:49:00 (ratio −1.3 to −2.1 per step), with θ anti-phased (moist–cool vs dry–warm, i.e. transport overshoot, not condensation). It saturates, relaxes and restarts; at 03:49:54 −4.5 g kg⁻¹, at 03:49:56 vapour 26.9 g kg⁻¹ at level 7 and 0.0 at level 8 (θ 290.3 / 323.2 K), at 03:49:58 θ 135.2 / 400.2 K, then NaN. Vertical wind there < 0.6 m s⁻¹; closure condition number 20–190 (well conditioned); `q_sq` 15–18 m² s⁻² (TKE ≈ 8 m² s⁻²). - **Why it is an instability of the explicit flux (inferred from the growth factor).** For explicit diffusion the 2Δz mode is amplified by 1 − 4D; the measured −1.3…−2 per step means an effective D ≈ 0.6–0.75. The closure's own estimate D = q l Δt/(2Δz²) at these faces is 0.10–0.30 in every 1-min frame 03:30–03:49 — the actual scalar flux is 2–3 × `q l/2`. Proposed feedback (not yet shown): the zig-zag makes every other face statically unstable, the stability functions raise the heat/vapour diffusivity on exactly those faces, the flux overshoots more.
- **Twins (restart from HDIAG's 03:30 restart, same layout, 40 min to 04:10 = 20 min past the crash; clones of HDIAG, not the template):** HR0 control 8667299; HR1 `pbl3d_t2_scalar = 1` 8667300 (A18: escalate when θ_v′² ≤ 0 / heat flux violates Cauchy–Schwarz — the unstable faces of the mode); HR2 `pbl3d_dn_max = 0.4` 8667301 (existing cap on l from the explicit diffusion number); HR3 class-19 `Z0MVT` 0.30 → 0 8667302 (the crest roughness doubles u*); HR4 `diff_6th_slopeopt = 1` 8667303. **2026-09-24, 23:30 (clock) — HERO'S 03:50 CRASH, ANALYSED WITHOUT A RERUN: RADIATION IS THE VICTIM, NOT THE CAUSE; A NaN IN THE ATMOSPHERIC STATE WAS BORN IN THE LAST COMPLETED STEP (03:49:58 → 03:50:00); THE BIRTH PROCESS IS NOT IDENTIFIABLE FROM THE 30-MIN FRAMES.** Elias asked for the cause without a rerun.
- **Code (measured).** In RRTMG-LW every pressure/temperature index is clamped (`jp` 1–58, `jt`/`jt1` 1–4, `indself`/`indfor`/`indminor`); the only unclamped index is the spectral `js = 1 + int(8·colh2o/(colh2o + r·colco2))` (12 sites in `taumol`). With the build's flags (`-O2 -ftree-vectorize -funroll-loops`, tested) `max(0., NaN) = NaN` (the driver's vapour clip passes NaN) and `js` from a NaN is −2³¹+1 → segfault. A negative layer thickness does not do it (numerator and denominator change sign together); zero thickness would (0/0). So the fatal input is a NaN in vapour or pressure of some column. A NaN temperature alone does not crash `taumol`.
- **Timing (measured + inferred).** The last completed step is 03:50:00; the crash is in the next step's radiation call (`radt = 1`). The surface layer checks every step for NaN (the 01:01 crash was caught that way), and a NaN anywhere in a column reaches its lowest level within a step through the implicit acoustic solve — so the NaN was born in the step ending 03:50:00 (inferred), and the crash in radiation is the 1-in-30 chance that the next step was a radiation step. Step times flat at 1.81 s to the end; no warning on any rank after 03:30.
- **Where.** Four of the five crashed ranks share one corner (i 152|153, j 95|96): the NaN sat within the halo width of that corner. Eight cells away is the domain's strongest updraft, 12.5 m s⁻¹ at 3.9 km over a 2469 m crest (46.52 °N, 10.79 °E), persistent since 02:30, statically stable (no overturning in the patch), under 20–30 m s⁻¹ south-westerly shear above 4.3 km. The fifth rank (i 264–300, j 438–469, valley floor with 750 urban cells) is 350 cells away and shows nothing at 03:30 (|w| ≤ 1.8, q² ≤ 1.8).
- **HERO against clean runs (measured).** Closure solve: cells with condition number > 1e4 are 440–590 in HERO vs 33–47 in X7 at the same hour (01 UT start) and 22–45 in X26 at 20–22 UT; HERO's maximum grows 1.2e5 → 2.6e5 → 1.6e6 (02:30 → 03:30), X26's stays ≤ 3.2e4. **Retracted within the analysis:** "300–400× more realizability flags than X26" — X7 at night has 72 000 flagged cells, HERO 150 000 (×2); the X26 comparison mixed evening with night.
- **Excluded by code reading:** the urban canopy's shadow model at grazing sun (shadow length capped at the road width, no division by cos z); the slope-radiation adjustment (guarded at SW↓ > 1e-3 and cos z > 1e-4); the SW interpolation (`swint_opt` off). **Not excluded:** a finite but absurd SW heating at first light (the corner patch's sunrise, extrapolated from `COSZEN`, falls 03:47–03:50; the valley patch's 03:37–03:39); the closure's ill-conditioned solve; the wave updraft. `RTHRATSW`/`RTHRATLW` and the canopy temperatures are not in the history stream.
- Rating: 6/10 for the project (radiation cleared, the NaN's birth step pinned, the source not), 5/10 for the model.

| length floor only | `pbl3d_l0_min` 8 → 0 | NaN at 01:00:52 |
| moist condition cap only | `pbl3d_moist_cond_max` 0 → 1e4 | NaN at 01:01:14 |

**Mechanism (from the reference twin's 1-min frames).** The level-2 start seeds `q_sq` (twice the TKE, m² s⁻²) in sheared layers of the free troposphere: at 01:00 its maximum is 516 at 4.4 km, 673 at 6.8 km, 1600 at 10.5 km AGL, with master length scales up to 844 m; the floor start has 1e-5 everywhere. Within one minute the seed spreads: at 01:01, 37–51 % of cells between 2.5 and 6.8 km carry `q_sq` > 10 and the maximum sits at the 1000 cap on every level from 1.3 km up (floor start at 01:01: max 8). The runaway reaches the first level in about 36 steps, and the surface layer gets a NaN temperature (the NaN cells report the 0.1 m s⁻¹ wind floor, i.e. a NaN wind). The near-surface stable night is not the trigger: the crash cells had `q_sq` of 0.1–3.7 at 100–200 m at 01:00. **Retracted:** my 17:40 suspicion of the filter option, the urban canopy or the soil input — each excluded by its own twin.

**Why the August test survived and this one does not is not established** (the August equilibrium start ran before the strain cap, pairing and filter changes; the level-2 iteration's `l0` integral over a column with q² ~ 10² aloft is the obvious lever). Not fixed; the switch stays default-off. **HERO is not requeued** — Elias decides between `pbl3d_init_opt = 0` (X26 lineage, measured clean here) and a repaired equilibrium start. Also fixed: `clone_twin_rundir.sh` dropped a parent's own table files (only symlinks were cloned), so the first four twins died reading an empty Noah-MP table (KNOWN_ISSUES E67). Twins `innval_pbl3d_HD0…HD6` (≈ 12 GB each) left on disk. Rating: 8/10 for the project (a one-switch attribution in 40 min of devel time, cause of the runaway itself only described), 6/10 for the model (the start option is now known unsafe; no repair).

**2026-09-22, 14:40 (clock) — HERO-RUN DESIGN, ELIAS'S DIRECTIONS AND THE CHECKS THEY TRIGGERED: START 17 JULY 01 UT (LEAD 1 OF THE 00 UT ICON RUN, ONE RUN TO 19 JULY 00 UT), ICON TERRAIN, THE SOUND LAND-COVER SET, TOWNS AS URBAN — BUT THE X19a FORM OVERSHOT AND THE CLASS SEMANTICS ARE SWAPPED.**
- **Start time (Elias).** The 18th is the target day; the 17th afternoon must be right first; ICON's 3-D fields exist from lead 1 (lead 0 is surface/invariants only, `icon-data` skill), so the earliest analysis-like initial state is 17 July 01 UT from the 00 UT run. One 48-lead run covers 01 UT → 19 July 00 UT with no forecast switch (the current forcing is the 17 July 12 UT run, leads 1–35). Alternative for the second day's boundaries: switch to the 18 July 00 UT run at lead 1 — fresher, but a discontinuity at 01 UT. Default: single run. Land state exists for both terrains at 01 UT (`spinup_winter_july` and `spinup_winter_icontopo_july`, `RESTART.2025071701`). **Prerequisite: no ICON data before 17 July 13 UT on disk** — fetch the 20250717_00 run (48 leads), `icon2wrf`, WPS on `geogrid_icontopo`, `real.exe`, land injection. Length 47 h → six 8-h segments at 3:15 wall (5 × 128, full output); ≈ 1.8 TB per run with 30-min history and WRFlux.
- **ICON terrain (Elias: why exclude?).** It was excluded only as a *cause* of the break-in (X12mt reproduces it). For the hero run it is the consistent choice: the forcing is in balance with ICON's surface (crests 63 m higher, 25 m rms after remap, Nh/U and dividing-streamline height identical to ICON), the ICON-terrain land spin-up and `geogrid_icontopo` exist. Included.
- **Land-cover set (Elias: keep the sound ones).** X20's table minus the inert class-19 vegetation edit (Noah-MP's `ISBARREN` ignores its LAI row): needleleaf `MP` 6→9, `VCMX25` 50→60 (Bowen 2.10→1.27, FLUXNET conifer 1.07); deciduous/mixed `VCMX25` +20; class 3 redefined as mountain meadow (LAI 4.5 Jun–Aug) with the wrfinput relabel 2→3 above 700 m (recorded as `A30_LU_EDIT`; to be redone on the new wrfinput); plus `Z0MVT(19) = 0.30` (X23: crest Cd₁₀ to ICON's, 10 m wind on ICON). Included.
- **Towns (Elias: why exclude, if physically correct?).** Measured now: the CORINE extension covers **39.5 % of the Inn-valley floor cells** (31: 1.6 %, 32: 30.9 %, 33: 7.0 %; 4.1 % of all land). Mapping by example: Innsbruck centre and Hall → 31 (CORINE 111 continuous fabric), Höttinger Au, Schwaz, Wattens → 32 (112 discontinuous), Jenbach → 33 (121 industrial). **In USGS-33 semantics 31 = low-intensity and 32 = high-intensity residential — the project's mapping is the reverse**, so any standard urban parameter table (URBPARM.TBL: impervious fraction 0.5 / 0.9 / 0.95 for 31 / 32 / 33) would give the 31 % of the floor that is discontinuous fabric 90 % impervious cover. X19a's form (`URBTYPE_beg = 30`, Noah-MP bulk urban flag, `sf_urban_physics = 0`) has no urban fraction at all: the cells become bare, dry, 1-m-rough surfaces, and the floor-class sensible heat at 14 UT went 151 → 273 W m⁻², i.e. town cells ≈ 450 W m⁻² against 150–300 observed over European towns of this density. That is why it was set aside — an overshoot by ~2×, not a wrong sign. **The physically correct form is the single-layer urban canopy model (`sf_urban_physics = 1`, standard with Noah-MP) with the impervious fraction per class, after swapping the 31/32 semantics (edit URBPARM.TBL rows or relabel 31↔32 in wrfinput).** Cost: a devel smoke (SLUCM + Noah-MP + the 3D closure is an untested combination here) and a 13→18 UT twin to see the floor heat and the Kolsass valley wind. Recommended as a conditional inclusion after that smoke, not blind.
- **Not included:** `pbl3d_lh_opt` (negligible by construction), X25's length/TKE settings (worst plunge), the 2 K km⁻¹ gate, the untapered filter, the urban bulk flag.
- **Control:** same initial state, terrain, table and forcing; production filter (`slopeopt = 1`). Judge on `afterany`: 13:30 bands, 14 UT crest wind, 17 UT floor break-in and Kolsass lidar column, lee barrier, 22 UT slope θ, 01 UT 0–100 m bias vs HATPRO, and the same set on the 18th.

- **Behavioural footprint.** Nothing changes by default: `diff_6th_slopeopt` keeps 0 (Registry) / 1 (production namelist); the new Registry real `diff_6th_hfxthresh` (20 W m⁻²) and the reused `diff_6th_stabthresh` (set 10 K km⁻¹ for option 5; the validator warns below 5) are read only when option 5 is on. HFX and perturbation θ are threaded as OPTIONAL keyword arguments to `sixth_order_diffusion` through `rk_tendency` and all six `rk_scalar_tend` call sites (moisture, scalars, chemistry, tracers, TKE now receive θ too, so options 3 and 5 act on every filtered field alike — Elias's requirement). ~150 lines in `module_big_step_utilities_em.F`, all inside the `slopeopt ≥ 1` branch; the option-5 block recomputes the raw 5th difference itself, so the existing path is untouched.
- **What the code does per face** (x and y alike; p0 = face i−1|i, stencil i−3…i+2): zlev = height of the field's own level (mass, u-, v- or w-staggered), dfdz = centred vertical gradient of the field; c = ½(dfdz_{i−1}+dfdz_i)·δ⁵zlev; the raw δ⁵f is corrected to δ⁵f − c only if same sign and |c| ≤ |δ⁵f| (the pair difference likewise), then the monotonic limiter; b = clip(stencil-max ∂θ/∂z / stabthresh); if min HFX over the six columns > hfxthresh: flux = (1−b)·corrected + b·taper·(production flux), else taper·(production flux). `slopedamp` is set to 1 because the taper is already inside the flux.
- **Two mishaps on the way, both mine, no damage.** (1) The first build started on an unmodified tree because the edit script failed an assertion after the build command had been queued in the same shell — killed; `./clean -a` had already removed `main/wrf.exe`, which the rebuild restores. (2) A `pkill -f` pattern matched its own shell and killed the session's command before the edits ran (E2 again, for `pkill`). Rule added below: kill build processes by PID list, never by pattern; never chain "edit && build" in one shell.
- **Gates armed** (`branko_runs/gate_opt5_after_build.sh`, waits on the build PID): SMOKEDEF (defaults, new binary, 5 nodes, 13:00–13:10) → bit-for-bit against X22 and the 09-21 SMOKEDEF archive at 13:05/13:10; SMOKEG5 (`slopeopt 5`, `stabthresh 10`, `hfxthresh 20`; run dir cloned from SMOKEGL5) → must differ, be finite, and act mainly on steep terrain. Then the hero run (below).
- Rating: model 7 (implemented as designed, default-off; unverified until the gates pass), research n/a.

**2026-09-22, 16:40 (clock) — CAN THE FILTER TELL THE REAL SLOPE-WIND LAYER FROM UNPHYSICAL < 6 Δx FLOW? YES, BY CONSTRUCTION OF THE η FILTER: A TERRAIN-FOLLOWING LAYER HAS 4–14× LESS ALONG-η STRUCTURE THAN A HORIZONTALLY STRATIFIED LAYER WOULD IMPRINT, AND THE SPURIOUS v STRUCTURE GROWS 10× IN 30 MIN ALONG η. WHAT NO SPATIAL CRITERION SEPARATES IS REAL GRID-SCALE COLD-AIR STRUCTURE AT NIGHT — WHICH THE REGIME GATE COVERS.** `filter_gate_design.py` (rewritten; laws `slim`, `slim2`, `rs2`, `rs2gN`), logs `exp/x16_judge/filter_gate_design_{slim,slim2,rs2g10}_*.log`.
- **Measured discriminator.** For every stencil face, the along-η 5th difference d of θ (or v) is compared with the imprint c = (∂f/∂z)_face · δ⁵z that a horizontally stratified layer leaves on an η surface over rough terrain (δ⁵z = the same 5th difference of the level height). Over the crests:
  | layer | 13:00 θ: var d / var c / corr | 13:30 θ | 22:00 θ | 13:00 v | 13:30 v |
  | 0–50 m AGL | 3.0 / 1.0 / 0.44 | 34 / 146 / 0.01 | 69 / 197 / −0.06 | 3.0 / 0.4 / 0.13 | 47 / 169 / 0.17 |
  | 0–300 m | 2.0 / 0.6 / 0.38 | 16 / 32 / 0.01 | 23 / 42 / −0.04 | 3.1 / 3.4 / 0.39 | 32 / 43 / 0.12 |
  | 300–1500 m | 1.8 / 1.4 / **0.88** | 2.3 / 1.5 / 0.49 | 1.9 / 2.3 / **0.64** | 1.5 / 2.9 / 0.58 | 9.4 / 4.5 / 0.20 |
  Above the surface layer the along-η structure IS the stratification imprint (corr 0.6–0.9, variances equal): that is what the taper rightly protects, and a correction can remove it exactly. In the surface layer, by day (skin layer) and by night (katabatic / slope-inversion layer), the θ field along η has 4–14× less structure than the imprint and no correlation with it: **these layers are terrain-following, the η filter does not see the layer itself.** The nocturnal damage of the untapered η filter (−1.06 K h⁻¹ on steep slopes 0–50 m) is therefore NOT the inversion being mixed across the slope; it is real along-η grid-scale cold-air structure (69 K² of 5th-difference variance at 22 UT vs 34 at 13:30) being removed — physical, terrain-locked, unresolvable, and indistinguishable from noise by any spatial test. It exists only in the stable regime ⇒ the regime gate is the discriminator that works for it.
- **The spurious v structure is along-η and fast:** the along-η 5th-difference variance of v at crest 0–300 m goes 3.1 → 31.5 m² s⁻² from 13:00 to 13:30 (10×) with the imprint uncorrelated (0.12) — grid-scale structure of the wind growing on minutes is what the η filter is for; no physical terrain-locked structure grows like that. The Zängl (true-horizontal) filter is the wrong tool inside the boundary layer for the same reason the η filter is right: on a horizontal it sees the terrain-following skin layer as a 2–4 Δx contrast (+0.35 K h⁻¹ at 13:30, 14:10 entry).

(`slim`, subtracting min(|c|,|d|) always, produced a −0.9 K h⁻¹ directional bias in the skin layer at 13:30 — the asymmetric limiter zeroes one sign preferentially; withdrawn.) `rs2` = slim2 in the convective regime (all six stencil columns HFX > 20 W m⁻²), production taper otherwise. **`rs2g10` = rs2, blended back to the tapered flux by the stencil-max ∂θ/∂z / 10 K km⁻¹** (the morning residual inversion is terrain-following too — |c| ≫ |d| at 08 UT — so slim2 cannot protect it; the inversion gate does).

The 6–12 Δx band is drained together with 2–6 (τ 47 / 13 min): partly wanted (WRF's 6–12 Δx grew +71 % in 15 min while ICON's was flat).

The lever remains the untapered filter (X21b) during the day; any gate must open on a daytime criterion (e.g. surface heat flux or local ∂θ/∂z of the cell pair with a looser threshold) — to be decided when the full pair and the judge's night rows are in. **2026-09-21, 12:10 (clock) — IMPLEMENTED ON BRANCH `3dpbl_lh_and_filtergate`: `diff_6th_slopeopt = 3` (STABILITY-GATED FILTER TAPER) AND `pbl3d_lh_opt = 1` (HORIZONTAL RESTORATION OF THE GREY-ZONE TAPER). BOTH DEFAULT TO THE PREVIOUS BEHAVIOUR BIT FOR BIT. REBUILD RUNNING ON THE LOGIN NODE (PID IN `.build_login.pid`).** Commit `9e8780f0d`.

**Behavioural footprint.** Nothing changes by default: `diff_6th_slopeopt` keeps its default 0 and the production namelist's value 1 is untouched; `pbl3d_lh_opt` defaults to 0. Both new paths are entered only when the namelist asks for them.

**`diff_6th_slopeopt = 3`** (`dyn_em/module_big_step_utilities_em.F`). The existing taper `slopedamp = max(1 − Δz/dzthresh, 0)` is blended with 1 on static stability: `slopedamp = (1−b) + b·taper`, `b = clip((∂θ/∂z)/crit, 0, 1)`, `crit = diff_6th_stabthresh` (new Registry real, default 2 K km⁻¹). In well-mixed air the filter runs at full strength even on a slope; in stratified air it falls back to the production taper. The gate is evaluated on the **most stable cell of the six-point 5th-difference stencil**, not on the cell pair — with a pair-mean gate the midnight cost was still −0.222 K h⁻¹, because a weakly stratified cell can have an inversion among its stencil neighbours. `sixth_order_diffusion` gains a θ argument (the `t` array, already in scope at all five call sites in `module_em.F`), used only by this option; the stability field is built once per call and skipped entirely otherwise.

**`pbl3d_lh_opt = 1`** (`dyn_em/module_pbl3d_my.F`, in `Calc_fluxes` after `Diagnose_fluxes`). The scheme's single `l_master` is multiplied by `Psig_bl` in `Make_scale_aware` and then used for all nine gradient components, so the grey-zone taper cuts horizontal sub-grid mixing as hard as vertical. The taper is justified because *vertical* transport becomes resolved as Δx shrinks; horizontal sub-grid mixing at Δx = 500 m should still be set by the grid scale. The option restores **exactly what the taper took**, `l_h = l_master·(1−Psig_bl)/Psig_bl`, so there is **no new constant**, and nothing is added when `Psig_bl = 1` or `pbl3d_scale_aware = 0`. The resulting `K_h = 0.39·q·l_h` is applied as a down-gradient addition to the **horizontal** components only — ⟨u′u′⟩, ⟨v′v′⟩, ⟨u′v′⟩ and the horizontal θ_v and q_v fluxes at Prandtl 1 — and **deliberately does not enter the q² budget**, because the algebraic solve already pays for the transport it represents; crediting q² twice is the error `pbl3d_sf_pair` was written to remove (A10).

*Why not the literal 3DTKE port:* `Diagnose_fluxes` is a point-wise algebraic Mellor–Yamada solve in which the single length sets the timescale τ = B₁l/q for the whole coupled system, so there is no per-direction length to swap. The restoration above is the smallest change that separates the horizontal from the vertical without restructuring the solve, and it carries the same physical argument as the 3DTKE split while inheriting neither of that scheme's two defects (the pthl/pu blend and the dimensionally inconsistent cap, 10:30 entry).

**Expected size.** At the crest with `l_master` = 30 m and Psig_bl ≈ 0.2 the restored length is ≈ 120 m, giving K_h ≈ 47 m² s⁻¹ on top of the closure's ≈ 12, so ≈ 59 against ICON's 65–130 — a 4 km damping timescale of ≈ 1.9 h against production's 9.6 h and ICON's 0.9–1.7 h.

**Still to do before any science run:** the mandatory bit-for-bit check (same MPI layout as the reference, defaults on, must reproduce the last good run exactly), then the offline θ-cost re-check with `slopeopt = 3` on to confirm the night column returns to production values, then the 13→22 UT twins. Rating: 7/10 research (implementation, not discovery), 8/10 model (two default-off options with the physics argument written into the source, no new tuning constants, and the known defects of the reference implementation avoided).

**2026-09-21, 10:30 (clock) — THE 3DTKE SCHEME IN THE goger19 TREE HAS THE RIGHT CONCEPT FOR OUR HORIZONTAL MIXING: SEPARATE HORIZONTAL AND VERTICAL LENGTHS, AND A HORIZONTAL DIFFUSIVITY THAT BLENDS A DEFORMATION CLOSURE WITH A TKE CLOSURE BY A HONNERT-TYPE GREY-ZONE FUNCTION. THE GRID SCALE ENTERS AS THE FILTER WIDTH OF THE DEFORMATION CLOSURE, NOT AS A TUNED LENGTH — WHICH ANSWERS THE OBJECTION TO HARD-CODING l_h = Δx. THREE DEFECTS FOUND IN IT, ONE OF THEM A REAL BUG.** Source `goger19WRF/dyn_em/module_diffusion_em.F`, the `km_opt = 5` block (marked `!!!XZ`; Goger's own diff touches MYNN, the surface layer, the PBL driver and the Registry, not this block).

**The concept.** `mlen_h = √(dx·dy)` map-factor corrected for the horizontal, `mlen_v = min(Δz, 0.76 q/N)` for the vertical — **two separate lengths throughout**. Two horizontal diffusivities: a deformation one, `xkmh_s = c_s² mlen_h² |D|` with |D| the **two-dimensional** horizontal deformation only, and a TKE one, `xkmh_t = c_k q mlen_h`. They are blended by a Honnert-type partition function of Δx/h_PBL, and the vertical diffusivity is blended the same way between a mesoscale length (`0.4 q dlk`) and an LES length (`c_k q mlen_v`). So Δx is used where it belongs — as the filter width of a deformation closure — and the diffusivity itself responds to the flow through |D| and q.

**Defect 1 (a real bug).** `xkmh(i,k,j) = pth1·xkmh_s + (1−pth1)·xkmh_t` blends the horizontal **momentum** diffusivity with `pthl`, the partition function for the **heat** flux. It should be `pu`, the momentum one, which is computed on the line above (`pu1`) and then used only for the vertical. `pu` and `pthl` are distinct fits (different coefficients and exponents, `module_diffusion_em.F:5489` and `:5517`). *Honest magnitude:* at our Δx/h_PBL = 500/828 = 0.60 they evaluate to 0.767 and 0.771, so under 1 %; at Δx/h = 0.2 it is 0.369 against 0.400, about 8 %. A correctness bug with a small quantitative footprint at this resolution — worth fixing in a port, not a reason to distrust the results.

**Defect 2 (dimensional).** `xkmh_s = min(xkmh_s, 10.·mlen_h)` compares a diffusivity (m² s⁻¹) with ten times a length (m). At Δx = 500 m the cap is 5000 against a typical `xkmh_s` of ~23 m² s⁻¹, so it never bites here — latent, but it would at fine Δx or in strong deformation, and it is not a CFL-type limit (that would be ∝ mlen_h²/dt).

**Defect 3 (inconsistency, and it bites us).** The terrain correction for `diff_opt = 2` — divide by α² where α = |∇z|·Δx/Δz — is applied **only to the deformation part** `xkmh_s`, never to the TKE part `xkmh_t`. The cross-η over-mixing argument it embodies applies to any horizontal diffusivity. And for us it works the wrong way: with a coordinate slope of 0.145, Δx = 500 m and Δz ≈ 30 m near the ground, α ≈ 2.4 and α² ≈ 5.8, so the correction cuts the deformation diffusivity sixfold **exactly over the steep terrain where we want more damping**.

**What it would buy, at the crest at 14 UT** (TKE ≈ 0.5 m² s⁻², |D| ≈ 1.45 × 10⁻³ s⁻¹, Δ = 500 m, c_s = 0.25, c_k = 0.15, partition 0.77), with τ = λ²/(4π²K) at λ = 4 km:

| | K_h, m² s⁻¹ | τ at 4 km |
|---|---|---|
| pbl3d today (tapered isotropic l_master = 30 m) | 12 | **9.6 h** |
| 3DTKE concept, as written with the α correction | 15 | 7.4 h |
| 3DTKE concept without the α correction | **30** | **3.8 h** |
| ICON | 65–130 | 0.9–1.7 h |

So porting the concept roughly halves the damping timescale at 3–6 km, or better if the α correction is reconsidered — a real improvement, still short of ICON, and crucially **flow-responsive rather than a hard-coded length**.

**And the α correction is the same problem as the filter's slope taper.** Both exist because horizontal mixing along η surfaces over steep terrain mixes across height; both are switched off or cut down precisely where we need them. **The stability gate (09-21 07:15) applies to both**: gate the terrain correction on static stability so it acts in stratified air and relaxes in well-mixed air. That would be one idea covering the numerical filter and the physical horizontal mixing together.

**Plan.** Port the 3DTKE horizontal concept into pbl3d as a default-off option — separate horizontal and vertical lengths, horizontal K blended between deformation and TKE forms by the **momentum** partition function — fixing defects 1 and 2 in the port rather than inheriting them, and gating the terrain correction on stability instead of applying it unconditionally. Rating: 8/10 research (the right concept found, its numbers worked out for our case, and three defects identified including one genuine bug with its magnitude honestly bounded), 7/10 model (a defensible replacement for the hard-coded length, with a known ceiling and a clean link to the filter work).

**2026-09-21, 09:00 (clock) — THE MECHANISM THAT LIMITS THE 6–12 Δx CIRCULATION IS EDDY VISCOSITY ACTING ON ITS OWN SHEAR, AND WRF GIVES IT AWAY: THE GREY-ZONE SCALE-AWARE TAPER CUTS THE MASTER MIXING LENGTH, WHICH IN THIS SCHEME IS ISOTROPIC, SO IT CUTS THE *HORIZONTAL* MIXING TOO. THE FIX IS TO TAPER THE VERTICAL LENGTH ONLY.** Log `exp/x16_judge/band_vs_mixing.log`; source `branko/dyn_em/module_pbl3d_my.F`.

**(1) Measured: only the runs that raised the mixing move the band.** 6–12 Δx variance of v at 1800 m ASL, 14:00, m² s⁻²: production **0.382**; **X25** (`pbl3d_l_opt = 5`, mixing length 30 → 76 m, subgrid TKE 1.0 → 3.0, i.e. ICON's level) **0.246, −36 %**; **X24m** (MYNN, TKE 3.3) **0.254, −33 %**; **X23** (roughness raised to ICON's, mixing unchanged) **0.451 — higher than production**; ICON **0.194**. Surface drag does nothing to the band; subgrid mixing takes it two-thirds of the way to ICON. (2–6 Δx behaves the same: X25 0.152, below ICON's 0.208.)

**(2) The scaling explains it.** A resolved overturning of wavelength λ is damped by eddy viscosity on a timescale τ = λ²/(4π²K_m). At λ = 4 km, with K_m ≈ S_m l q and S_m = 0.39: production (l ≈ 30 m, q ≈ 1.0, K_m ≈ 12 m² s⁻¹) **τ ≈ 9.6 h**; X25 (l ≈ 76 m, q ≈ 1.73, K_m ≈ 51) **τ ≈ 2.2 h**; ICON (TKE 1.4–1.9 so q ≈ 1.7–2.0, a 1D Blackadar-type length of 100–200 m in a ~1 km mixed layer, K_m ≈ 65–130) **τ ≈ 0.9–1.7 h**. The circulation builds in about an hour, so **production is effectively undamped at 3–6 km while ICON equilibrates at half the amplitude.**

**(3) Why WRF's K_m is small there, and it is a design choice, not an accident.** `pbl3d_scale_aware = 1` (Registry default) calls `Make_scale_aware`, which tapers `l_master` and `l_boulac` by the grey-zone factor Psig_bl — at Δx = 500 m with an 800–1500 m boundary layer that is a large cut, and it is what leaves the crest length at 30 m against ICON's ~150. ICON's 1D scheme applies no such taper. **The taper's premise is that what the closure gives up, the resolved flow takes over correctly. Here the resolved flow takes over and over-produces.**

**(4) The defect is that the length is isotropic.** `Diagnose_fluxes` (`module_pbl3d_my.F:1720`) takes a **single** `l_master` and applies it to all nine gradient components — du_dx, du_dy, du_dz, dv_dx … — so the scale-aware taper cuts the **horizontal** SGS mixing exactly as hard as the vertical. That is the wrong physics for the grey zone: the taper is justified because *vertical* transport becomes resolved as Δx shrinks; the *horizontal* SGS mixing at Δx = 500 m should be set by Δx, not by a tapered boundary-layer length.

**(5) And this is why the obvious lever backfired.** X25 damped the band by 36 % — and had the **worst floor plunge of the whole set** (−9.6 m s⁻¹ at 17 UT against production's −7.5 and ICON's −1.5), because raising the isotropic length also raises the **vertical heat flux**, which destroys the lee-slope buoyancy barrier that holds the current off the valley floor (09-20 14:30: ICON keeps +1.79 K, every WRF run ~0). One knob, two opposing effects, and the harmful one wins.

**Design.** Split the length: keep the scale-aware-tapered `l_master` for the vertical flux components, and use an untapered, Δx-based length for the horizontal ones. Sizing: to damp a 4 km structure in ~1 h needs K_h ≈ 113 m² s⁻¹; l_h = Δx = 500 m with q ≈ 1 gives K_h ≈ 195 m² s⁻¹ (τ ≈ 35 min), and a Smagorinsky form with C_s = 0.2 gives only 15 m² s⁻¹ (τ ≈ 8 h) — so the length has to be Δx-scale, not C_sΔx. Default-off switch, no Registry change beyond one option value. **This damps the resolved circulation without touching the vertical heat flux, so the buoyancy barrier survives — which is exactly what X25 could not do.** Rating: 9/10 research (the damping mechanism identified, confirmed by three runs and a timescale argument, the design flaw traced to one isotropic length, and the failure of X25 explained rather than merely recorded), 7/10 model (a targeted code change with the physics argument in hand, but it is the scheme's own formulation and needs the usual default-off discipline and a night check).

**2026-09-21, 07:15 (clock) — ELIAS'S STABILITY GATE BEATS THE MOMENTUM-ONLY DESIGN: GATING THE FILTER ON STATIC STABILITY ACROSS THE STENCIL GIVES 95 % OF THE UNTAPERED ACTION IN THE AFTERNOON AND THE PRODUCTION TAPER'S COST EXACTLY AT MIDNIGHT, WITH ALL FIELDS STILL FILTERED ALIKE. AND THE 6–12 Δx BAND IS 61 % TERRAIN-LOCKED — IT IS A REAL CIRCULATION, SO NO FILTER SHOULD CHASE IT.** Scripts `wrf3dpbl-diag/filter_slopeopt_cost.py` (gate modes), `band_coherence.py`; logs `exp/x16_judge/filter_stability_gate.log`, `filter_stability_gate2.log`, `band_coherence.log`.

**(1) The gate, and why it is better than untapering momentum only.** Instead of exempting a *variable*, gate on the thing that actually causes the damage — the static stability. `slopedamp = (1−b)·1 + b·max(1−Δz/dzthresh, 0)` with `b = clip((∂θ/∂z)/crit, 0, 1)`: full filter in well-mixed air, the production taper in stratified air, all variables treated alike so **field consistency is preserved** and the 09-21 05:30 objection to the momentum-only design disappears. The gate must be evaluated on the **most stable cell in the 6-wide 5th-difference stencil**, not on the pair: with a pair-mean gate the night cost was still −0.222 K h⁻¹ on steep slopes, because a weakly stratified cell can have an inversion among its stencil neighbours. With the stencil maximum:

| steep slopes, 0–50 m, K h⁻¹ | production taper | no taper | gate 1 K km⁻¹ | gate 2 | gate 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **13:30** (onset, ∂θ/∂z = −23 K km⁻¹) | −0.001 | **−0.191** | **−0.186** | **−0.187** | −0.187 |
| **00:00** (night, ∂θ/∂z = +17 K km⁻¹) | **−0.069** | −1.292 | **−0.070** | **−0.070** | −0.073 |

**95 % of the untapered filter action in the afternoon, and the production taper's cost to the third decimal at midnight.** Crest 0–300 m at 00 UT: taper −0.002, gate2 −0.003. Valley floor: identical to production. The threshold is not delicate — 1, 2 and 5 K km⁻¹ give the same answer, because the diurnal contrast is a factor 40 (−23 against +17 K km⁻¹), not a factor 2. At `diff_6th_factor = 0.5` the gate still holds (night −0.291 against the taper's −0.069, i.e. 1.2× the longwave cooling) while quadrupling the afternoon action.

**Implementation:** one gate expression in `sixth_order_diffusion`, the stencil stability passed in or recomputed there, behind `diff_6th_slopeopt = 3` with a namelist threshold; **no Registry change** for the option itself, one new real for the threshold. Default-off, bit for bit.

**(2) The 6–12 Δx band is not noise, so the filter is the wrong tool for it.** Stationarity test on X22's thirteen 5-minute frames, v on a constant-height surface, second half of the window:

| band | variance | terrain-locked share | r(5 min) | r(10 min) | r(20 min) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–6 Δx | 0.268 | 62.8 % | 0.708 | 0.597 | 0.471 |
| **6–12 Δx** | 0.332 | **61.1 %** | **0.691** | 0.554 | **0.434** |
| 12–24 Δx | 0.683 | 76.9 % | 0.835 | 0.740 | 0.660 |
| > 24 Δx | 0.531 | 78.6 % | 0.877 | 0.772 | 0.649 |

6–12 Δx (3–6 km) is **61 % terrain-locked and still correlated at 0.43 after twenty minutes** — that is a real, terrain-forced circulation, not grid noise, and 3–6 km is at or above WRF's effective resolution. **Raising `diff_6th_factor` to chase it is therefore not justified**; the amplitude error there is physics (most plausibly the horizontal mixing at the grey scale) or a resolution-dependent amplitude that has to be accepted.

**An important nuance for the band we *do* filter.** 2–6 Δx is also 63 % terrain-locked with r(5 min) = 0.71 — it is not white noise either. **Terrain-locked is not the same as physical**: below the effective resolution the model cannot represent the flow, so a terrain-locked response there is the *grid's* systematic artefact rather than a random one. That is precisely what a scale-selective filter should remove, and it is why ICON — same terrain, steeper — sits at 0.208 in that band against WRF's 0.271.

**Revised plan.** Implement the stability gate (not the momentum-only untaper, which is now superseded), re-run the offline cost to confirm the night column returns to production values, then the 13→22 UT twin. Do **not** raise `diff_6th_factor` to chase 6–12 Δx. Rating: 9/10 research (a better design than ours, tested offline in an hour, plus a measurement that stops the next escalation before it is attempted), 8/10 model (a defensible, default-off change with the cost provably equal to production's where it matters, and a clear statement of what it will and will not fix).

**2026-09-21, 05:30 (clock) — TWO CHECKS ON THE PROPOSED FILTER CHANGE: IT TARGETS THE SPURIOUS SIGNAL, NOT THE BACKGROUND FLOW, AND THE EXISTING TAPER ALREADY CONFINES IT TO THE RANGE. BUT FILTERING MOMENTUM WITHOUT θ BREAKS FIELD CONSISTENCY AND THAT RISK IS UNQUANTIFIED.** Logs `exp/x16_judge/filter_targets_spurious.log`, and the slopedamp map below.

**(1) The filter is inactive until the spurious band exists.** Offline momentum tendency on steep slopes (|∇h| > 0.2), 0–300 m, untapered, m s⁻¹ h⁻¹: **13:00 mean −0.081, rms 5.76; 13:30 mean −0.244, rms 24.13; 14:00 −0.235, rms 21.38.** At 13:00 — when WRF is still identical to ICON and the 2–6 Δx band has not filled — the untapered filter does almost nothing (2 % of the real ADV_H of 3.6 m s⁻¹ h⁻¹). Its rms rises **4.2×** across the same 30 minutes in which the spurious variance doubles. It is removing the spurious signal, not adding drag to the background flow. At factor 0.5 the same pattern holds (−0.33 at 13:00 against −0.97 at 13:30).

**(2) The existing taper is already a range/valley switch, so untapering is spatially confined by construction.** Production filter strength (mean `slopedamp`, and fraction of cell pairs zeroed): **Inn valley floor 0.763 (5.5 % zeroed); gentle terrain |∇h| < 0.05: 0.861 (0 %); the range: 0.183 (65 % zeroed).** The valley and foreland already run at near-full filter; only the range is switched off. Untapering therefore changes essentially nothing where the real valley circulations live — measured valley-floor momentum cost −0.003 → −0.030 m s⁻¹ h⁻¹. **This also retires the inverted taper**: the existing taper already isolates the range, so "no taper" achieves the targeting without a new law, and the 09-21 04:05 finding that the inverted form barely discriminates no longer matters.

**(3) The objection to our own change, and it is not small.** Filtering u, v, w but not θ **breaks the consistency between the momentum and thermodynamic fields**. The filter alters the divergence field, which the mass and θ equations then respond to, while θ itself is left unfiltered — near a sharp inversion that is exactly where a spurious divergence–θ mismatch would appear. The change therefore does not remove risk, it **moves** it: from a measured, quantified θ error (−1.2 K h⁻¹ at midnight) to an unquantified consistency error. It must be checked after implementation, not assumed: the WRFlux θ budget residual and the mass-divergence behaviour over the slopes have to stay where they are now.

**(4) What this does and does not buy.** It buys the ability to run X21b's afternoon benefit **through the night**, which X21b could not (it ran 13→15 UT). It does **not** improve on X21b's afternoon result, which was 100 % of the 2–6 Δx gap, 32 % of the gradient gap and **18 % of the wind gap**. The residual is the 6–12 Δx band (0.382 against ICON's 0.194), which a sixth-order filter cannot reach at any sane factor.

**Next, and it decides everything downstream: is the 6–12 Δx growth spurious or real?** Free test on X22's 5-minute frames — the temporal coherence of the 6–12 Δx band over 13:00–14:00, the same stationarity test used for the momentum flux on 09-20. Decorrelating in 5–10 minutes ⇒ noise, and a stronger momentum-only filter is justified. Terrain-locked and stationary ⇒ a real circulation that WRF over-amplifies and ICON lacks, and no filter is the right answer; the lever would then be the horizontal mixing in pbl3d at the grey scale. Rating: 8/10 research (two cheap checks that convert "plausible lever" into "targeted and spatially confined", and one honest objection raised against our own proposal before it was built), 6/10 model (the change is now well-founded but its ceiling is known and modest, and the decisive question is still open).
