**2026-09-21, 04:05 (clock) — THE OFFLINE COST TEST VETOES THE OBVIOUS RUN AND HANDS BACK A BETTER DESIGN: UNTAPERING THE FILTER COSTS ALMOST NOTHING IN THE AFTERNOON BUT −1.2 K h⁻¹ ON STEEP SLOPES AT MIDNIGHT, FIVE TIMES THE LONGWAVE COOLING. THE COST IS ENTIRELY IN θ AND THE BENEFIT IS ENTIRELY IN MOMENTUM — SO UNTAPER u, v, w AND LEAVE θ ALONE.** No runs. Script `wrf3dpbl-diag/filter_slopeopt_cost.py`; logs `exp/x16_judge/filter_slopeopt_cost.log`, `filter_slopeopt_cost_V.log`. Exact offline port of `sixth_order_diffusion` (monotonic branch) under four taper laws, on frames at 14, 19, 22 and 00 UT.

**Why the night is the danger.** The filter acts along η surfaces. Over steep terrain those surfaces change height fast, so a horizontal 5th difference also samples the *vertical* structure: the grid-scale roughness of the terrain imprints grid-scale structure on the height of the η surface, and the filter removes it as though it were a field extremum. The spurious tendency scales as (∂θ/∂z) × (grid-scale roughness of the η-surface height). In the afternoon mixed layer ∂θ/∂z over the crests is ~0.6 K km⁻¹; in the nocturnal slope inversion it is 15–17 K km⁻¹. A run that fixes the afternoon continues into exactly that period.

**Systematic θ tendency on steep slopes (|∇h| > 0.2), 0–50 m AGL, K h⁻¹ — the damaging, signed error:**

| | ∂θ/∂z, K km⁻¹ | taper (production) | no taper (X21b) | inverted | inverted, factor 0.5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 14 UT | −22.1 | +0.007 | −0.211 | −0.218 | −0.910 |
| 19 UT | +8.9 | −0.034 | −0.611 | −0.577 | −2.405 |
| 22 UT | +15.2 | −0.059 | −1.135 | −1.077 | −4.486 |
| **00 UT** | **+16.8** | **−0.069** | **−1.292** | **−1.223** | **−5.096** |

Yardstick, the longwave cooling rate over the crest 0–300 m layer: +0.278 / −0.153 / −0.205 / **−0.236** K h⁻¹.

**Three readings.** (1) **The production taper is essentially free** — −0.001 to −0.069 K h⁻¹ at every hour. It is doing its job well. (2) **In the afternoon the cost of untapering is acceptable**: −0.22 K h⁻¹ against real terms of several K h⁻¹ (lee-slope ADV_Z −3.8, SGS +2.6), i.e. about 5 %. (3) **At night it is fatal**: −1.22 K h⁻¹ is **five times the longwave cooling**, and at factor 0.5 it is −5.1 K h⁻¹, more than twenty times — in precisely the hours where the warm bias we are chasing lives. **This vetoes the S1 run as proposed** (X21b extended to 13→22 UT): X21b ran only 13→15 UT and therefore never paid the night cost.

**Momentum behaves completely differently.** The same reconstruction applied to v, m s⁻¹ per hour, steep slopes 0–300 m: taper −0.009 (14 UT) and +0.005 (00 UT); untapered **−0.225 (14 UT) and −0.211 (00 UT)**; factor 0.5, −0.94 and −0.88. **Flat across the diurnal cycle**, because ∂v/∂z does not sharpen at night the way ∂θ/∂z does. Against the real horizontal advection of ≈ 10 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻² = 3.6 m s⁻¹ h⁻¹, the untapered cost is **~6 %**.

**Therefore: untaper the momentum variables only.** `sixth_order_diffusion` is called once per variable and already receives `name` ('u', 'v', 'w', or a scalar), so `diff_6th_slopeopt = 2` can mean "no slope taper for u, v, w; production taper for θ and moisture" — two lines, behind a switch that defaults to current behaviour bit for bit, and **no Registry change** (`diff_6th_slopeopt` is a plain integer, `Registry.EM_COMMON:3037`), so a 12-minute `./compile -j 1 em_real` rather than a reconfigure. The θ cost is then **zero by construction**, and the benefit is retained, because the spurious 2–6 Δx variance measured on 09-21 is in the *wind* field and it is filtering v that removes it.

**A second finding that changes the inverted taper.** `inv` and `none` come out nearly identical in every row (00 UT steep slope −1.223 vs −1.292; valley floor +0.222 vs +0.239). The reason: `dzthresh = diff_6th_thresh × g × dx = 50 m` while the **domain-median height difference between adjacent cells is 42 m**, so `min(Δz/50, 1) ≈ 0.84` even on nominally flat ground. **The inverted taper as specified barely discriminates.** To use terrain as the discriminator at all, `diff_6th_thresh` must go to 0.3–0.5 (dzthresh 150–250 m), giving ~0.25 on flat ground against 0.5–1.0 over the crests. That makes the inverted taper a secondary refinement behind the momentum/scalar split, not the primary lever.

**Revised plan.** (a) Implement `diff_6th_slopeopt = 2` as the momentum-only untaper, default-off, 12-minute recompile. (b) Re-run the offline θ and v cost with it to confirm the θ column returns exactly to the production values. (c) Then the afternoon-plus-evening twin 13→22 UT, judged on the 2–6 and 6–12 Δx variance (targets 0.208 and 0.194), the 17 UT floor break-in, the lee barrier and the 01 UT bias — with the θ structure now protected by construction. (d) `diff_6th_factor` 0.5 remains available for momentum only, at a 25 % cost against the real advection, if 6–12 Δx has to be reached. Rating: 9/10 research (a free test that vetoed a wrong run before it was submitted, isolated the cost to one variable and one time of day, and produced a strictly better design from the same switch), 8/10 model (a two-line change with the cost provably zero on the damaging side, and a measured 6 % on the other).

**2026-09-21, 02:20 (clock) — FOUND IT. IN THE 15 MINUTES AFTER 13:00 WRF MORE THAN DOUBLES ITS CROSS-RANGE WIND VARIANCE AT 2–6 Δx, IN THE BAND BELOW ITS OWN EFFECTIVE RESOLUTION, WHERE THE SIXTH-ORDER FILTER IS SWITCHED OFF OVER STEEP SLOPES. ICON NEVER DOES. THE SHARPENED GRADIENT DOUBLES −v ∂v/∂y, AND EVERYTHING ELSE FOLLOWS. TERRAIN AND BLOCKING ARE EXCLUDED BY THE ICON-TERRAIN RUN; THE FILTER TWIN REMOVES EXACTLY THE SPURIOUS BAND.** Scripts `wrf3dpbl-diag/advh_and_blocking.py`, `gradient_scale_onset.py`, `range_budget_onset.py`; logs `exp/x16_judge/advh_and_blocking.log`, `advh_and_blocking_runs.log`, `gradient_scale_onset.log`, `range_budget_onset_runs.log`. **(1) It is the gradient, not the wind, and it is the cross-range part.** ADV_H = −(u ∂v/∂x + v ∂v/∂y) over the range box at 1800 m ASL, 10⁻⁴ m s⁻²: the −u ∂v/∂x part is the **same** in both models (WRF −1.1 → −2.9, ICON −2.0 → −2.8); the whole difference is **−v ∂v/∂y**, WRF −1.6 → −10.1 → −8.0 against ICON −1.6 → −2.8. And ADV_H ≈ |U|·|∇v| decomposes as (wind ratio 1.22) × (**gradient ratio 1.51**) = 1.85, against the measured 1.95.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| WRF | **0.956** | **1.505** | 1.493 | 1.455 |
| ICON | **0.979** | — | — | **0.965** |

**Identical at 13:00; WRF gains 57 % in one 15-minute step and holds it; ICON never rises.**

**(2) The scale says it is not physical.** Variance of v in the range patch at 1800 m ASL, m² s⁻², by cross-range wavelength band:

| | 2–6 Δx | 6–12 Δx | 12–24 Δx | > 24 Δx |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WRF 13:00 | 0.194 | 0.193 | 0.452 | 0.618 |
| **WRF 13:15** | **0.408** | **0.330** | 0.561 | 0.793 |

| **ICON 14:00** | **0.208** | **0.194** | 0.504 | 0.698 |

Every band agrees at 13:00. In fifteen minutes WRF **more than doubles** the 2–6 Δx variance (+110 %) and adds 71 % at 6–12 Δx, while the large scales grow only 24–28 %. **ICON's 2–6 Δx variance falls over the whole hour** (0.233 → 0.208) and its 6–12 Δx is flat; all of ICON's growth is at > 12 Δx. WRF's effective resolution is ~6–7 Δx, so the band it fills is below what it can represent — this is spurious generation, not resolved frontogenesis.

**(3) Terrain and blocking are excluded, by the run that was already on disk.** X12mta carries ICON's terrain (crest mean 2037 m against ICON's 2042, |∇h| 0.318 against 0.341) and **reproduces ICON's blocking parameters exactly**: non-dimensional mountain height Nh/U = 2.04 against ICON's 2.02, dividing-streamline height z_d = 512 m against 511 m, upstream N = 0.0052 against 0.0051 s⁻¹ — where the production run sits at Nh/U = 1.70, z_d = 389 m. **And it makes no difference:** X12mta's ADV_H at 14:00 is −9.5 (production −10.9, ICON −5.6), its |∇v| is **1.600 — the largest of the whole set** — and its 2–6 Δx variance 0.325 against production's 0.271. Giving WRF ICON's steeper terrain buys ICON's Froude number and *worsens* the small-scale generation. Terrain, terrain interpolation and low-level blocking are out.

**(4) The filter twin closes the loop.** X21b (`diff_6th_opt = 2`, `diff_6th_slopeopt = 0`) differs from the PRODUCTION configuration only in the taper; the numbers below are against production (X22/X17a), which is the correct control. Note X21a is **not** production — it has `diff_6th_opt = 0`, the filter switched off entirely, and over the range it behaves like production (|∇v| 1.437 vs 1.455, v −3.97 vs −3.94), which is independent confirmation that the taper already disables the filter there. Its 2–6 Δx variance is **0.250 at 13:30 and 0.209 at 14:00 against the control's 0.304 and 0.271 — a reduction of 18 and 23 %, landing on ICON's 0.208.** Its |∇v| falls 1.437 → 1.285 and ADV_H −8.5 → −8.0, and the split moves exactly where it should: −v ∂v/∂y from −6.4 to −4.1. **The filter removes precisely the band WRF spuriously fills**, which is why it was the only intervention that ever moved the lee current (−25…−30 %, 09-19).

**The chain, complete.** 13:00 WRF ≡ ICON in wind, gradient and spectrum → in 15 minutes WRF fills 2–6 Δx over the steep slopes where its filter is tapered off → the cross-range gradient sharpens 57 % → −v ∂v/∂y doubles → the northerly accelerates 2.6× faster than ICON's (−2.30 → −3.94 m s⁻¹ against −2.33 → −2.95) → the crest excess over-runs the range → descends the sunlit lee slope → destroys the buoyancy barrier that ICON keeps (+1.79 K) → plunges to the valley floor → adiabatic warming at +7 → +22 K h⁻¹ after sunset → the evening and nocturnal warm bias of +3.3 K at 0–100 m. **[The adiabatic-warming link is RETRACTED 2026-09-26 09:30 — frame error, E75.]**

**Soundness, before anyone calls this tuning.** The band being removed lies below WRF's own effective resolution, so it is not flow the model can represent; ICON, at the same 500 m, has no such variance and no such gradient growth; and the taper `diff_6th_slopeopt = 1` exists to protect genuine flow over steep slopes, which here is exactly where the spurious generation happens. Turning it off is a correction, not a tuning knob — but the claim needs the full afternoon, not two hours: X21b ran only 13→15 UT. **Next run:** `diff_6th_slopeopt = 0` for the full 13→22 UT afternoon and evening, judged on the floor break-in at 17 UT, the lee barrier and the 01 UT temperature bias; and, separately, a scan of `diff_6th_factor` with the taper off, since 0.12 is the value tuned for the tapered configuration. **2026-09-21, 00:40 (clock) — THE ONSET HOUR, BUDGETED ON TRUE CONSTANT-HEIGHT SURFACES: THE ONE TERM THAT DIFFERS IS HORIZONTAL ADVECTION OF MOMENTUM, TWICE ICON'S OVER THE RANGE AT 1800–2000 m ASL. PRESSURE FORCE, CORIOLIS AND SUBGRID STRESS ALL MATCH.** Script `wrf3dpbl-diag/range_budget_onset.py`; log `exp/x16_judge/range_budget_onset.log`. Range box 47.35–47.45° N, 11.30–11.85° E; WRF from X22's 5-minute frames 13:00–14:00, ICON at 13 and 14 UT; cells masked to those whose terrain lies below the surface (1059 at 1800 m, 1464 at 2000 m). Terms in 10⁻⁴ m s⁻², negative accelerates the northerly.

**Why this frame.** On a true constant-height (ASL) surface the pressure force is a plain horizontal gradient — no cancelling terrain term, no 1-in-165 residual — and the advection split carries no (∂z/∂y)∂/∂z contamination. Both halves of the E61 trap are absent, which is why the range box at 1800–2200 m was chosen: most of it lies above the terrain.

**1800 m ASL, the hour in which the excess is made:**

| | WRF 13:15 | WRF 13:30 | WRF 13:45 | **WRF 14:00** | ICON 13:00 | **ICON 14:00** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADV_H | −5.9 | −7.2 | **−12.4** | **−10.9** | −3.6 | **−5.6** |
| ADV_V | −0.6 | +0.9 | +3.8 | +4.0 | +2.0 | −1.4 |
| PGF | +8.4 | +1.5 | +4.0 | −0.1 | +14.0 | +3.0 |
| COR | −3.5 | −3.6 | −3.9 | −4.1 | −2.9 | −3.7 |
| SGS | — | +0.6 | — | +2.4 | +1.4 | +1.9 |
| sum | −1.5 | −7.8 | −8.4 | −8.7 | +11.0 | −5.9 |
| dv/dt | −1.6 | −3.6 | −9.3 | −3.6 | — | −1.7 |
| residual | −0.1 | +4.2 | −0.9 | **+5.1** | — | **+4.2** |
| v (m s⁻¹) | −2.45 | −2.78 | −3.62 | **−3.94** | −2.33 | **−2.95** |

WRF's meridional wind goes −2.30 → −3.94 m s⁻¹ in the hour; ICON's −2.33 → −2.95. **Same start, 2.6× the acceleration.** Of the five terms, four agree: the pressure force declines from a brake of +8…+14 to ~0 in both, Coriolis is −4.1 against −3.7, the subgrid stress is +2.4 against +1.9, and vertical advection is small. **Horizontal advection is −10.9 in WRF against −5.6 in ICON** — a difference of 5.3 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻², i.e. 1.9 m s⁻¹ per hour, which is the size of the excess that appears. The same holds at 2000 m (−10.9 vs −5.0) and weakens by 2200 m (−6.6 vs −4.7).

**On the closure.** The budget closes to ±5 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻², comparable to some terms, so no single absolute number is strong. But **the residual is the same in both models** (+5.1 against +4.2), so the model-to-model comparison of terms is much firmer than the absolute closure: the difference of residuals is 0.9 × 10⁻⁴ against an ADV_H difference of 5.3.

**Reading.** The excess northerly is made by the resolved nonlinear advection term, not by any force and not by the closure. Horizontal advection of momentum is self-amplifying once a core exists — but at 13:00 the two models are identical in both v (−2.30 vs −2.33) and ADV_H (−2.7 vs −3.6), so the amplification starts from parity and runs away in WRF over the following 45 minutes. Two ways that happens at fixed wind speed: a **sharper cross-range gradient ∂v/∂y** (a more concentrated northerly core), or a **different geometry of the u ∂v/∂x part** across the range. Separating them is the next measurement and needs no new data: ∂v/∂y and ∂v/∂x over the same box and hour, both models, alongside the wind itself. It also connects to the one intervention that ever moved the lee current, X21b's filter over steep slopes, which acts on exactly the resolved gradients that this term multiplies. Rating: 8/10 research (the onset hour is budgeted in a clean frame, four of five terms match and the fifth carries the whole difference, with the closure caveat stated), 5/10 model (it names the term but not yet the reason, and the term is resolved dynamics rather than a switch).

It first appears at and just below crest level, 1500–2000 m ASL, in the hour in which the lee up-slope layer is flushed (13:05–13:30, X22). | **ICON total, l = 50 m** | **0.310** | **0.312** | **0.284** | **0.168** |
| ICON total, l = 80 m | 0.367 | 0.360 | 0.324 | 0.180 |

WRF's own subgrid flux is ~0 above 100 m (measured `VW_SGS_MEAN` = 0.002, −0.025, −0.046, −0.044), so WRF's total *is* its resolved flux. At any ordinary daytime mixing length the two models deliver **the same total momentum flux** into the crest column.

**(3) And it is not the lever anyway.** Resolved crest flux at 100 m across the run set: X21b **0.475**, X20 0.418, X17m 0.330, X21a 0.329, X17a 0.322, X19a 0.312, X23 0.294, X24m 0.289, X25 **0.134**. Against the outcome: X25 has the **lowest** flux and the **worst** floor plunge (−9.6 m s⁻¹ at 17 UT against X17's −7.5, X23's −6.0, ICON's −1.5), while X21b has the **highest** flux and the **weakest** crest wind (−3.9 m s⁻¹ at 15 UT against its own twin X21a's −4.6). The crest momentum flux and the descent are uncorrelated to anti-correlated across nine runs.

**What is retracted.** The causal framing built on 2026-09-20 09:15 → 19:40 — that the crest jet is fed by a resolved terrain-locked pump which WRF over-produces, and that this is where WRF differs from ICON. **The measurements stand** (the partition within WRF, the 19 Δx scale, the r/σ decomposition, the terrain-slope and wind numbers); the inference from them does not. Also withdrawn: "ICON damps the terrain-forced vertical motion to 0.43 of its kinematic value while WRF passes 0.72–0.80" — that compares resolved w in one model with resolved w in the other and is the same partition artefact.

**What survives, untouched.** The lee-barrier result (the resolved descent destroys the barrier, the closure maintains it — 14:30 entry) is about the descent itself, not the crest flux. The evening mechanism (adiabatic warming by the plunging flow, +7 → +22 K h⁻¹ at 17–18 UT, floor ADV_H +4.3) stands. **[RETRACTED 2026-09-26 09:30 — frame error, E75.]** The numerics exclusions (the circulation is at 19 Δx; the coordinate error is 19 % at 24 m and 2–9 % above; `hybrid_opt` cannot reach the layer) stand. And the plain fact stands: **WRF's crest wind at 100 m is 6.25 m s⁻¹ against ICON's 4.57, and roughness reaches the 10 m wind and not the 100 m wind** (X23: U₁₀ 4.18 ≈ ICON's 4.0, V₁₀₀ still 5.83).

**Where that leaves it.** Same cross-range pressure force as ICON (09-18), same total downward momentum flux (this entry), and a surface stress that can be pushed past ICON's (X23: 0.390 against 0.25 N m⁻²) — and the 100 m crest wind is still 37 % too strong. The terms that balance the crest column therefore do not balance, and the one never measured cleanly is **horizontal advection of momentum into the crest column** (the 09-18 budget closed only to ⅓ there). That is the next measurement: a term-by-term crest-column momentum budget from the WRFlux momentum fluxes (`FVX/FVY/FVZ_ADV_MEAN_2ND`), with ICON's horizontal advection computed from its own u, v on the same grid. Rating: 8/10 research (a retraction that removes a whole false trail in one measurement, with the partition estimator validated and a cross-run consistency check), 3/10 model (it undoes two days of apparent progress and names one untested term).

**2026-09-20, 19:40 (clock) — THE WHOLE DIFFERENCE IS σ_w: WRF'S RESOLVED VERTICAL VELOCITY OVER THE CRESTS IS 1.8× ICON'S AT EVERY HEIGHT, FOR THE SAME KINEMATIC TERRAIN FORCING AND OVER *GENTLER* TERRAIN. THE HORIZONTAL PERTURBATION MATCHES AND ICON'S v′–w′ CORRELATION IS HIGHER. RAISING ROUGHNESS FIXES THE 10 m WIND AND NOT THE 100 m WIND.** No runs. Script `wrf3dpbl-diag/crest_flux_scaling.py`; logs `exp/x16_judge/crest_flux_scaling.log`, `remap_smoothing_control.log`. Crest cells as before, X17a/X23/X24m/X25 and ICON all sampled at 14, 15, 16 UT so the sampling is matched.

**(1) The scaling test is a null — and the apparent collapse is a coincidence.** The efficiency E = ⟨v′w′⟩(100 m) / (V₁₀₀² |∇h|), with V₁₀₀ the crest-mean wind speed at 100 m AGL and |∇h| the crest-mean terrain slope, comes out **X17a 0.0338, X23 0.0356, X24m 0.0315, ICON 0.0313** — a 15 % spread (X25 is the outlier at 0.0153). But the collapse is three compensating factors, not a law: ICON has a **39 % steeper** slope (|∇h| 0.336 vs 0.241 — its crests are also 63 m higher and carry more small-scale height variance) and a **27 % weaker** wind (V₁₀₀ 4.57 vs 6.25 m s⁻¹). Do not read E as physics.

**(2) The decomposition ⟨v′w′⟩ = r · σ_v′ · σ_w′ is decisive.** Window means at 25 / 100 / 150 / 250 / 400 m AGL:

| | 25 m | 100 m | 150 m | 250 m | 400 m |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| σ_w′ WRF (X17a) | 1.233 | 1.197 | 1.157 | 1.076 | 0.958 |
| σ_w′ **ICON** | **0.666** | **0.666** | **0.629** | **0.584** | **0.540** |
| ratio | 1.85 | 1.80 | 1.84 | 1.84 | 1.77 |
| σ_v′ WRF / ICON | 1.450 / 1.341 | 1.234 / 1.205 | 1.106 / 1.078 | 0.953 / 0.880 | 0.898 / 0.690 |
| r WRF / ICON | 0.112 / 0.112 | 0.214 / **0.275** | 0.253 / **0.353** | 0.259 / **0.428** | 0.170 / **0.403** |

σ_w′ (m s⁻¹, the standard deviation of the box-mean deviation of vertical velocity) is **1.8× larger in WRF at every height, uniformly**; σ_v′ matches to within 10 %; and ICON's v′–w′ correlation is *higher* everywhere above 25 m, rising monotonically to 0.40 while WRF's peaks at 0.26 near 250 m and falls. All three other WRF runs sit with X17a (σ_w′ 1.02–1.08 at 100 m against ICON's 0.67).

**(3) Normalised by the kinematic terrain forcing the two models are not alike at all.** Near the ground w is close to slaved to the terrain, w ≈ **V**·∇h. That scale is **the same in both models**: V₁₀₀|∇h| = 1.51 m s⁻¹ (WRF) and 1.54 (ICON), because ICON's steeper slope exactly offsets its weaker wind. Yet **WRF realises 0.72–0.80 of it in σ_w′ and ICON only 0.43.** ICON damps the terrain-forced vertical motion to about half its kinematic value; WRF passes it through almost fully.

**(4) Control against the remapping.** ICON's fields reach our grid through CDO `remapdis`, which smooths. Its **terrain travelled the same path**: after remapping it is steeper (|∇h| 0.293 vs WRF's 0.252 over the same crest cells), has more 5.5 km-box height variance (174 vs 150 m) and more 3-cell residual (53 vs 48 m). The remap therefore cannot account for a factor 1.8 in σ_w′.

**(5) Two side results.** *Roughness reaches 10 m and not 100 m:* X23's crest U₁₀ is 4.18 m s⁻¹ against ICON's ~4.0 — on target — while its V₁₀₀ is still 5.83 against ICON's 4.57. That is why X23 looked like a success at the surface and changed nothing aloft. *The long length scale works by decorrelation, not by damping w:* X25 leaves σ_w′ at 1.048 but drops r to 0.111 at 100 m, halving the flux (E 0.0153) — and its lee plunge was nevertheless the worst of the set. **Cutting the crest flux is not sufficient to stop the descent.**

**Correction to the 17:10 entry.** With matched hourly sampling the flux ratio is 2.1 / 1.50 / 1.40 / 1.23 / 0.98 at 25 / 100 / 150 / 250 / 400 m, not the 3.3 / 2.1 / 1.8 / 1.5 / 0.94 obtained from a 5-minute WRF mean against hourly ICON. The height dependence is real but milder, and its cause is not that ICON absorbs the flux near the ground: σ_w′ differs by a constant 1.8 at all heights, and the narrowing with height comes from ICON's correlation growing to 0.40. **The "near-surface coupling below 250 m" framing is withdrawn**; the difference is uniform through the crest layer.

**Reading.** The chain now reads: WRF's resolved vertical velocity over steep terrain is twice ICON's for the same forcing → twice the downward momentum flux → the crest jet → the lee descent that destroys the buoyancy barrier → the plunge to the valley floor → adiabatic warming at up to +22 K h⁻¹ after sunset → the evening and nocturnal warm bias. Every link is now measured. The open end is the first one, and it is a question about vertical structure and damping of resolved w over steep terrain, not about any parameterisation: WRF has ~9 model levels below 150 m over the crest where ICON has 4–5, and WRF's `epssm = 0.9` and `smdiv = 0.15` already damp w harder than the defaults. Rating: 9/10 research (it isolates the difference to a single measured quantity, σ_w′, with a control against the remapping, and it explains X23 and X25 as side results), 6/10 model (a sharp target and two testable handles, but the obvious numerical knob would be tuning, not a fix).

**2026-09-20, 17:10 (clock) — ICON HAS THE SAME TERRAIN-LOCKED CREST OVERTURNING, AT THE SAME WAVELENGTH, RESOLVED IN THE SAME WAY — IT IS 2–3× WEAKER, AND THE GAP IS ENTIRELY BELOW 250 m. THERE IS NO ICON MOMENTUM SINK AT 150–400 m OVER THE RANGE: AT 400 m THE TWO FLUXES ARE EQUAL. THE STANDING OPEN ITEM SINCE 09-18 IS RETIRED.** First like-for-like on ICON's own vertical velocity. `icon2wrf` gained `--with-w` (default off, WPS product unchanged) which carries ICON's half-level `wz` (geometric vertical velocity, m s⁻¹) and `tke` (turbulence kinetic energy, m² s⁻²) averaged onto the 65 full levels, the same centring WRF's `W` gets; 13–19 UT converted onto the production 500 m grid in `$DATA/ICON/TEAMx_1712_withw` (~600 MB/h). The committed orchestrator had never imported its own extractor functions (`NameError: extract_3d_native is not defined`) — fixed in the same commit. Script `wrf3dpbl-diag/icon_vs_wrf_crest_flux.py`; logs `exp/x16_judge/icon_vs_wrf_crest_flux_14.log`, `..._hours.log`, `crest_flux_variability.log`.

*Method:* one code path for both models. ⟨v′w′⟩ is the flux of meridional momentum in +z, m² s⁻²; **positive = northerly momentum carried downward**. v′ and w′ are deviations from an 11 × 11-cell (5.5 km) box mean at fixed heights above ground; crest cells are each model's **own** terrain above 1800 m inside 47.27–47.42° N, 11.30–11.85° E (E59: match on terrain, not index) — 657 cells at 1997 m for WRF, 680 at 2057 m for ICON, i.e. **ICON's crests are 63 m higher on the same grid**. ICON's u, v are earth-relative already; WRF's are rotated with `COSALPHA`/`SINALPHA`. Because a single instantaneous frame badly under-samples a convective afternoon, WRF is taken as the **mean of thirteen 5-minute frames 13:00–14:00** (X22) and ICON as the mean of its five hourly snapshots 13–17 UT.

**The flux profile, m² s⁻²:**

| height AGL | 25 m | 100 m | 150 m | 250 m | 400 m |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WRF (X22 mean ± sd) | 0.364 ± 0.100 | 0.466 ± 0.114 | 0.441 ± 0.106 | 0.330 ± 0.071 | 0.134 |
| ICON (13–17 UT mean) | 0.110 | 0.225 | 0.244 | 0.224 | 0.142 |
| ratio | **3.3×** | **2.1×** | **1.8×** | **1.5×** | **0.94×** |

ICON's individual hours (0.196–0.260 at 100 m) lie at or below WRF's **minimum** over the hour (0.166), so the separation is far outside WRF's own frame-to-frame scatter. **Profile shape is the mechanism:** ICON's flux *falls away* towards the ground, 0.244 at 150 m to 0.110 at 25 m — the profile of a downward flux absorbed before it reaches the surface layer — while WRF's stays high all the way down, 0.441 to 0.364. Above 400 m the two are the same.

**It is the same circulation, and it is resolved in both.** Cross-range cospectrum of ⟨v′w′⟩ at 100 m AGL: ICON peaks at **9.5 Δx = 4.8 km** with 0.8 % of the flux below 4 Δx and 48.9 % at 8–16 Δx; WRF peaks at 10.9 Δx = 5.4 km with 1.9 % below 4 Δx and 48.6 % at 8–16 Δx. (The peak sits at 10–13 Δx here and at 19 Δx in the 14:30 entry because the box-mean removal high-passes at 11 cells; both say the flux-carrying scale is 5–10 km.) **ICON does not park this transport in its subgrid** — this is not a grey-zone partition problem, and no body force is hiding it.

**And matching ICON's subgrid energy does not fix it.** Crest TKE at 14 UT: ICON 1.09 / 1.71 / 1.86 / 1.73 m² s⁻² at 25 / 100 / 150 / 250 m, production X17a **0.42 / 0.55 / 0.51 / 0.42**, X25 (the new length scale) **1.13 / 1.51 / 1.51 / 1.38** — X25 reaches ICON's level, and its resolved flux at 100/150 m is still 0.312 / 0.369, i.e. 1.4–1.5× ICON's. The closure's energy level is not the lever.

**Reading.** The 09-18 open item — "a momentum sink of ≈ +10…+15 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻² acting through depth over the range, which ICON has and WRF has not" — is **wrong and retired**: at 400 m the two models' resolved fluxes agree to 6 %, and ICON's own subgrid stress cannot be doing the work either since the difference lies below 250 m, not through the layer. What ICON does and WRF does not is **stop the downward momentum flux from penetrating the last 150 m to the surface layer**. That is a near-surface coupling question — how the resolved overturning is allowed to reach into the lowest levels over steep terrain — not a missing drag over the range. It is also consistent with everything else: the crest wind excess is 0–150 m, the wind profile has a surface maximum where ICON's has a friction profile, and raising surface drag (X23) or subgrid mixing (X25) changed neither, because both act *on* the surface layer rather than on what is delivered *into* it. Rating: 9/10 research (it retires the standing hypothesis, localises the difference to a 250 m layer, and shows the two models share the circulation itself), 6/10 model (it says where to look — the lowest 150 m over steep terrain — and rules out the closure energy level and any range-scale body force, but names no switch).

**2026-09-20, 14:30 (clock) — THE THREE NON-RUN DIAGNOSTICS RETIRE BOTH NUMERICS HYPOTHESES AND EXONERATE THE CLOSURE BY MECHANISM: THE CREST CIRCULATION IS A PROPERLY RESOLVED ~10 km OVERTURNING (19 Δx, NOT GRID NOISE), THE TERRAIN-FOLLOWING PRESSURE-GRADIENT ERROR IS 2–19 % OF THE DRIVING FORCE, AND THE LEE BUOYANCY BARRIER IS DESTROYED BY THE RESOLVED DESCENT WHILE THE CLOSURE ACTIVELY MAINTAINS IT.** No runs. Scripts `wrf3dpbl-diag/crest_flux_scales.py`, `crest_pgf_cancellation.py`, `lee_barrier_physical.py`; logs `exp/x16_judge/crest_flux_scales.log`, `crest_flux_scales_locked.log`, `crest_pgf_cancellation.log`, `lee_barrier_physical.log`, `lee_barrier_physical_afternoon.log`.

**(1) Scale of the flux-carrying circulation.** Rectangular patch over the northern range (76 × 121 cells, 60 × 38 km, terrain 511–2635 m); v (earth-relative meridional wind, m s⁻¹) and w (vertical wind, m s⁻¹) interpolated to fixed heights above ground, planar trend removed, Hann-windowed 1-D spectra along x (along-range) and y (cross-range). *Fraction of the v′w′ cospectrum — the momentum flux, m² s⁻² — by wavelength band, cross-range, at 100 m AGL:* X17a 1.2 % below 4 Δx, 9.3 % at 4–8 Δx, **40.2 % at 8–16 Δx**, 33.9 % above 16 Δx, peak **19 Δx = 9.5 km**; X24m (MYNN) 2.1 / 14.6 / 38.3 / 28.9, same 19 Δx peak; X25 1.8 / 9.4 / 43.9 / 28.1, same peak. Along-range the peak is 40 Δx = 20 km. On the terrain-locked part alone (X22 time mean of seven 5-min frames 13:30–14:00) the small scales fall further: **0.8 % below 4 Δx**, 41.8 % at 8–16 Δx, 41.9 % above 16 Δx. The w variance does carry small scales (7–10 % below 4 Δx) but they are flux-neutral. Terrain height itself is 0.3 % below 4 Δx. ⇒ **the overturning is ~20 grid points across and fully resolved; WRF's effective resolution (~6–7 Δx) and 2 Δx noise are excluded as the cause.** The 6th-order filter is 6th order and therefore does essentially nothing at 19 Δx, so X21b's −25…−30 % on the lee current is not this circulation being damped.

**(2) Terrain-following pressure-gradient error.** PGF_y = −(1/ρ)∂p/∂y|_η − g ∂z/∂y|_η = A + B, evaluated on the model's own η surfaces over the crest cells (> 1800 m, 1959 cells) at 14 UT. *X17a:* A = −3659.5, B = +3639.2, net **−22.1 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻²** at 24 m; the two terms are each ±0.365 m s⁻² and the cancellation ratio R = mean(|A|,|B|)/|A+B| is **165 (24 m), 214 (108 m), 276 (146 m), 385 (238 m), 876 (400 m)**. Truncation estimate from re-differencing with a 4th-order stencil: 4.3 / 0.8 / 1.2 / 0.2 / 0.3 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻², i.e. **19 % of the force at 24 m and 2–9 % above**; X24m 7–13 %. On a true constant-height surface above the crest tops (2800–3600 m ASL, where one exists across the patch) the coordinate route and the z-surface route differ by 0.0–0.6 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻², 0–8 %. The coordinate slope |∂z/∂y|_η is **0.145 at the surface and still 0.141 at 400 m** — confirming quantitatively that no `hybrid_opt` setting reaches this layer (the Klemp cubic changes B(η) by < 0.1 % below 150 m AGL over a 3621 m crest; 1 % only at 413 m, 5 % at 896 m). ⇒ **a real but minority error, largest at the lowest level; it cannot produce a −4.9 vs −2.9 m s⁻¹ crest-wind excess.**

**(3) The lee buoyancy barrier: cause and effect, settled.** θ budget in a physical constant-height frame (the η-coordinate split is degenerate here — horizontal and vertical advection of ±850 K h⁻¹ cancelling to < 1 K h⁻¹, see `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` E61), over the south-facing lee slope (terrain 900–1700 m, aspect within 60° of south, slope > 0.10, 598 cells, mean terrain 1353 m) against the valley floor (< 700 m, 556 cells, 599 m), 0–300 m AGL. ADV_Z = −w ∂θ/∂z, ADV_H on the same surface, SGS = the closure's −(1/ρ)∂(ρ w′θ′)/∂z from `FTZ_SGS_MEAN`. *Barrier θ(lee) − θ(floor):* X22 **+0.70 (13:00) → +1.23 → +1.44 K (13:30)** — at midday WRF has ICON's barrier — then X17 **+1.29 (14:00) → +1.19 → +0.95 → +0.53 → +0.32 → +0.14 (16:30) → +0.07 K (17:00)**; X24m +1.10 → +0.87 → +0.27 → −0.04. *Who destroys it:* over 14:00–16:00 the **floor warms +1.68 K and the slope only +0.71 K** — the barrier is closed from below, not cut from above. On the slope the resolved descent cools continuously, **ADV_Z = −3.8, −3.8, −4.0, −3.1, −1.7 K h⁻¹** at 14:30–16:30 with w going −0.21 → −1.72 m s⁻¹ and v −2.3 → −5.6 m s⁻¹, because the heated slope layer has ∂θ/∂z < 0 (−2.9 mK m⁻¹) and the descent brings lower θ down. Meanwhile **SGS warms the slope MORE than the floor** (2.59 vs 1.50 K h⁻¹ at 14:30; 1.84 vs 1.07 at 15:30). ⇒ **the closure opposes the barrier's destruction; the resolved descent causes it.** This is the first exoneration of the closure by mechanism rather than by a null run.

**(4) [RETRACTED 2026-09-26 09:30 — frame error in the ADV_Z term, E75; the daytime ADV_Z numbers in (3) likewise] New, and it links the afternoon to the evening bias.** After sunset the lee-slope stratification flips to stable (∂θ/∂z = −0.03 → +0.35 → +1.33 → +2.10 mK m⁻¹ at 17:00–18:00) while the descent holds at w = −2.1…−2.3 m s⁻¹, so ADV_Z flips sign and reaches **+7.1 (17:00), +16.9 (17:30), +22.4 K h⁻¹ (18:00)** on the slope, and the floor receives ADV_H = +4.3 K h⁻¹ of warm advection at 18:00 (X24m: +19.8 and +1.6, same behaviour). **The evening warm bias is adiabatic warming by the plunging flow, delivered to the floor by horizontal advection** — the missing link between the afternoon break-in and the +3.3 K at 0–100 m by 01 UT.

**(5) Blocked, and the soundness check that kills the obvious next fix.** The like-for-like ICON resolved flux cannot be computed: `met_em` carries no vertical velocity (84 fields; UU, VV, TT, RH, PRES, GHT and the SSO fields VAR, VAR_SSO, OA1–4, OL1–4, no W) — it needs native ICON GRIBs. And **WRF's own sub-grid orography drag is not the answer at 500 m**: `gwd_opt` exists (registry default 0, unset in all 144 run namelists) and metgrid already supplies its input fields, but the crest median `VAR` (sub-grid orographic standard deviation) is **473 m against a resolved terrain standard deviation of 236 m inside a 5.5 km box** — the field is interpolated from a ~10 km-scale data set and at 500 m it would apply drag for orography the grid already resolves, a factor-2 double count. Turning it on would be tuning, not physics.

**Reading.** Four families are now excluded for the crest jet: the closure (three configurations, and now by mechanism), the surface (Cd₁₀ to ICON's level), the coordinate (a 2–19 % force error, no switch that reaches the layer) and the effective resolution (the circulation is 19 Δx). What remains is that a properly resolved ~10 km terrain-locked overturning is simply too strong in WRF, under a cross-range pressure force equal to ICON's, on terrain ICON resolves more steeply. The next measurement is ICON's own w over the crests from native GRIBs — not a WRF run. Rating: 8/10 research (three clean negatives, one positive mechanism for the evening bias, and a soundness check that stops a wrong fix before it costs nodes), 5/10 model (it forecloses four candidate fixes and identifies no new one, but it names the single measurement that would discriminate).

**2026-09-20, 09:15 (clock) — THE MOMENTUM THAT BUILDS THE CREST JET IS CARRIED BY THE RESOLVED FLOW, NOT BY ANY CLOSURE: 90 % OF THE VERTICAL FLUX OVER THE CRESTS IS A TERRAIN-LOCKED CIRCULATION AT THE GRID SCALE, AND IT PUMPS NORTHERLY MOMENTUM FROM 150–600 m DOWN INTO THE LOWEST 150 m. THAT IS WHY FOUR YEARS' WORTH OF CLOSURE AND ROUGHNESS KNOBS DID NOTHING.** Three diagnostics, no runs; logs `exp/x16_judge/crest_momentum_partition.log`, `crest_flux_stationary_vs_transient.log`, `pbl_depth_and_flat_excess.log`; scripts `wrf3dpbl-diag/crest_momentum_partition.py`. *Method:* crest cells > 1800 m in 47.27–47.42° N, 11.30–11.85° E; resolved flux = covariance of the deviations of v and w from a local 11 × 11-cell (5.5 km) box mean, taken at fixed heights above ground, zone mean; subgrid flux = the closure's own `VW_SGS_MEAN` (30-min mean); the split into a terrain-locked and a transient part uses the seven 5-min frames of X22 (time mean of the spatial deviation = locked). **(1) Partition at 14 UT, production run, m² s⁻²:** resolved 0.23 / 0.43 / 0.44 / 0.32 / 0.13 at 25 / 100 / 150 / 250 / 400 m AGL against subgrid 0.07 / 0.00 / −0.01 / −0.02 / −0.01 — the resolved share is **77 % at 25 m and 92–99 % from 100 to 400 m**. With the new length scale (X25) the subgrid rises to 0.19 at 25 m (31 % share there) but the resolved flux is unchanged above 150 m; the MYNN twin's subgrid flux is not written, its resolved flux is the same 0.44 at 150 m. **(2) It is not turbulence, it is the flow over the peaks:** 90 % of the resolved flux at 25–150 m is stationary over half an hour (0.443 of 0.495 at 100 m); the transient part is 0.04–0.05 at every height. **(3) The profile the flux produces:** the flux grows from 0.35 at 25 m to 0.50 at 100 m and falls to 0.08 at 600 m, so its divergence **accelerates the northerly below ~150 m and brakes it between 150 and 600 m** — a downward pump. The result is visible in the wind profile: over the crests at 14 UT ICON has −2.4 / −3.3 / −3.8 / −4.6 m s⁻¹ at 0–150 / 150–400 / 400–800 / 800–1500 m AGL, a normal friction profile increasing with height, while WRF has **−4.9 / −4.7 / −3.6 / −4.4**, a surface maximum. **The two models agree above 400 m** (−3.6 vs −3.8, and −9.5 vs −9.3 at 1500–2500 m) and their mixed layers over the crests are the same depth (parcel method: ICON 814 m, production 828, MYNN twin 826, new length 963) — so the deeper-boundary-layer/coupling-to-aloft idea of this morning is **wrong and withdrawn**. **(4) The domain-wide component is minor:** over flat low ground north of 47.75° N (23 408 cells, mean height 529 m) the excess is 0.6 m s⁻¹ at 14 UT and 0.2 by 16 UT, against 1.9 m s⁻¹ over the Karwendel at 16 UT — an initialisation imbalance exists but is a tenth of the effect and decays. **Reading.** The defect lives in the resolved flow at the grid scale over steep terrain, not in a parameterisation. This explains every null result at once: the closure (X17m), the surface roughness (X19a/r, X23), the land-cover heat (X20) and the length scale (X25) all leave the resolved circulation untouched, and the only change that ever moved the lee current — keeping the sixth-order filter ON over steep slopes, −25…−30 % (X21b) — is the only one that acts on resolved small scales. Consistent with the 09-18 conservation bound: the pressure-force input to the crest column cannot be balanced at the surface, and in WRF it leaves by a resolved circulation that descends the lee slope. Pressure-force input to the 0–400 m column over the crests ≈ 18 × 150 + 14 × 250 = 0.62 m² s⁻² (× 10⁻⁴ m s⁻² × m); surface stress/ρ 0.11 (X17), 0.17 (X19a), 0.25 (ICON). **2026-09-18, 16:17 (clock) — SNAPSHOT MOMENTUM BUDGET OF THE NORTHERLY (no run; 5-min frames of X22 + 30-min WRFlux means; script `wrf3dpbl-diag/northerly_snapshot_budget.py RUN [HH:MM-HH:MM …]`, logs `exp/x16_judge/X22_snapshot_budget.log`, `X17a_…`, `X19a_…`, `tke_profile_icon_vs_wrf.log`): THE CREST JET IS PRESSURE-DRIVEN BY A REAL CROSS-RANGE THERMAL FORCE THAT ICON SHARES; WRF BRAKES IT ONLY IN THE LOWEST ~150 m; WHEN THE SURFACE BRAKE IS RAISED (X19a) THE UNBRAKED LAYER ABOVE RESUPPLIES THE MOMENTUM BY VERTICAL ADVECTION. X23 RESUBMITTED ON ELIAS'S WORD WITH `Z0MVT(19) = 0.30` TO LAND ON ICON'S CREST DRAG LEVEL (queue: 8648830 wrf_X23 PENDING 2026-09-19T00:00:00;8648831 judge_X23 PENDING N/A;).** *Method:* zone × layer means (Karwendel, crests > 1800 m, south-facing lee slope, floor; 0–150 and 150–400 m AGL) of the grid-meridional momentum equation, 10⁻⁴ m s⁻², negative = strengthens the northerly: tendency from the first/last frame; pressure force −α ∂p/∂y at constant height discretised as the model does (one-Δy difference along η at the v-point minus (∂p/∂z)(∂z/∂y)); advection at constant height from instantaneous fields (contains the resolved eddies as spatial covariance); Coriolis; vertical divergence of the closure's flux `VW_SGS_MEAN`. **Closure of the budget:** residual ≤ ⅓ of the leading term over the Karwendel, the crests and the floor (crests 0–150 m, 13:30–14:00: tendency −10.6, residual +3.9); it does NOT close on the lee slope (residual −11…−20, the steep cells) — lee numbers are qualitative. **Crests, 0–150 m (X22/X17a):** 13:05–13:15 PGF −14.3; 13:30–14:00 tendency −10.6 = PGF **−18.2** + horizontal advection +3.7 + vertical advection −4.6 + Coriolis −3.7 + SGS **+8.3**; near-steady 14:30–15:00: PGF **−22.9** balanced by SGS +11.1 and horizontal advection +14.5 (the momentum is exported down the lee slope). **Crests, 150–400 m:** PGF −13…−14.5 at every window, SGS flux divergence **≈ 0** (+0.0…+0.6), balanced by vertical advection +12…+16. The force is there from the first frames and is hydrostatic: the 1500–2500 m layer is 1.2 K warmer over the Inn valley than over the Karwendel (298.5 vs 299.75 K at 14 UT; ICON 298.6 vs 299.9 — the same contrast), ≈ 40 Pa over 25 km ≈ 16 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻². So the forcing is physical and shared with ICON (consistent with the box-mean PGF of the 15:1x entry); the difference is the sink. ICON's own surface stress spread over the same 150 m is 0.25 N m⁻² / (ρ · 150 m) ≈ **+17**, i.e. it balances the force at its 3–4 m s⁻¹ wind. **Why roughness alone fails (X19a, same windows):** the surface brake does rise, SGS +11.1 → **+16…+17**, PGF unchanged (−17…−20), and vertical advection turns from ≈ 0 to **−14…−17** — air from the unbraked 150–400 m layer descends into the surface layer over the crest and replaces what friction removed; layer wind −4.6 vs −4.9 m s⁻¹. This is the measured form of 'the current re-adjusts', and the expectation for X23. **Lee slope (qualitative):** the up-slope (anabatic) pressure force is large, +40…+53, and is overwhelmed by vertical advection of northerly momentum, −33 (13:30–14) → −79 → −112 (14:30–15): the slope layer is destroyed from above by the descending current, not by a lack of slope forcing. **Subgrid TKE over the crests, 0–400 m AGL, 14 UT:** ICON 1.1–1.7 m² s⁻², 3D closure 0.4–0.6, MYNN twin 1.0–1.1 — the 3D closure carries a third of ICON's, but MYNN carries as much as ICON and has the same jet, so the TKE level alone does not discriminate. **Reading:** what WRF lacks is a momentum sink in the 150–400 m layer above the crests (and over the Karwendel), where its subgrid stress divergence is zero in both closures; a surface-only change cannot supply it. That is the specification for an opt-in depth-distributed form-drag term (or deeper subgrid momentum mixing over sub-grid orography); ICON's SSO scheme is the presumed counterpart (fields present, settings unverified). Rating: 8/10 research (driver, balance and the compensation mechanism are measured, with a budget that closes where it is used), 4/10 model (the fix is now specified by a layer and a magnitude: ≈ +10…+15 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻² at 3–5 m s⁻¹ in 150–400 m above crest cells).

Size: a stress deficit of 0.1 N m⁻² on a 150 m deep current is 6.7 × 10⁻⁴ m s⁻² = 2.4 m s⁻¹ per hour — the size and the growth rate (0.5 m s⁻¹ per 10 min) of the crest excess timed with X22. **Why the earlier roughness runs did not settle it:** X19a (0.05 m from 13 UT) reached Cd₁₀ 9.7 × 10⁻³, 60 % of ICON's, and carried the urban flag that over-heated the floor and removed the barrier from below; X19r (0.20 m) reached 13 × 10⁻³ but started at 16 UT with the current already plunging, and ran 1 h before the floor break-in. Neither was the clean test. **X23 (8648767):** X17a twin 13 → 18 UT, only `Z0MVT(19) = 0.27` (ICON's crest median) through the opt-in bare-ground path of A29, run-local table copied as a real file (E60), default table verified clean; judge 8648768 afterany → `X23_northerly_origin.log`, `X23_breakin_check.log`, `northerly_sections_X23.log`. Gate: crest Cd₁₀ ≥ 13 × 10⁻³, crest v 0–300 m within 1 m s⁻¹ of ICON by 15 UT, lee-slope up-slope layer surviving past 13:30, Innsbruck/Jenbach 17 UT from the east. Not covered by X23 and possibly needed on top: ICON's sub-grid orography drag (not in `AUMFL_S`) and the floor towns' roughness. Known side effect: Noah-MP's bare ground uses z0h = z0m, so the crest sensible heat rises with the drag (X19a: +20 %). Rating: 9/10 research (first mechanism that is measured in both models, sized correctly, located correctly and confirmed by a station), 5/10 model (a concrete, literature-backed parameter with a clean test in the queue).

**2026-09-18, 15:29 (clock) — ONSET TIMED WITH 5-MIN FRAMES (X22, job 8648413, X17a twin 13 → 14 UT; log `exp/x16_judge/X22_onset_timing.log`, script `northerly_onset_timing.py`): WRF DOES FORM THE UP-SLOPE LAYER ON THE SUNLIT LEE SLOPE (13:05–13:20), AND IT IS FLUSHED FROM 13:20–13:30, WHEN THE NEAR-SURFACE CREST NORTHERLY PASSES ≈ 3 m s⁻¹; THE CREST CURRENT THEN GROWS LINEARLY AT 0.5 m s⁻¹ PER 10 MIN TO −4.7 m s⁻¹ BY 14:00.** Zone means (lon 11.30–11.85° E), v = meridional wind, w = vertical wind 50–300 m AGL. *Lee slope (south-facing, 800–2000 m):* v 0–50 m AGL −0.2 (13:00), **+0.6 / +0.9 / +0.5** (13:05/10/15, up-slope, w up to +22 cm s⁻¹, HFX 300–317 W m⁻²), −0.2 (13:25), −0.5 (13:30), −1.1 (13:45), −1.7 (14:00); w +22 → 0 (13:30) → **−39 cm s⁻¹** (14:00) — the slope layer turns from rising to descending at 13:30. *Crests:* v 0–50 m −1.9 (13:00), −2.3 (13:20), −2.8 (13:30), −3.6 (13:40), −4.2 (13:50), −4.7 (14:00); 300–600 m AGL only −2.8 → −4.0; w over the crests +13…+18 cm s⁻¹ until 13:25 (slope flows converging at the ridge), ≈ 0 from 13:35 (the convergence is gone: the flow goes over instead of up). *Karwendel interior:* 50–150 m −2.0 → −4.1, 300–600 m −2.5 → −4.2 over the hour, steady, q² 0 → 1.8 m² s⁻², u* 0.62; *Alpine rim:* saturates at −3.3 by 13:45; *floor:* unchanged (−1.6…−1.8). ICON at 14 UT for comparison: Karwendel 300–600 m −3.5, crests 0–300 m −2.9, lee −0.9. **Reading:** the order of events is (i) the whole lower layer over the Karwendel accelerates (not only the surface layer → a pressure-driven, thermally forced inflow toward the heated range, 0.7 m s⁻¹ above ICON by 14 UT); (ii) between the Karwendel and the crest WRF speeds the current up (−4.2 → −5.0 m s⁻¹ in the lowest 150 m) where ICON slows it (−3.5 → −2.6): crest u* is only 0.32–0.40 m s⁻¹ under 5 m s⁻¹ wind; (iii) once the crest current passes ≈ 3 m s⁻¹ the up-slope layer of the lee slope, which WRF had formed correctly, is over-run and replaced by a descending current. So the missing up-slope layer is a consequence, not the cause; the defect sits in steps (i)–(ii): the strength of the cross-range thermal inflow and the lack of deceleration at the crest. Rating: 8/10 research (the sequence is now timed to 10 minutes and the causal order of two candidates is settled), 3/10 model.

In ICON the sunlit south-facing slope carries a weak up-slope (southerly) or calm layer; in WRF it carries a 3 m s⁻¹ DOWN-slope northerly in full sun on both days. **Location (17 July 14 UT, standard run − ICON, 0–300 m AGL):** by height over the crests the excess is −2.2 / −2.4 / −1.7 / −0.4 / +0.6 m s⁻¹ at 0–50 / 50–150 / 150–300 / 300–600 / 600–1000 m AGL — a shallow jet (ICON's profile rises with height, WRF's is strongest at the ground); by terrain class over the whole domain −0.5 on flat low ground, −0.7 on north-facing slopes, −1.2…−1.4 on south-facing slopes at 1200–2400 m; by range: northern range −1.8, Wetterstein/Lechtal −1.4, main crest −1.1, the sheltered ranges south of the Inn −0.3; by latitude a domain-wide −0.6 over the foreland rising to −1.5 (14 UT) / −2.4 (15 UT) at the Nordkette crest and lee (47.35–47.40° N). **Reading:** the 'first-hour' growth on 17 July is WRF spinning up to its own daytime state, not a shock that decays. The defect is a thermally driven daytime circulation: the near-surface inflow from the north over the Karwendel (on the observations at Achenkirch) over-runs the crests as a shallow current and descends the sunlit lee slopes, where ICON (and the valley stations) keep the up-slope/up-valley regime. Candidates now: (1) the daytime up-slope layer on the south-facing slopes is missing or too weak in WRF on this grid in both closures — the slope flow that should oppose the over-running current; (2) the shallow current is too fast for lack of drag among the peaks (profile strongest at the ground); (3) the over-heated Alpine interior (+40 % H vs ICON) strengthens the cross-range thermal current — X20 changed H by 8 % only, so untested at full size. X22 (5-min frames 13 → 14 UT, job 8648413) will time the spin-up. Rating: 8/10 research (initialisation and terrain excluded with an independent 28-h-lead case; the defect is now a named circulation with a place, a depth and a daily cycle), 3/10 model.

**2026-09-18, 14:58 (clock) — FILTER TWINS JUDGED (X21a 8647478 `diff_6th_opt = 0`, X21b 8647479 `diff_6th_slopeopt = 0`, 13 → 15 UT, both complete; logs `exp/x16_judge/X21_northerly_origin.log`, `X21_breakin_check.log`): THE SIXTH-ORDER FILTER IS NOT THE CAUSE OF THE RANGE-SCALE EXCESS NORTHERLY; RESTORING IT OVER STEEP SLOPES TAKES A QUARTER TO A THIRD OFF THE LEE-SLOPE CURRENT.** Zone means as in the previous entry, v 0–300 m AGL at 15 UT (ICON | X17 | filter off | filter on slopes): foreland −2.9 | −3.6 | −3.8 | −3.6; Karwendel −2.6 | −4.3 | −4.2 | −3.9; crests −2.3 | −4.9 | −5.1 | −4.5; **lee slope −1.1 | −3.6 | −3.9 | −2.8**; floor −1.7 | −1.4 | −1.1 | −1.4; Karwendel 1500–2000 m ASL −2.9 | −4.4 | −4.3 | −4.1. Rating: 6/10 research (one candidate bounded: a quarter to a third of the lee-slope excess), 3/10 model.

**2026-09-17, 23:36 (clock) — THE SLOPE-HEATING EXPLANATION OF THE BREAK-IN FAILS ITS FIRST CHECK (`exp/x16_judge/lee_slope_heating_X17_vs_ICON.log`).** *How compared:* model against model on the south-facing lee slope of the northern range (terrain rising northward by more than 8.5°, 800–2000 m, Zirl–Jenbach; 715 WRF / 708 ICON cells, each on its own terrain); ICON = hourly surface series, X17 = instantaneous frames; slope means. **14 / 15 / 16 / 17 UT, ICON | X17:** shortwave down 667 / 591 / 478 / 516 | 662 / 599 / 437 / **249** W m⁻²; sensible heat 181 / 138 / 77 / 105 | **250 / 193 / 104** / 19; 2 m temperature 14.8 / 14.7 / 14.4 / 13.5 | 14.5 / 14.7 / 14.3 / 13.3 °C; 10 m meridional wind −0.2 / −0.4 / −0.6 / −1.1 | **−1.7 / −2.7 / −3.7 / −5.4** m s⁻¹. Until 16 UT WRF's slope receives the same sun and gives the air 30–40 % *more* heat than ICON's, at the same air temperature — and the down-slope northerly still grows steadily from 14 UT. The cloud deck only bites at 17 UT, when the current is already established. So candidate (1) of the 23:45 entry is demoted: the lee-slope current is not a consequence of a starved slope boundary layer. What remains is dynamical: how the two models let the cross-ridge flow behave in the lee — attached and accelerating in WRF, separated in ICON — i.e. lee-side mixing/drag (ICON carries a sub-grid orographic drag and blocking scheme and a TKE closure with its own horizontal-shear terms; this WRF has no orographic drag at all and, with the 3D closure, a laminar stable layer above the slope) and the upstream low-level northerly. **Is it a WRF bug?** Nothing so far points at a coding error: the flow aloft, the crest wind, the pressure gradient and the slope energy balance all agree with ICON; the disagreement is confined to the lee-side response, a known sensitivity of kilometre- and sub-kilometre models (downslope-wind/lee-separation behaviour depends on turbulence closure, numerical diffusion and orographic drag). The one test that separates 'this configuration's closure' from 'WRF dynamics at this resolution' is a 13 → 18 UT twin of X17a with the standard MYNN boundary layer instead of the 3D closure: break-in present → dynamics/configuration (terrain, no orographic drag, filter); absent → the 3D closure's treatment of the lee slope. Rating: 6/10 research (kills the most convenient explanation quickly and names the discriminating run).

**2026-09-17, 22:03 (clock) — ARE THE UNSET-CLASS ROUGHNESS DEFECTS IN THE OLDER (ECMWF- AND ICON-FORCED, WRF 4.6) RUNS TOO? **2026-09-17, 21:55 (clock) — OFFLINE ROUGHNESS SENSITIVITY (`exp/x16_judge/hrldas_z0_sensitivity.log`, script `hrldas_z0_sensitivity.py`; six 51 h HRLDAS runs 15–18 July from the winter spin-up state, patched Noah-MP): THE MOMENTUM FIX DRAGS THE HEAT EXCHANGE ALONG, BECAUSE NOAH-MP SETS THE ROUGHNESS FOR HEAT EQUAL TO THE ROUGHNESS FOR MOMENTUM OVER BARE GROUND — AND THE URBAN FLAG TURNS THE TOWNS INTO DRY CONCRETE. X19a, WHICH CARRIES BOTH, IS CONFOUNDED.** *How compared:* model against model, identical prescribed forcing (ICON 2 m T/q, 10 m wind, radiation, given at 10 m), hourly output, means over all 42 147 class-19 cells / 12 212 town cells; the air does not respond offline, so flux changes are upper bounds; no observations. *Bit identity:* the patched offline code with the default table reproduces the old segment exactly (0 differing variables). *Drag scaling (neutral, first level 8.9 m):* drag coefficient ×2.2 / 2.6 / 3.5 / 4.9 for 0.03 / 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20 m relative to 2 mm; wind for the same stress ×0.68 / 0.62 / 0.53 / 0.45 — leaving 2 mm is the big step, the choice within the literature range is secondary; WRF's crest wind was 1.6–1.9× ICON's, i.e. needs ×0.53–0.63. **High ground, 17 July 10–14 UT, z0 = 0.002 / 0.03 / 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20 m:** sensible heat 176 / 253 / **270** / 294 / 317 W m⁻², ground flux 144 / 103 / 95 / 84 / 74, latent 186 → 157, skin temperature 20.7 / 16.3 / **15.4** / 14.2 / 13.0 °C, heat exchange coefficient ×2.7 at 0.05 m. Night 21–00 UT: sensible +3 → −5 W m⁻², skin 5.4 → 4.1 °C. **Towns, control (bare soil) → urban-flagged:** day sensible 105 → **346** W m⁻², latent 211 → **0**, skin 26.8 → 21.8 °C, exchange coefficients ×9; night sensible +6 → +42 W m⁻². **Why (source read):** `SurfaceEnergyFluxBareGroundMod.F90:125–127` sets the bare-ground roughness for heat equal to that for momentum; the Zilitinkevich reduction `exp(−C_zil κ 258.2 √(u* z0))` is present but commented out. For bluff-rough surfaces (rock, scree, buildings) heat is transferred far less efficiently than momentum (the literature's kB⁻¹ of ~2–8 and more, i.e. z0h = z0m/10 … /1000), so +94 W m⁻² of crest heating and a 5 K cooler skin are largely an artefact of z0h = z0m, not a consequence of roughness. The bulk-urban type shuts off all evaporation on cells that are 60–70 % green (Tyrolean 'discontinuous urban fabric') and covers 27 % of the Kolsass-reach floor — a threefold sensible heating there is not defensible. **Consequences:** (1) X19a (running, 8640916) tests drag *plus* a 50 % crest heating boost *plus* dry, hot towns; if the break-in disappears we will not know which did it; if it persists, that is still an informative negative. Left running, to be read with this caveat; Elias may cancel. (2) Sound version, proposed, not applied: (a) keep the per-class bare-ground momentum roughness (0.05 m) but give the same opt-in path a reduced roughness for heat — re-activate the commented Zilitinkevich form *only* when the class roughness is in use (default untouched), with `CZIL` from the table; (b) towns: no urban flag; roughness-only through the same path (`HVT = 0`, `Z0MVT = 0.5` for 31–33) so they stay evaporating soil/vegetation cells, with the heat roughness reduced as in (a); (c) longer term the high ground and the towns need vegetation (both are 35–70 % green in the satellite fraction and carry LAI 0 in the table). Rating: 8/10 research (the offline screen caught two unsound side effects before they were interpreted as physics — exactly what it was for), model unchanged.

Consequence for A29: the case for a larger roughness is stronger, not weaker (sparsely vegetated 0.03–0.2 m in the literature tables; alpine grass/shrub 0.03–0.1 m), and Noah-MP also gives these partly green cells no vegetation at all (table LAI 0 → no canopy, no transpiration) — a land-surface question beyond drag. 0.05 m stays the conservative first value.

**2026-09-17, 20:55 (clock) — ROUGHNESS AUDIT OF ALL LAND-USE CLASSES (Elias) + LITERATURE: TWO CLASSES GROUPS RUN ON NOAH-MP'S 2 mm BARE-SOIL CONSTANT — THE ROCK CRESTS (14 % OF THE DOMAIN) AND, NEW, THE PROJECT'S URBAN CLASSES 31–33 (4.1 %, THE TOWNS ON THE VALLEY FLOOR). OPEN_ISSUES A29.** *Inventory (X17 frame, 14 UT; table value → roughness the model actually uses, `ZNT`):* cropland 0.15 → 0.15; grassland 0.12 → 0.12; mixed shrub/grass 0.09 → 0.09; forests 0.8–1.09 → same; wetland 0.12 → 0.12 — all consistent. **Barren/sparse (19; 14.2 %, median height 2384 m): table 0 → 0.002 m. Snow/ice (24; 0.8 %): 0 → 0.002 m. Urban 31/32/33 (0.07/3.46/0.57 %, 520–570 m): table 1.00 m → used 0.002 m.** *Why the urban classes fail (source read, `GroundRoughnessPropertyMod.F90`, `drivers/wrf/ConfigVarInTransferMod.F90:156`):* the surface takes `Z0MVT` only if the cell is vegetated (LAI + SAI > 0) or flagged urban; the urban flag is set only for class = `ISURBAN` (1) or class > `URBTYPE_beg` (50, the LCZ range). Classes 31–33 are neither, their table LAI is 0, so they are treated as bare soil: roughness `Z0SOIL` = 2 mm and none of the bulk-urban thermal treatment. The 2026-09-10 note 'not a defect: 31–33 carry urban parameters in the table' is corrected — the parameters are there and are not used. This fits the 10 m winds at the town stations being 50–90 % too strong in the 3D chain (Innsbruck 4.6 vs 2.5, Kufstein 3.9 vs 2.0 m s⁻¹, 18 July 10–18 UT) and 27 % of the Kolsass-reach floor cells being these classes. **Literature (effective z0 for momentum):** bare rock, CORINE 332: 0.05 m in the CORINE-based wind-atlas tables (EMD/WindPRO; Silva, Ribeiro & Guedes 2007 EWEC, same order); block fields ≈ 0.05 m, debris-covered glacier 0.005–0.5 m (Miles et al. 2017, JGR-A); sparsely vegetated, CORINE 333: 0.03–0.2 m; generic 'flat desert or ice' 0.001–0.005 m is what Noah-MP assumes. Snow and glacier ice: 0.5–2.4 mm (snow) and 0.7–4.5 mm (ice) from eddy covariance on mid-latitude glaciers (Fitzpatrick et al. 2019, The Cryosphere) — **2 mm is right for class 24, leave it.** Urban: discontinuous fabric 0.4 m, continuous 0.5 m, industrial/commercial 0.7 m (EMD CORINE table); the table's 1.0 m with a 15 m canopy is a city-core value, high for Tyrolean towns. **Proposal (nothing applied; plan → soundness check → approval):** (1) class 19: effective roughness 0.05 m (literature anchor), 0.1 m as the sensitivity — but `Z0SOIL` is one global constant, so the clean way is a per-class bare-ground roughness (small, default-preserving code change) or, table-only, `Z0MVT(19) = 0.05` with a token LAI/SAI so the vegetated branch is taken (side effects on the canopy energy balance to be checked first); (2) classes 31–33: make them urban for Noah-MP — table-only candidate `URBTYPE_beg = 30` (then class > 30 is flagged urban and mapped to the bulk-urban type when `sf_urban_physics = 0`; to be verified in the driver that nothing indexes LCZ arrays in that case), with `Z0MVT` 0.4 / 0.5 / 0.7 m; this also switches on the bulk-urban thermal properties and removes the bare-soil evaporation there, i.e. it changes the valley-floor energy balance, not only the drag — a deliberate physics change; (3) class 24 unchanged. Test design: one 13 UT twin of X17a with (1) only, judged on the Innsbruck/Jenbach/Rinn station winds 16–18 UT, the StanserJoch sonic and the Kolsass lidar; (2) separately, judged on the town stations' 10 m wind and 2 m temperature. Rating: 8/10 research (a second, independent roughness defect found by the audit, both documented in the source and visible in the output), model unchanged until tested.

**2026-09-17, 20:51 (clock) — ICON DOES NOT HAVE THE BREAK-IN (`exp/x16_judge/icon_breakin_check.log`), AND THE LAND-COVER QUESTION RESOLVED: THE CORINE CLASS IS RIGHT, THE ROUGHNESS PARAMETER IS NOT.** *How compared:* ICON 10 m wind (hourly series, freshest run at lead 9–20 h) as the vector mean over ICON cells within 1.5 km and ±40 m of the station height (n = 11–18), against the stations' hourly vector-mean 10 m wind; no exposure correction; both on ICON's/the station's own height, so like for like to within the siting. **17 July, station | ICON, m s⁻¹ / ° from:** Innsbruck 14 UT 3.8/62 | 2.8/70, 16 UT 4.6/70 | 3.2/79, 17 UT 4.7/79 | 3.6/79, 18 UT 2.6/74 | 2.2/73; Jenbach 4.7/64 | 3.0/54, 2.7/60 | 4.1/51, 2.9/61 | 3.3/46, 2.9/66 | 2.4/64; Rinn 3.0/69 | 3.1/53 … 1.1/84 | 1.1/69 — direction within 5–15° all afternoon, speed 25–35 % low at Innsbruck, the collapse at 19 UT on time. On the whole Innsbruck–Jenbach floor (403 cells < 650 m) ICON's up-valley component is +3.2…+3.9 m s⁻¹ from 13 to 17 UT and decays to +0.4 by 20 UT; 20–27 % of the floor cells carry a northerly component above 3 m s⁻¹ at 15–17 UT (cells under the northern slopes), but the floor flow stays up-valley. 16 July (weak, variable winds) is mixed for both and tells nothing. **Land cover:** the crest cells are CORINE bare rock / sparsely vegetated → USGS class 19, correct; `NoahmpTable.TBL` gives that class `Z0MVT = 0`, `HVT = 0`, so Noah-MP falls back to the global bare-soil constant `Z0SOIL = 0.002 m` (snow: `Z0SNO = 0.002`) — a flat-soil value applied to rock and scree. Finer land cover makes this worse, not better: it puts the rock cells in the right place, where they receive a desert roughness. Candidate fixes (not applied; plan → soundness check → approval): raise `Z0SOIL` to 0.05–0.1 m in a run-dir copy of the table, or give class 19 a vegetation-type roughness after reading how the source treats a zero-vegetation class.

**2026-09-17, 20:47 (clock) — WHAT THE WIND MAP LED TO (`exp/x16_judge/northerly_depth_X17_vs_ICON.log`): THE AMBIENT NORTHERLY ALOFT IS THE SAME IN WRF AND ICON; WRF'S NEAR-SURFACE WIND ON THE CRESTS OF THE NORTHERN RANGE IS 1.6–1.9× ICON'S, AND 73 % OF THOSE CREST CELLS CARRY A ROUGHNESS LENGTH OF 2 mm.** *How compared:* box 47.27–47.50° N, 11.30–11.85° E (Innsbruck–Jenbach reach with the range north of it), box means of the meridional wind v (negative = northerly); ICON = met_em native levels interpolated to fixed heights ASL and its 10 m series; WRF X17 = instantaneous frames, column interpolated to the same heights, 10 m diagnostic; crest = model terrain > 1800 m (each model's own). Model against model, no observations in this table. **v at 3000 / 2500 m ASL, ICON | X17:** 13 UT −3.5 / −2.5 | −3.5 / −2.5; 16 UT −2.4 / −2.7 | −1.8 / −2.0; 18 UT −3.1 / −2.8 | −2.1 / −1.9 — WRF's flow aloft is the same or weaker. **10 m v on crests:** ICON −2.4…−2.7 all afternoon; X17 −1.4 (13 UT) → −4.3 (14 UT) → **−5.0 (17 UT)** → −2.9 (19 UT): within an hour of the start WRF's crest wind exceeds the flow 1 km above it, which ICON's never does. **Roughness:** of 834 crest cells, 603 are land-use class 19 (barren/sparsely vegetated) with z0 = 0.002 m (speed 5.9 m s⁻¹, u* 0.31 m s⁻¹); the grass and shrub cells (z0 0.09–0.12 m) have 4.8–5.0 m s⁻¹ with u* 0.5. Two millimetres is a desert/ice value; Alpine rock, scree and krummholz at 500 m resolution are centimetres to decimetres, and WRF here runs with no sub-grid orographic drag at all (`gwd_opt`, `topo_wind` off) where ICON carries an SSO scheme. **Hypothesis (not yet tested):** too little drag on the crests → a too-fast near-surface current over the northern range → it descends the lee slopes into the upper Inn valley and floods the floor from 16 UT (the break-in), killing the up-valley wind there and its cold advection past Kolsass. The A24/A25 crest blow-ups (31 m s⁻¹ over `znt 0.002`) happened on the same class. **Cheap test:** a twin from X17a's 16 UT restart — or better a 13 UT cold start — with the barren class's roughness raised to a rock/scree value (0.05–0.1 m, `Z0MIN/Z0MAX` of class 19 in the vegetation table used by the surface layer; table change only, default behaviour untouched if done in a run-dir copy), judged on the Innsbruck/Jenbach station winds at 16–18 UT and the Kolsass lidar. Rating: 7/10 research (a concrete, physically plausible and cheap-to-test cause for the break-in), model unchanged until tested.

**2026-09-17, 20:43 (clock) — FIGURE: 10 m wind maps ICON / X17 / ICON-terrain twin at 14, 16, 17, 18 UT with the nine GeoSphere station winds (`plot_output/diagnostics/wind/wind_maps_stations_20250717.png`, script `wrf3dpbl-diag/plot_wind_maps_stations.py [HH …]`).** What the maps add to the point table: the WRF northerly of 16–18 UT is not a gap jet — it is a region-wide current over the whole northern range (Karwendel to Rofan) that descends into the valley between Innsbruck and Jenbach and crosses it; ICON's field over the same ranges is weak and terrain-following with the up-valley flow intact on the floor; the two WRF terrains are alike. **2026-09-17, 20:37 (clock) — RETRACTION of the 20:24 headline ('WRF's along-valley pressure gradient is 35 % of the observed') and of 'ICON terrain recovers half the daytime forcing' (20:24, 20:36): BOTH WERE ARTEFACTS OF A SURFACE-BASED, SINGLE-CELL PRESSURE DIFFERENCE (log `exp/x16_judge/dp_spinup_X17.log`; prompted by Elias: 'the difference dropped within an hour of the ICON initialisation — is that not weird?').** It was: the initial state carries ICON's value (+1.27 hPa surface-based), and the half-hourly snapshots then read +1.00, +0.66, +0.93, +0.11, +0.04, +0.78, +1.06 — ±0.5 hPa of convective single-cell noise in `PSFC` reduced with `T2`, from which I had sampled 14/16/18 UT. **Measured properly — pressure at fixed heights, 5 × 5-cell box means, Kufstein − Innsbruck, hPa, 800 / 1000 m ASL:** ICON (met_em, native levels) 13 UT +1.01 / +0.77, 15 UT +0.78 / +0.62, 17 UT +0.90 / +0.75, 18 UT +0.74 / +0.62, 20 UT +0.06 / +0.01; X17 +1.01 / +0.76, +0.86 / +0.64, +0.90 / +0.66, +0.83 / +0.62, +0.06 / −0.01; ICON-terrain twin +1.02 / +0.77, +0.89 / +0.67, +0.98 / +0.72, +0.89 / +0.69, +0.16 / +0.08. WRF holds ICON's along-valley pressure difference to within 0.1 hPa all afternoon and evening on either terrain; the only departure is a −0.2 hPa dip at 14 UT, the first-hour adjustment of the cold start. **What stands from 20:24:** the wind comparisons (direct, robust over 5 × 5 cells): from 16 UT WRF puts 8–10 m s⁻¹ northerlies on the upper-valley floor (Innsbruck, Zirl, Jenbach) where the stations and ICON keep a 3–5 m s⁻¹ up-valley easterly; the StanserJoch cross-ridge northerly 1.5–1.8× too strong; the Kolsass up-valley wind 35–50 % too weak at 150–300 m from 18 UT; the afternoon momentum budget's ranking (pressure force vs turbulent drag). **What changes:** the weak evening valley wind is *not* a deficit of the large-scale pressure forcing — the forcing is ICON's; the error is in how WRF's flow responds inside the valley (the northerly break-in across the northern ranges and its interaction with the valley wind), and 'terrain' loses its measured support as a lever for the forcing (the break-in is as strong on ICON terrain). The ICON-terrain smoke (8640752) and offline rerun (8640754 → 8640755) are cheap and stay queued, but an X18 chain is no longer motivated by this result; decide after the break-in is understood. Method rule: horizontal pressure differences only at fixed heights with box means, never from `PSFC` of single cells (added to E59). Rating: 3/10 for my 20:24 entry in hindsight; 7/10 for this check (it removes a wrong cause an hour after it was written and sharpens the question to the break-in).
