**2026-09-17, 20:24 (clock) [HEADLINE ON THE PRESSURE GRADIENT RETRACTED at 20:37 — see the entry above; wind findings stand] — THE THREE VALLEY-WIND DIAGNOSTICS (log `exp/x16_judge/valley_wind_forcing_diagnostics.log`, `X17a_/X12ma_momentum_afternoon.log`): ON 17 JULY THE MODEL'S ALONG-VALLEY PRESSURE GRADIENT IS LESS THAN HALF THE OBSERVED, BECAUSE WRF LETS A NORTHERLY CURRENT CROSS THE NORTHERN RANGES INTO THE UPPER INN VALLEY — ICON, WHICH DRIVES IT, DOES NOT.** **(2) Station pressure, Kufstein − Innsbruck reduced to 540 m, hPa (+ drives the up-valley wind; absolute ±0.3):** 17 July 14 / 16 / 18 UT observed +1.70 / +1.58 / +1.09; X17 +0.66 / +0.78 / +0.24; X12m +0.62 / +0.70 / +0.09; mean 12–18 UT +1.54 vs +0.56 / +0.47 — 35 % of the observed forcing. The Kufstein–Jenbach leg carries +0.64…+0.75 observed and **0.0** in both runs: the lower valley has no gradient in the model. 18 July (no northerly aloft): observed mean +1.43, X12m +1.28, MYNN control +1.45 — right, as is that day's valley wind. **ICON itself on the 17th:** +1.35 / +1.43 / +0.88 (80–90 % of observed), Innsbruck 10 m wind 3.3–4.3 m s⁻¹ from 78–82° at 16–17 UT (obs 4.8 from 70–76°), Jenbach 3.6–4.4 from 51–54° (obs 3 from 60°), Achenkirch northerly decaying 5.5 → 1.9 m s⁻¹ as observed (5.0 → 0.9). **(3) Inflow path, X17 ≈ X12m:** at 14 UT the valley is right (Innsbruck 4.3 from 81°, Jenbach 2.5 from 53°) while the Achensee gap already carries 9 m s⁻¹ from 338° at 200 m; by 16–17 UT the northerly floods the floor — Innsbruck 8–9 m s⁻¹ from 347–5°, Zirl 9.8 from 359° (Seefeld saddle 7.7 from 355°), Jenbach 8 at 10 m and 10.5 at 200 m from 325°; Wörgl and Kufstein keep a 5–7 m s⁻¹ up-valley flow. The stations show no such break-in (steady easterlies to 18 UT). **Terrain twin (existing X12mt, ICON's terrain in WRF):** the daytime gradient recovers, +1.05 / +1.40 at 14 / 16 UT (cell-averaged SRTM: +0.62 / +0.70), so the flattened crests and raised floor cells (Jenbach cell 563–621 m for a 529 m station; ICON 528) cost half the daytime forcing; but the 17 UT northerly break-in is there as well, stronger (Innsbruck 9.3–10.2 m s⁻¹ from 349–353°, gradient back to +0.25 at 18 UT). The MYNN control shows the same northerly at Innsbruck on the afternoon of the 18th. So the break-in is WRF's own, independent of closure, soil, forcing levels and terrain source. **(1) Afternoon momentum budget at Kolsass, 13:30–16:30 UT means, 10⁻⁴ m s⁻², 50–150 m (X17a / X12ma):** pressure-gradient force +12.4 / +13.8, vertical turbulent mixing −8.1 / −9.1, along-valley advection −6.0 / −3.2, the rest small, residual +4.7 / +1.6 (150–300 m residual +7…+10: a point budget, read the ranking only). The wind is a balance of pressure force against turbulent drag; the observed large-scale force on the 17th is ~21 × 10⁻⁴, the model's 8–14. X17's force is 10 % below X12m's — the soil's share. **Answer to 'roughness or TKE?':** neither — the forcing. **Open:** why WRF lets the northerly through where ICON blocks it: no orographic drag of any kind is on (`gwd_opt`, `topo_wind` off; excluded list), the 3D closure and MYNN both do it, so candidates are the flow over the Karwendel/Seefeld–Achensee gaps under WRF's numerics and missing sub-grid blocking; first test = cross-sections of the northerly across the northern range at 15–17 UT, WRF vs ICON model levels. Consequence for the case choice: 17 July is a northerly-aloft day on which WRF's upper valley is wrong from 16 UT; the evening of the 17th cannot test the closure until this is understood — the 18th (clean valley wind) can. Rating: 8/10 research (one measured cause for the weak valley wind, the transition deficit's advective leg and the wrong upper-valley wind; ICON and the stations agree against WRF), 2/10 model.

**2026-09-17, 19:58 (clock) — THE OLD `sf_sfclay_physics = 5` RUN (8306272) SHOWS NO SIGN OF A STRONGER VALLEY WIND — IT HAS THE WEAKEST (log `exp/x16_judge/sfclay5_valley_wind_18July.log`; Elias's question).** 18 July, Kolsass up-valley component, mean 10–18 UT, bands 50–150 / 150–300 / 300–600 m AGL, m s⁻¹: lidar +5.5 / +6.2 / +5.3; MYNN + sfclay 5 (ECMWF parent via ndown) **+3.2 / +3.2 / +2.3**, onset 2–3 h late (13 UT +1.2 vs +5.2); MYNN + sfclay 1 (ICON control) +4.3 / +4.4 / +3.7; 3D closure chain X12m (native ICON) +5.5 / +5.9 / +4.5 (peak 8.0–8.7 vs 9.9–10.5 at 16–17 UT, and the wind held too long: 21 UT +5.0 vs −1.0). Not a clean surface-layer test (forcing, boundary interval and model top differ), but nothing here says scheme 5 helps: on the floor at 12 UT it gives *more* sensible heat (227 vs 191 W m⁻²) with the same u* (0.54 vs 0.53 m s⁻¹) and still the weaker wind, so the difference between the two MYNN runs is the forcing, not the surface layer. **What the three runs say about the mechanism of a too-weak valley wind:** (a) *surface roughness* — not supported: the 3D chain's 10 m winds at the valley-floor stations are too strong (10–18 UT mean, obs / X12m: Innsbruck 2.5 / 4.6, Jenbach 3.6 / 4.4, Kufstein 2.0 / 3.9 m s⁻¹) while the MYNN runs sit near the stations (2.1–3.0); u* is scheme-independent; (b) *too much momentum mixing (TKE)* — supported for MYNN only: its profile is flat (4.3 / 4.4 / 3.7) where the lidar has a nose at 150–300 m, the classic over-mixed jet; the 3D closure keeps the nose and the daytime mean; (c) *thermal forcing* — supported: X17 with the wetter soil (H −20 %) has the weaker evening wind on the 17th (3.1 vs 4.3 m s⁻¹ at 150–300 m, lidar 5.3), and X17's late afternoon is cloudier than observed (SW 125 vs 231 W m⁻² at 17 UT) — less late-day heating, earlier decay of the valley–foreland contrast; (d) *inflow path* — the MYNN runs put a northerly at Innsbruck airport in the afternoon of the 18th as well (14 UT 3.1 m s⁻¹ from 341°, 16 UT from 1–14°, obs 2.8–4.8 from 68–83°), like X17 on the 17th: foreland air crossing the northern ranges/gaps instead of coming up the valley relieves the along-valley pressure gradient; X12m on the 18th has the right direction (66°). Discriminating diagnostics: afternoon along-valley momentum budget at Kolsass (pressure-gradient force vs vertical mixing vs advection) from the WRFlux means of X17a/X12m; station pressure difference Kufstein–Innsbruck obs vs model; the wind map for (d). Rating: 5/10 research (a clean negative on scheme 5 and a first sorting of mechanisms), 2/10 model.

**2026-09-17, 19:55 (clock time) — X17b's OWN θ BUDGET FOR THE TRANSITION (18–21 UT, live frames; `exp/x16_judge/X17b_budget_1800_2100.log`) AND THE BEST CURRENT EXPLANATION.** Kolsass 5 × 5, window means, K h⁻¹ (obs HATPRO | model tendency = adv + SGS + LW + other + eps): 0–100 m −1.45 | −0.95 = −0.06 − 0.42 − 0.20 − 0.01 − 0.25; 100–300 m −0.95 | −0.77 = **−0.59** + 0.02 − 0.08 + 0.02 − 0.13; 0–300 m −1.20 | −0.85 = −0.37 − 0.17 − 0.13 + 0.01 − 0.18 (the Hall–Jenbach floor class agrees within 0.1). **What the budget says:** (1) above 100 m the model's transition cooling *is* resolved advection (−0.59 of −0.77) — the 'deep cooling is advective' reading (label 20:40) holds in the model's own terms; (2) in the lowest 100 m the advective term vanishes (−0.06, oscillating between −0.85 and +0.50 from window to window) although the layer just above receives −0.59: near the floor the along-valley cold advection is cancelled — by a near-calm surface flow at Kolsass (10 m wind 1 m s⁻¹ at 17 UT under a 3 m s⁻¹ up-valley flow at 50–150 m) and by the descent of the slope circulation's return branch; (3) the floor shortfall (0.50 K h⁻¹) is therefore essentially missing advective cooling, since SGS (−0.42 with HFX −12…−24 W m⁻², sonic −12…−33) and LW (−0.20, lid included) are within ~0.1–0.2 of what the observed surface terms allow; (4) the closure gap is −0.13…−0.25 K h⁻¹ of *extra* cooling here, so the numerics are not the culprit in the transition. **Best explanation, ranked (share of the 0–100 m shortfall of ~0.5 K h⁻¹; confidence):** 1. *Missing along-valley cold advection* (0.3–0.4; medium-high aloft, medium at the floor): the real valley cools to 600 m depth mainly because the dying up-valley wind imports colder lower-valley air (observed 2 m gradient −0.3 K per 10 km × 4–5 m s⁻¹ ≈ −0.5 K h⁻¹); the model has the gradient but an up-valley wind 35–50 % too weak at 150–300 m from 18 UT, none of it reaching the floor, and a wrong late-afternoon wind field in the upper valley (6–9 m s⁻¹ northerlies at Innsbruck and Jenbach against the observed 3–5 m s⁻¹ up-valley easterly; the cross-ridge northerly at StanserJoch 1.7× too strong) — the foreland air seems to arrive over the northern ranges and through the gaps instead of up the valley. 2. *Spurious stratus deck of the native forcing* (0.1–0.2 near the ground; existence certain, size pending X17n): LW↓ +55 W m⁻², surface net loss halved. 3. *Slope drainage that does not weaken between 20 and 22 UT* (≤ 0.1–0.2 via return-branch descent; medium-low, matched cells): onset and strength right until 20 UT. 4. Filter +0.05–0.1 (measured); soil: fixed, bought 0.7 K at arrival. The night after 21 UT keeps the X16s reading (surface right, transport wrong). **Tests:** X17n (cloud share); a 10 m / 200 m wind map of X17 at 16–19 UT against the ten stations (inflow path); the lidar profile to 1 km vs the model through 16–21 UT (depth and strength of the up-valley current); a `proc` virtual tower at Kolsass for the near-surface wind.

**2026-09-17, ~22:00 [label kept for cross-references; actually written 19:47 CEST — this evening's labels ran ahead of the clock] — SITE-TO-CELL MATCHING CHECKED (Elias: 'how can Hochhäuser be 114 m off at 500 m?') AND THE DRAINAGE CLAIM NARROWED (`exp/x16_judge/drainage_matched_cells.log`).** *Hochhäuser:* a 27° slope rises 255 m across one 500 m cell; the station (1009 m) lies 306 m up-slope of the nearest cell centre, whose cell mean is 895 m; the model terrain interpolated bilinearly to the station's position is 983 m (−26 m) — the 114 m is my nearest-centre sampling, not the terrain (SRTM, cell-averaged, `smooth_option = 0`; model slope there 22° vs 27°). *Crest sites are different:* cell averaging flattens crests — StanserJoch bilinear 1778 vs 1861 m, Arbeser 1881 vs 2015 m with the cell centre only 41 m from the station — and at StanserJoch the nearest cell lies on the **south-facing** side of the ridge (fall line 168°) while the station faces NNW (340°): every earlier 'StanserJoch drainage' number compared opposite sides of the Joch. With a matched cell (within 5 × 5, aspect within 60°, then closest height; downslope along the cell's own fall line): StanserJoch model −2.8…−3.9 m s⁻¹ vs sonic −1.6…−2.2 — same sign now, a northerly cross-ridge flow that the model has 1.5–1.8× too strong (the same northerly that reaches Innsbruck and Jenbach at 6–9 m s⁻¹ in the model, 21:15 entry). Eggen sits on a 1° terrace the model does not resolve (cell 5.9° toward 156° vs station aspect 53°): its 'downslope' comparison is dropped. **Floor-side slopes, matched cells, sonic / X17:** Weerberg 19:00 +2.1 / +2.8, 20:00 +1.7 / +1.8, 21:00 +0.9 / +2.9, 22:00 +1.2 / +2.9; Hochhäuser (965 m cell) +2.4 / +2.5, +2.4 / +1.4, +1.5 / +3.8, +1.2 / +2.7; Terfens +1.2 / +0.9, +0.9 / +2.2, +0.9 / +3.1, +0.8 / +0.9. **Narrowed claim:** onset and strength are right until ~20 UT; the model's drainage fails to *weaken* between 20 and 22 UT (observed halving, modelled increase to 2–3×), and X16s relaxes to the sonics by 23–00 UT. 'Too strong all evening' (19:30 entry) is withdrawn. Rule → KNOWN_ISSUES E59.

**2026-09-17, ~21:15 [label kept for cross-references; actually written 19:40 CEST — this evening's labels ran ahead of the clock] — STATION LINE ALONG THE INN FETCHED (GeoSphere Austria Data Hub, open API, `data/stations/tawes/`, dataset `klima-v2-10min`; the `tawes-v1-10min` endpoint only holds a rolling 3-month window and returns empty rows for 2025) AND HELD AGAINST X17 (`exp/x16_judge/tawes_along_valley_X17.log`): THE ALONG-VALLEY COLD GRADIENT IS REAL AND THE MODEL HAS IT; WHAT THE MODEL GETS WRONG IS THE LOW-LEVEL WIND OF THE UPPER VALLEY IN THE LATE AFTERNOON.** 2 m θ, Kufstein − Innsbruck airport (70 km), obs / X17, K: 13 UT −4.5 / −3.0; 16 UT −3.3 / −3.8; 18 UT −2.5 / −2.5; 19 UT −1.9 / −2.8; 21 UT −1.1 / −0.7; fitted gradient through Innsbruck–Kolsass–Jenbach–Kufstein −0.19…−0.60 K per 10 km observed, −0.36…−0.56 modelled (equal at 18 UT, the model ~35 % stronger at 17 and 19 UT). So the 20:40 mechanism has its observational leg: an up-valley wind of 5 m s⁻¹ against −0.3 K per 10 km is −0.5 K h⁻¹ of cold advection at 18–19 UT, and the gradient is not what the model lacks. **10 m wind, obs / X17:** Innsbruck airport 15–17 UT 5.1–5.8 m s⁻¹ from 64–74° (up-valley) / 3.8 from 54°, then **5.9 from 20°, 8.2 from 347°, 8.2 from 352°** at 16/17/18 UT (obs 18 UT: 2.8 from 73°); Jenbach 16–18 UT 3.0–3.6 from 63–67° / 4.1 from 341°, **6.7 from 324°**, 2.2 from 20°; Kufstein 1.4–2.6 from N (its up-valley direction) / 2.7–4.1 from 6–60°. No rain at any of the ten stations 12–22 UT and none in the model (box mean ≤ 0.02 mm h⁻¹), so the model's 6–8 m s⁻¹ northerlies at Innsbruck and Jenbach are not convective outflow. Their directions point at the Seefeld saddle (NNW of Innsbruck) and the Achensee gap (NW of Jenbach): in the model the cooler foreland air appears to enter the upper Inn valley through the side gaps in the late afternoon instead of flowing up the valley from Kufstein as observed — to be checked on a map of the 10 m wind at 17 UT (inference so far, from two stations). If so, the model replaces a steady along-valley cold advection past Kolsass by gap inflows elsewhere, which fits the too-weak up-valley wind at Kolsass (lidar 5.3 vs 3.1 m s⁻¹ at 150–300 m at 18 UT). **By-catch for A28:** Innsbruck airport soil temperatures at 10 / 20 / 50 cm on 16 July 00 UT 21.2 / 20.0 / 19.2 °C — the Kolsass probes are not a local oddity; TAWES soil temperatures (`tb10/tb20/tb50`) are available at every station for validating the spun-up state along the valley. Rating: 7/10 research (the cold-advection mechanism gains its observed leg and the error moves from 'a too-weak valley wind' to 'a wrong inflow path in the upper valley'), 3/10 model.

**2026-09-17, ~20:40 [label kept for cross-references; actually written 19:34 CEST — this evening's labels ran ahead of the clock] — WHY THE EVENING TRANSITION UNDER-COOLS (scale analysis, log `exp/x16_judge/X17_transition_depth_and_gradient.log`; to be confirmed by X17b's WRFlux budget): THE OBSERVED COOLING IS DEEP AND THEREFORE ADVECTIVE, AND THE MODEL'S UP-VALLEY WIND — THE CARRIER OF THAT COLD ADVECTION — IS 35–50 % TOO WEAK ALOFT FROM 18 UT.** Kolsass cooling rate 18 → 21 UT by layer (0–100 / 100–300 / 300–600 / 600–1000 m AGL), K h⁻¹: HATPRO −1.52 / −1.14 / −0.73 / −0.55; X17 −1.16 / −0.84 / −0.48 / −0.21 — the model delivers 0.76 / 0.73 / 0.66 of the observed through 600 m, a uniform shortfall of 0.25–0.35 K h⁻¹. A surface flux of −20 W m⁻² cools a 100 / 300 / 600 m layer by 0.65 / 0.22 / 0.11 K h⁻¹, so above 100 m neither the surface, the closure at the floor nor the longwave lid (≈ 0.1 K h⁻¹ of clear-air cooling) can be the missing term: it is resolved advection. **The model's own along-valley field says which:** X17's θ (0–300 m) falls toward the lower valley by 0.5–0.7 K per 10 km between Kolsass and Kufstein at 16–19 UT (299.3 → 296.4 K at 16 UT; 0.4–0.5 K per 10 km at 300–600 m), decaying to 0.33 by 21 UT. While the up-valley wind blows, it imports that colder air: with X17's 3 m s⁻¹ at 18 UT the term is ≈ −0.5 K h⁻¹, with the lidar's 5 m s⁻¹ it would be ≈ −0.85. Wind at 150–300 m, lidar / X17 / X12m: 18:00 +5.3 / +3.1 / +4.3; 18:30 +5.0 / +3.3 / +1.6; 19:00 +3.9 / +1.9 / +2.6; 19:30 +3.3 / +2.7 / +3.0 (50–150 m: 3.8 / 3.3 / 3.9 at 18:00). The missing 1.5–2 m s⁻¹ times the model's gradient is 0.26–0.36 K h⁻¹ — the size of the shortfall at 100–600 m. **Reading:** in the transition the valley cools mostly because the dying up-valley wind still carries colder lower-valley air past Kolsass; the model under-forecasts that wind (the long-standing valley-wind under-forecast, A21 and the MYNN control) and so under-delivers the cooling at every height; in the lowest 100 m the cloud lid adds its part (H −1…−7 vs −12…−33 W m⁻²). After ~21 UT the wind is gone and the night's problem takes over (X16s: slope-circulation subsidence, laminar core). Note the soil's side effect: the spun-up, wetter soil heats the valley less by day (H −20 %) and X17's up-valley wind at 18 UT is weaker than X12m's (3.1 vs 4.3 m s⁻¹ at 150–300 m) — right surface, weaker valley wind: the valley-wind deficit is not a soil artefact, it got slightly worse. **Caveats:** the along-valley gradient is the model's, unverified (no station line along the Inn on disk — TAWES/ZAMG stations would settle it); this is a scale estimate at one site; X17b's budget (adv term 18–21 UT, available when the job ends) is the test. Rating: 7/10 for the research (first mechanism that matches the deficit's depth and size), 3/10 for the model (it points at the valley-wind strength, the oldest open problem of the setup).

The observed evening is clear (336 → 309, smooth); X17 carries a stratus deck at 2.9 km ASL, 0.1–0.4 g kg⁻¹, over 40–80 % of the floor cells from 17:00 to 21:30 UT (X17 is also cloudier than X12m in the late afternoon: SW 125 vs 282 at 17:00, obs 231 — the wetter soil feeds the deck). Why 19 UT and not 16 UT: before 17:30 the real sky had cloud (SW 231–278 vs 456 clear), so clear-sky SW from 16 UT would over-heat the late afternoon; the deck's pre-19 UT cost is bounded by the +0.5 K gained 18→19 UT. With a 1 m s⁻¹ wind, +0.3 K h⁻¹ of import needs 0.2 K km⁻¹ along the flow; there is none. **Advection decomposition (WRFlux, exact):** Kolsass floor class 0–50 m, 21:30–01:00 window means: adv +0.21 K h⁻¹ = advZ +0.19 (subsidence of warmer air) + advH +0.02 (from `x16w_slope_diag` section C); the eta-frame X/Y split is uninterpretable (±200–500 K h⁻¹ compensating). **2026-09-17, ~15:30 — THE 21 UT COLD-START STATE IS NOT THE DEFICIT (`icon_init_check.py X16s`, log `exp/x16_judge/icon_init_check_X16s.log`): ICON AS WRF RECEIVED IT IS ON HATPRO ALOFT AND ON THE LIDAR, 0.9 K WARM IN THE LOWEST 100 m; THE GAP OPENS IN THE FIRST 90 MINUTES, WHILE THE MODEL TURNS TO A DOWN-VALLEY FLOW THAT THE LIDAR DOES NOT SHOW.** Kolsass, 17 July 21:00 UT, model − HATPRO layer θ: 0–100 m **+0.90 K**, 100–300 m +0.22, 300–600 m +0.03, 600–1000 m −0.30 (HATPRO warm-biased there, E51 — so ~0). 2 m: station 14.9 °C, ICON 14.4, wrfinput 15.2. Wind in the same 90 min: lidar keeps a weak flow **from the east** (up-valley remnant; 50–150 m 0.4–0.6 m s⁻¹ from ~95°, 150–300 m 1.4–1.5 from 91–98° all evening) while X16s turns to a **westerly, down-valley** flow at 50–150 m (0.6 from 253° at 21:30, 1.2 from 272° at 22:00) and loses the 150–300 m flow (0.4–0.7 from 225–275°). The Inn flows ENE here, so the model starts the nocturnal down-valley current 1–2 h early and the observed valley does not; the model's floor is then fed from the west (the Innsbruck–Hall reach), the observed floor from the east. Together with the X16s budget (resolved import +0.31 K h⁻¹ warm) this points the warm import at the early down-valley current bringing warmer western air — testable with θ along the valley axis in the 21 UT state and the first frames (`valley_transect.py`-type section) and the along-valley pressure force. *Conclusion for the five-item list:* item 1 (boundary/initial state) is excluded except for the +0.9 K in the lowest 100 m, which is ICON's evening near-surface warmth (also in its 2 m T at Kolsass, −0.5 K vs the station, so it is the layer above 2 m); item 2 (wind and momentum budget on X16s) is promoted to first, with the along-valley θ contrast as its first question.

Hochhäuser G −41 / −31 / −12, HFX −14 / −19 / −21, LW↑ +13.5 (forest cell for a meadow, unrepresentative). Eggen G −38 (from −14), HFX −14 (from −23). StanserJoch unchanged (HFX −55, forest cell). **Skin at Kolsass overshoots:** LW↑ 362.9 obs / 372.6 X16s / 362.8 X16b → radiative skin +2.0 K (X16b 0.0), canopy TV +1.1 K — the ground–canopy coupling of the meadow modelled as cropland, exactly the 2026-09-15 17:00 warning that a soil fix alone pushes the night skin above the observed (offline HRLDAS had +1 K; WRF's warmer floor air adds the rest). **Drainage at ~9 m, 21–01 UT (obs / X16s / X16b, m s⁻¹):** Eggen 1.36 / 1.44 / 1.77, Terfens 0.65 / 0.88 / 1.11, Kolsass 0.51 / 0.81 / 1.25, Hochhäuser 1.61 / 1.47 / 1.31, Weerberg 1.23 / 0.90 / 1.32 (now too slow), Arbeser 2.57 / 1.73 / 1.98, StanserJoch 1.89 / 3.11 / 3.08 (unchanged) — the katabatic excess on the floor-side slopes is gone with the ground flux restored; the slope-air θ deficit vs the core keeps its wrong sign (Eggen obs +1.0 K, model −0.5). **θ budget, Kolsass 5×5, window means 21:30–01:00 UT, K h⁻¹ (obs HATPRO / X16s / X16w=X16b physics):** 0–100 m −0.86 / **−0.29** / −0.48 = adv +0.31 / +0.39, SGS −0.31 / −0.50, LW −0.29 / −0.39; 100–300 m −0.77 / −0.33 / −0.44 (adv −0.22 / −0.33, SGS 0, LW −0.11 / −0.13); 0–300 m −0.82 / −0.31 / −0.46. HATPRO 21→01 cooling gate (−3.4 K at 0–100 m): X16s −1.0 K, X16b −1.7 K. **Reading (the mechanism):** with the soil supplying the surface, the air no longer pays (HFX −7 vs −13) and the surface-flux cooling of the floor layer halves (−0.50 → −0.31); the warmer skin also trims the longwave divergence (−0.39 → −0.29); the warm resolved import stays (+0.31). The compensating error that the cold ICON soil had provided (air-to-ground flux cooling the pool) is removed, and the model's pool now cools at a third of the observed rate. Since the surface terms now match the observed surface energy balance to ~5 W m⁻², the missing −0.55 K h⁻¹ over 0–100 m can only be resolved transport: the observations need roughly −0.3 K h⁻¹ of cold-air import where the model delivers +0.3 of warm import (2026-09-12 20:15 conclusion, now without the surface confounder). **Consequences:** (1) A28's state part is settled — the spun-up land state (winter restarts) is production; (2) the nocturnal question is atmospheric: where the slope drainage detaches, how much it is diluted (closure on slopes, filter, implicit diffusion), and the along-valley import — the X16w programme stands, to be run on X16s's WRFlux stream (on); (3) the skin overshoot and the top-layer swing are the land-surface parameter item (meadow class, ground–canopy insulation), second order for the pool but first order for LW↑ and 2 m T at the floor; (4) X16s is the new evening reference in place of X16b. Statistical caveat (E14): site cells and class means only; the class means over 115 floor cells give the same −0.36 vs −0.53 K h⁻¹ picture as Kolsass.

**2026-09-17, ~10:20 — WINTER-START SPIN-UP DONE (8639481, 34 min for 211 days; July segment 8639482; check 8639483 → `exp/x16_judge/hrldas_winter_check.log`): AT 18 JULY THE REAL-FORCING STATE FROM 18 DECEMBER EQUALS THE APRIL-START FIRST PASS TO 0.1 K IN EVERY LAYER, AND THE CYCLED SECOND PASS WAS A WARM ARTEFACT AT DEPTH. Snow > 1 cm on 18 July: 1420 land cells (pass 2 1168, ICON 16 878). **So the question of 2026-09-16 ('how much would a December start help') is measured: < 0.1 K and < 1 W m⁻² at the surface for the July case; 0.7 K at 1–2 m relative to the cycled pass; the moisture memory argument does not bite here because the spring of 2025 wetted the column to the same state from either start.** Consequences: (1) no second WRF twin from the winter restart — it would differ from X16s below the E14 noise; the winter restarts (`spinup_winter_july/LDASOUT/RESTART.20250717{13,21}`) are the production land state for future twins and chain starts; (2) `SPINUP_LOOPS` stays 0 in every future setup; (3) the remaining discrepancies — top layer 2–3 K cold at midnight with a 40 % too-large diurnal swing, 3 vol-% dry at the floor, slope-cell H — are land-surface parameters (meadow LU class, ground–canopy insulation, loam hydraulics), not state, not forcing length. Setup details: `make_setup.py` took ICON's own W_SNOW (`SNEQV_init`, median density 343 kg m⁻³ vs the old assumption 350) and soil ice for SH2O (177 555 cells); the pack melted out realistically (1420 cells left in July, all > ~2500 m).

**2026-09-15, ~17:00 — THE DAYTIME PARTITION AND THE SKIN CONTRAST: THE 3D CHAIN'S DAYTIME SENSIBLE HEAT FLUX AT THE FLOOR IS 4× THE OBSERVED, AND IT FOLLOWS THE SOIL MOISTURE; THE NIGHT SKIN FOLLOWS THE SOIL TEMPERATURE WITH A COUPLING STRONGER THAN THE MEADOW'S (figure `plot_output/diagnostics/radiation/ibox_radiation_1718.png`, logs `exp/x16_judge/X12m_daytime_seb.log`, `X16w_slope_radiation.log`).** **Night skin (from LW↑, ε = 1), 17→18 July 22–04 UT, model − station, K:** Kolsass X12m chain +2.9, MYNN −1.3, X16b 0.0; Weerberg +0.1/−3.3/−1.1; Hochhäuser +3.0/−2.2/+1.4; Arbeser +0.7/−1.4/−2.1. Same physics in X12m and X16b; the chain's top soil had the afternoon to warm (17.6 °C at 23 UT vs 13.1 in the cold start, probe 19.6) and its floor skin ends 3 K too warm while its soil is still 2 K too cold — the skin–soil gradient is 5.5 K in the model against 10 K between the station's radiative skin and its 5-cm probe: Noah-MP's soil–canopy–skin coupling at this cell is stronger than the meadow's (an effective conductance of ~7.6 vs ~4.9 W m⁻² K⁻¹ from G/ΔT, with the caveat that the observed ΔT spans the canopy air layer). **Consequence:** a soil-temperature correction alone would push the night skin above the observed (the chain already does with a soil still too cold); the temperature fix is only safe together with the moisture and the vegetation/litter insulation, i.e. a consistent land-surface state, not a patched field. **Daytime, 18 July 10–14 UT, W m⁻² (obs Rn / H / LE, sonic level 1, EC closure 59 %):** Kolsass 521 / 47 / 260 (Bowen 0.2), soil moisture 31 vol-%; X12m chain 536 / **197** / 258 (Bowen 0.8), soil moisture 22 %; MYNN control 544 / 35 / 391 (Bowen 0.1), 38 %. Weerberg obs 516 / 126 / 204, X12m 544 / 173 / 250, MYNN 546 / 57 / 335. Hochhäuser's cell (forest for a meadow, albedo 0.08 vs 0.22 measured) is unrepresentative (Rn 606–637 vs 350, H 307–368 vs 35). **Reading:** the daytime partition at the floor is set by the soil moisture — ICON's native W_SO at 22 % against 31 % measured makes the chain put four times the observed heat into the air (even a full closure correction leaves the observed H near 80), while the old-forcing control at 38 % gets H right and LE 50 % too large. The afternoon over-heating of the wide reach (2026-09-10 ~13:15, the source of the persistent evening wind) has a soil-moisture cause alongside the cloud error. **A28 is a soil-STATE issue (temperature and moisture), and the fix is a spun-up state: HRLDAS on the ICON 500 m surface forcing (Elias 2026-09-15), not a temperature rewrite.** Caveats: EC daytime closure 59 % (residual 213 W m⁻²); Arbeser's MYNN cell has LE = 0 and a 15 °C midday skin — a land-use/snow oddity of the old-forcing wrfinput, not investigated.

**2026-09-15, ~14:30 — THE SLOPE GROUND-FLUX DEFICIT IS A FORCING ERROR: THE ICON SOIL IS 3–7 K TOO COLD (`slope_soil_check.py`, log `exp/x16_judge/X16w_slope_soil.log`; OPEN_ISSUES A28).** Soil temperature at 5 cm, 17 July night mean 21:30–01:00 UT, probe / X16w Noah-MP layer 1 (0–10 cm): Kolsass 19.5 / 13.0 °C, Eggen 17.8 / 12.0, StanserJoch 11.0 / 7.5 — the model soil is 6.5 / 5.9 / 3.5 K too cold; its 10–40 cm layer 15.5 / 13.8 / 8.9 °C against a Kolsass column the eight BODEN2 sensors put at 20.3–21.5 °C. Traced through the chain: **X16b wrfinput (21 UT) TSLB 13.5 / 15.7 / 16.3 / 15.6 °C at Kolsass (obs 20.6 at 5 cm); MYNN control wrfinput (01 UT) 12.0 / 14.1 / 15.9 / 15.6 (obs 18.6); X12ma at 13 UT 19.2 / 16.9 / 15.6 / 15.6 (obs 20.7)** — every run starts from it, the chains recover only 2 K of it by 23 UT (X12mb 17.6/17.7) and the MYNN control's top layer swings 12 → 23.5 → 16.6 °C over the 18th (obs 18.6 → 21.5 → 19.8). met_em (native ICON soil levels, 21 UT): SOILT at 0.5/2/6/18/54/162/486 cm = 12.4/12.4/13.8/15.4/16.4/15.5/13.3 °C, SOILHGT 569 m vs HGT 562 — no elevation issue; **raw ICON GRIB (13 UT run +1 h)**: Kolsass 20.7/20.7/18.8/17.2/15.6/15.6/13.4/9.7 °C at 0.5/2/6/18/54/162/486/1458 cm, Eggen 17.7/17.7/16.5/15.5/14.2/14.2, StanserJoch 7.8/7.8/8.9/9.1/8.2/8.4 — ICON's own soil, not the conversion: its top follows the skin (20.7 at 14 UT, 12.4 at 21 UT, a 5 K nocturnal drop at 6 cm where the probe drops 1 K) because the column below sits 3–5 K colder than measured (54 cm: 15.6 vs ~20.5 at Kolsass; 14.2 vs ≈18 at Eggen; 8.2 vs ≈11 at StanserJoch). **Consequence for the night (measured):** the ground supplies the surface 20–25 W m⁻² less than observed (GRDFLX −30 / −25 / −14 at Kolsass / Weerberg / Hochhäuser vs observed residual −47…−49 / −47 / −41), the air pays it (HFX −13 / −21 / −42 vs sonic −4 / −16 / −14), the skin is colder and the katabatic forcing stronger (drainage 1.5–2.5× at Eggen/Terfens/StanserJoch). A soil this cold also sinks extra heat by day (weaker sensible heating, shallower CBL, weaker valley wind) — the daytime under-forecast of the valley wind (MYNN control) and the 2 K wall-layer deficit now have a common upstream suspect. **Sign for the nocturnal pool NOT predictable from the budget alone:** a warmer soil reduces the model's excess air-to-surface flux (which cools the air) but also weakens the over-strong drainage and warms the skin; only a twin run tells. **Proposed fix (default-off, setup-level, no source change; needs Elias's go):** `fix_soil_temp.py` rewriting `TSLB` (and `TMN` consistently) in a wrfinput from an elevation-dependent profile anchored on the three i-Box soil records at the start hour (Kolsass 545 m, Eggen 829 m, StanserJoch 1861 m: ≈ −6.5 K km⁻¹ at 5 cm; deep layers from BODEN2 ≈ isothermal 20.5 °C at Kolsass), `SMOIS` untouched, validated by `check_wrfinput.py`; twin of X16b (X16s, 21→05 UT) with gates: slope HFX toward −13…−15, GRDFLX toward −41…−47, LW↑ at Weerberg/Hochhäuser, drainage speeds at Eggen/StanserJoch, and the HATPRO 21→01 cooling (−3.4/−3.1 K). Report upstream to the TEAMx ICON providers as well (their soil initialisation). Caveats: Kolsass BODEN2 sensor depths unknown (isothermal reading assumed to span ~1 m); the Eggen `ms` and Kolsass `mso` moisture units taken as vol-%; 30-min model snapshots vs 1-min obs.

Model SEB residual −20 W m⁻² at the two slope sites (canopy storage / snapshot timing) noted, not interpreted. **Retraction 1 — 'slopes 0.6–1.2 K too cold, crests 3.7 K' (~10:30):** it used sonic temperature, which is not a calibrated thermometer. With the ventilated sensors: Eggen model − obs +0.03 K, StanserJoch −1.2 K (≈0.5 of it the 135 m lower cell), Kolsass +1.85 K (floor warm bias stands). The 'inverted slope–core contrast' rested on the same sonic θ and is withdrawn until recomputed with ventilated T (Weerberg/Hochhäuser/Arbeser raw tables not obtained). **Retraction 2 — a ridge-level cold bias of the model (1.5–3.5 K at 1500–2150 m ASL vs HATPRO, found today, not yet reported):** the 05 UT Kolsass sonde agrees with X12m, MYNN and X16b to ±0.3 K from 1600 to 3500 m ASL while HATPRO is +0.7 (1600 m), +1.6 (1900), +2.0 (2200), +3.0 K (2500) too warm; at 17 UT the same. **2026-09-15, ~10:30 — X16w SLOPE-FLOW DIAGNOSES (`x16w_slope_diag.py`, log `exp/x16_judge/X16w_slope_diag.log`): THE MODEL MAKES COLD AIR ON ITS SLOPES TOO VIGOROUSLY, THE SLOPE–CORE CONTRAST IS INVERTED, THE FLOOR'S WARM ADVECTION IS HORIZONTAL AND WARM ONLY AGAINST OVER-COOLED FLOOR AIR, AND THE DEFICIT SITS IN THE 100–250 m LAYER WHERE THE POOL'S CONVERGENCE-DRIVEN LIFTING RUNS AT ~60 %.** Night means 21→01 UT, near-surface, model − sonic θ: Kolsass +1.6, Terfens +0.9 (floor, warm); Eggen −1.2, Weerberg −0.6, Hochhäuser −0.6 (slopes, cold); StanserJoch −3.8, Arbeser −3.6 (crests; model terrain 120–135 m lower explains ~0.5). Drainage speed sonic/model: Eggen 0.75/1.9, Terfens 0.7/1.5, StanserJoch 2.0/3.5 m s⁻¹ (Weerberg, Hochhäuser ≈ right). θ of the slope air minus the valley core at the same height ASL: observed +0.1…+1.3 K (the real core cools faster than its sidewalls), model −0.5…−1.0 K (inverted). Slope-class HFX −37 W m⁻² vs −13…−15 observed; slope-cell 0–50 m budget SGS −2.5 K/h re-warmed by along-slope advection +2.7 (Prandtl balance at 2–3× the observed intensity). **Floor cells (≤10 km), K/h:** 0–50 m tend −0.60 = advZ(−w∂θ/∂z) −0.11 + advH +0.54 + SGS −0.54 + LW −0.57 (cooled in place at −1.1, the converging drainage is warmer than that); 50–150 m −0.43 = advZ −0.27 + LW −0.16 (SGS 0.00); 150–300 m −0.47 = advZ −0.47 + LW −0.12; w over the floor +0.3/+0.7/+1.4 cm s⁻¹ — the pool is lifted by low-level convergence and that is the model's only cooling above 50 m. **Profile 22→00 UT, K, HATPRO/model:** 10 m −1.7/−3.0, 30 m −1.7/−1.9, 100 m −1.6/−1.0, 150 m −1.5/−0.8, 200 m −1.4/−1.0, 400–700 m −1.4…−1.5/−1.0…−1.1 — observed cooling height-uniform to 700 m (≈160 W m⁻² equivalent, i.e. valley-scale import + lifting, not surface or radiation); the model is within 25 % above 300 m, over-cools the lowest 30 m and is short by half in 100–250 m. A 1 cm s⁻¹ ascent through the observed pool stratification (2 K/100 m) alone gives −0.8 K/h. **Reading:** the resolved sidewall circulation at Kolsass is too strong, not too weak, and its cold air feeds the 300–700 m layer (nearly right) rather than the pool; what is short is the pool's deepening — the ascent over the floor driven by low-level convergence from slopes, tributaries and along-valley. Candidate causes, both measurable: (a) the nocturnal slope surface energy balance — RRTMG longwave has no sky-view factor, the sidewalls radiate to the full sky and over-cool by the same order as the HFX excess (24 W m⁻²); (b) the tributary outflows into the Inn at Kolsass (Wattental, Weerbach), marginal on a 500 m grid, which supply the observed low-level convergence. Caveats: HATPRO's ~1 K cold bias below 100 m leaves rates intact; the advZ/advH split uses 30-min means (resolved covariance sits in advH); on slopes the Cartesian split is ±50 K/h and only the sum is read. **Next:** net longwave at slope cells vs the slope-site radiometers (if on disk — ask), and the model's tributary outflow at the Wattental/Weerbach mouths vs any station there.

**2026-09-12, ~20:15 — X16w VERDICT (job 8599482, X16b rerun 21→01 UT with the WRFlux θ budget on, `exp/x16_judge/X16w_budget.log`, script `x16_theta_budget.py`): IN THE CALM VALLEY THE MISSING COOLING IS THE SLOPE-FLOW COLD-AIR IMPORT — the model's diabatic cooling of the floor layer already equals the observed total, resolved advection WARMS the floor, the closure is exactly zero above 100 m, and the 0–300 m column receives no net cold air where the observations need ~35 W m⁻² equivalent.** Window means 21:30→01:00 UT, K/h, Kolsass 5×5 cells (Hall–Jenbach floor in brackets): 0–100 m obs −0.86, model −0.48 (−0.53) = adv **+0.39** (+0.20) + SGS −0.50 (−0.37) + LW −0.39 (−0.39); 100–300 m obs −0.77, model −0.44 (−0.47) = adv −0.33 (−0.39) + SGS **0.00** + LW −0.13; 0–300 m obs −0.82, model −0.46 (−0.49) = adv −0.02 (−0.14) + SGS −0.21 + LW −0.24. Closure gap ≤ 0.03 on the means, but +0.15…+0.23 K/h in every layer after 00 UT (the 6th-order filter warming a sharpened pool, cf. 2026-09-01). HFX at Kolsass −12…−27 W m⁻² (sonic −2…−7). **Reading:** (1) surface and radiation are not short: SGS + LW on the floor = −0.89 K/h > observed −0.86, and RRTMG's −0.24 K/h over 300 m is a normal clear-sky divergence; (2) the resolved slope circulation warms the floor at +0.2…+0.4 K/h — the drainage arrives warmer than the floor air or the return branch subsides warm air onto it (the calm-case isolation of C1, 'drainage arrives too warm'); (3) above 100 m the closure is laminar all night while the slope-site sonics hold q² 0.2–0.4 m² s⁻² — the layer cools only by advection; (4) over 0–300 m the resolved transport is net zero at Kolsass (redistribution only), so the observed extra −0.36 K/h must be cold air imported from the slopes, which the model does not deliver. This confirms the demotion of the surface-coupling family (~14:30) with the model's own budget and moves the hunt to the slope drainage: how cold it is when it leaves the slope, how much it is diluted on the way down (closure on slopes, filter, implicit diffusion), and where it detaches. **Next (diagnoses on X16w, no run):** (a) slope drainage vs the slope-site sonics (Eggen 829, Weerberg 930, Hochhäuser 1009 m: `mean_spd`, `wdir`, sonic θ) — speed, depth, temperature deficit; (b) a cross-valley section at Kolsass of W_MEAN and the resolved θ advection per term to separate warm subsidence from warm horizontal import; (c) the slope-cell θ budget (SGS vs adv on the slope band) to see whether the drainage current is over-mixed. Trap E49 (missing output dir) cost the first attempt.

Both surface layers collapse 5–100× below the sonics; the MYNN surface layer is marginally less collapsed at two floor sites and worse at the crest. **θ bias vs HATPRO at Kolsass (0–100/100–300/300–600 m):** 18 July 23 UT sfclay=5 +3.92/+2.13/+1.49 vs sfclay=1 +3.30/+2.06/+1.31; spin-up night 18 July 03 UT +4.01/+2.43/+1.07 vs +1.89/+1.78/+1.16 — the MYNN surface layer is 0.6–2 K WARMER at the floor, never cooler (forcing differs, so only the sign of the surface-scheme effect is read: no help). **Skin:** LW up at Kolsass 18 July 23 UT obs 377, sfclay=5 382, sfclay=1 379 W m⁻² — both skins at or 1 K above observed on this night (X16b's 1.5 K cold skin on the 17th is not reproduced by the MYNN runs). **Surface sensible heat flux H at Kolsass (sonic level 1, W m⁻², upward positive):** 17→18 night 22–04 UT observed −2…−7 (one +4); X16b −10…−16, X12m −4…−14, X15 −4…−15, MYNN −13…−23; 18→19 night 21–00 UT observed −15, +1, −1, −3; sfclay=5 −31, −24, −11, −20; sfclay=1 −25, −17, −17, −11. Seven-site night medians: observed −1…−15, models −10…−38. Ground flux: model GRDFLX −22…−50 (soil feeding the surface), observed soil+storage ≈ 49 (2026-09-01) — same magnitude. **Reading (the mechanism):** the real valley air cools 0.8–1.1 K h⁻¹ over 0–300 m (≈ 80–100 W m⁻² equivalent) while giving the surface only 2–15 W m⁻²; the model surface takes 10–30 W m⁻² from the air — two to five times more — and the air still cools 20–40 % too slowly. So the surface-to-air heat transfer is not missing; it is, if anything, too strong (a warm air mass over a cooling skin, with MOST delivering exchange even at the u* floor). The observed cooling of the column must come from the air's own processes — longwave flux divergence and cold-air import (drainage, along-valley) — and that is where the model's deficit lives. The wall-q² deficit (100× at the sonics) is real but is not the carrier of the heat: closing it cannot cool the pool, which X15 (warmer), EVE2 (null) and now the MYNN surface layer (warmer) all say from three directions. **Demoted:** the surface-coupling lever family, including the planned X16c (wall-q² + relaxed u* floor) — not worth 2.5 h of 5 nodes unless as a formal closure of the family. **Promoted:** (1) the radiative-divergence term of the θ budget in 0–300 m vs an estimate from the observed profiles (RRTMG's clear-sky cooling of a 2–4 K too-warm, possibly too-moist layer) and (2) the cold-air import — the slope-flow and along-valley advection terms of the WRFlux reach budget held against the slope-site sonics and the tower; both are diagnoses on existing archives, no run. Caveats: sonic H under calm stable conditions is a lower bound in magnitude (non-stationarity, no spectral gap), but the observed surface energy balance closes within ~10 W m⁻² with it (net LW −58, soil+storage 49), so the bound is tight; the sfclay=5 run has different forcing, boundary interval and model top, so its warmer floor is not attributed to the surface layer alone.

**2026-09-11, ~06:30 — X16b VERDICT (evening cold start, 21 → 05 UT, job 8590062, judge `exp/x16_judge/X16b.log`): THE EVENING WIND IS INHERITED, NOT MADE — from ICON's calm, flat 21 UT state WRF holds the calm and never re-forms the up-valley wind or its pressure force; BUT THE CALM VALLEY STILL COOLS 40 % TOO SLOWLY under a clear sky with a skin colder than observed, and from 01:30 UT it drains at 2.5–3.4 m s⁻¹ where the tower and lidar stay below 0.6.** Run: 5 × 128, 8 h in ≈2.6 h wall, 17 frames, cloud-free (GLW within +4 W m⁻² of the radiometer 21:30 → 05:00; one thin layer at 2.2 km at 22:00 only).
**Gate (a)/(b) — measured at Kolsass, 50–150 / 150–300 m, m s⁻¹ (lidar | X16b | X12m):** 22:00 0.6/1.5 | 1.2/0.6 | 1.9/2.4; 22:30 0.4/1.4 | 0.6/0.7 | 1.2/2.4; 23:00 0.1/0.7 | 0.3/0.2 | 0.5/2.1; 00:00 0.1/0.3 | 0.5/0.9 | 1.3/0.6. Hall–Jenbach force (10⁻⁴ m s⁻²): 21:30 −4.2, 22:00 +0.2, 22:30 +2.4, 23:00 −1.5 (X12m +3.4, +5.3, −2.1, −5.8) — no pulse. Reach contrast (Hall–Jenbach minus Strass–Brixlegg, first level): −0.37 K at 21, +0.68 at 22, +0.65 at 23, −0.1 at 00–01 UT (X12m +1.3…+2.0) — WRF makes a third of the warm reach from flat and loses it by midnight. The tower (SONIC2, four levels to 20 m) reads 0.3–0.8 m s⁻¹ from 21 to 04 UT. → **Gate (b): the persisting up-valley force of the chains is the daytime along-valley contrast carried into the evening, not a product of the evening physics.** The 22 UT pulse of X12m is that inheritance plus the cloud lid (A26).
**Second finding — the calm valley's own cooling deficit (measured, θ vs HATPRO, 0–100 / 100–300 / 300–600 m):** 21:00 +0.90/+0.22/+0.03 (ICON's state is already 0.9 K warm at the floor), 22:00 +1.46/+0.77/+0.20, 23:00 +1.57/+0.78/+0.20, 01:00 +2.21/+1.54/+0.33, 05:00 +2.21/+2.02/+1.25 (X12m chain at 05: +3.30/+2.65/+1.80). Cooling 21 → 01 UT: observed −3.4 K (0–100 m) and −3.1 K (100–300 m); X16b −2.1 and −1.8 — 38 % and 42 % short, in a calm, clear valley. Surface: model longwave up 356–358 W m⁻² against 362–365 observed at 23–01 UT (skin ≈1.5 K colder than the real surface), 2 m air +0.5…+1.1 K warmer, 0–100 m +1.5…+2.2 K warmer — a stronger-than-observed skin inversion: the surface cools, the air above does not follow. This is the wall-layer collapse of the sonic referee (2026-09-09 ~20:00) isolated from the wind error: with no mean wind to mix warm air down, the missing wall turbulence can only be a cooling deficit. **The X15 switch (`pbl3d_sfc_qsq_bc=1`) must be retested on this run** — its "warmer" verdict came from a windy floor (2026-09-10 ~08:30), the mechanism that produced it is absent here.
**Third finding — the spurious drainage:** from 01:30 UT X16b runs 2.5–3.4 m s⁻¹ down-valley at 50–300 m (from ~250°) to 05 UT, driven by a valley-scale down-valley force of −6…−14·10⁻⁴ m s⁻² (Strass minus Hall −15…−18 Pa at 850 m at 00–02 UT) balanced by along-valley advection; the tower stays at 0.3–0.5 m s⁻¹, the lidar 0.3 at 00 UT (no lidar after 00:30). The floor bias jumps +2.3 → +3.1 K at 03:30 with it. The chains and ICON drain at ~1–1.6 m s⁻¹ from 23 UT on; X16b's is stronger — the real cold pool is dense enough to decouple from the along-valley gradient, the model's is not (or the model's nocturnal along-valley gradient is too large: no downstream pressure observation on disk).
**Accounting.** At 05 UT the chains carry +3.3 K at the floor, X16b +2.2 K: the inherited evening wind is worth ≈1.1 K; the calm-valley coupling deficit (≈1.3 K by 01 UT) and the drainage own the rest, 0.9 K of which ICON's 21 UT state already holds. **Consequences:** (1) the evening-wind lever is on the day side — the afternoon insolation/cloud error over the wide reach (A26 follow-up, obs/model SW 278/456 at 16 UT) sets the 19:30 contrast; (2) the nocturnal lever is the wall coupling, retested cleanly as X16b + `pbl3d_sfc_qsq_bc=1` (same wrfinput, one key; proposal, not submitted); (3) the drainage needs a downstream pressure/wind observation (Jenbach–Kundl) before any model change. Fixes: `evening_momentum_budget.py` no longer writes to the scratchpad; `valley_transect.py` draws the transect for its first argument.
**2026-09-10, ~15:00 — X16b SUBMITTED: THE EVENING COLD START — WRF initialised at 2025-07-17 21:00 UT from the native ICON analysis (calm Kolsass 0.2/1.7 m s⁻¹, along-valley θ flat within ±0.5 K, Hall–Jenbach pressure force ≈ 0), 8 h to 05 UT, X12m physics and output, one run directory (`branko_runs/innval_pbl3d_X16b`, env `vsc5_X16b.sh`, archive `exp/X16b`).** Jobs: real 8590061 (devel) → wrf 8590062 (`zen3_0512`, 5 × 128, 3:15; afterok) → report-only judge 8590063 (devel, afterok; `exp/x16_judge/judge_x16.slurm` → `exp/x16_judge/X16b.log`: judge_x12 X16 b against X12mb/X10b, lidar and HATPRO; `valley_transect.py X16b X12mb X10b`; the radiometer/cloud check; the Kolsass momentum budget). (a) If by 22–23 UT the Kolsass 150–300 m wind is back at ≥ 2 m s⁻¹ up-valley, the Hall–Jenbach force ≥ +3·10⁻⁴ m s⁻² and the reach contrast ≥ +1 K, WRF re-creates the warm wide reach from a flat state → the evening physics (sidewall circulation / venting at 500 m) is the origin, and the 250 m nest over Hall–Strass is the next run. (b) If the wind stays ≤ 1 m s⁻¹, the force stays ≤ +1·10⁻⁴ and the 0–100 m cooling to 01 UT is within 0.5 K of HATPRO (obs 292.7 → 289.3 K, 21 → 01 UT), the daytime heating of the reach is the origin → the afternoon cloud/insolation error (obs/model SW 278/456 at 16 UT) is the target. Caveats: no spin-up (first hour discounted: q² from `pbl3d_init_opt=0`, soil from ICON), ICON's own 1 m s⁻¹ midnight drainage is inherited, and a cold start cannot separate ICON's evening state from truth — the lidar/HATPRO comparison does that. **Initial state verified (wrfinput, 21 UT):** all checks pass (SMOIS mean 0.285), Kolsass 0.3 / 1.6 m s⁻¹ at 50–150 / 150–300 m (lidar 0.2 / 1.7), reach contrast −0.37 K, Strass-minus-Hall pressure +0.9 Pa at 850 m, reach up-valley wind +0.6 m s⁻¹ — against X12mb's +1.95 K, +3.9 Pa, +1.2 m s⁻¹ at the same hour: the test starts from the calm, flat valley it was meant to.
**2026-09-10, ~14:30 — A26 FOLLOW-UP: THE 18TH EVENING IS CLOUD-FREE IN MODEL AND RADIOMETER (its late transition is not a cloud effect); THE RAW ICON HAS THE MOIST LAYER AT 19–20 UT AND DISSIPATES IT BY 22 UT, WRF KEEPS IT.** Scripts `evening17_cloud_lw_check.py` (now `DAY=18 PREV=e … X12mf X12pf X15f X10f`), `icon_raw_rh_check.py` (`icon` env, cfgrib). **18 July (measured):** X12mf/X12pf/X10f GLW within −8…+0 W m⁻² of the Kolsass radiometer from 19:30 to 22:00 UT (observed 346 → 331), no cloud column at Kolsass after 17:30; the model over-insolates the afternoon again (obs/model SW 514/800 at 13:30, 386/528 at 15:30) and carries one cloud at 2.4–2.7 km at 16:30–17:00 (GLW +39 at 17:00) that is gone by 17:30. So the 18th's 21 UT up-valley 4.6–6.5 m/s against a 1–2 m/s lidar is a clear-sky failure of the transition — the old-lineage kind, not the 17th's cloud kind. **Raw ICON (17 July, GRIB on disk = pres, t, u, v, q, h; no cloud water, no cloud cover — the one unnamed surface field is z₀):** Kolsass RH at 2.5/3 km ASL 89/90 (19 UT), 92/91 (20), 85/88 (21), 90/85 (22), 90/84 (23) — the met_em's 89–98 % is ICON's own within the conversion. The radiometer says clear at Kolsass at both times, so the forcing is wrong at 20 UT too (or its cloud is thin/elsewhere in the box). **Consequences:** the 19:00-restart twin with humidity above 2 km capped at 90 % tests both halves at once (the initial layer and its persistence; the lateral boundaries are >150 km away and cannot resupply it within 4 h); the ACINN FTP inventory should be checked for ICON cloud fields (CLC, QC, CLCT) before the next download (`icon-data` skill) so the forcing's cloud can be judged directly.
**2026-09-10, ~14:00 — THE CHEAP CHECK FOUND THE 22–23 UT STALL: A SPURIOUS MID-LEVEL CLOUD DECK FROM THE NATIVE ICON FORCING CUTS THE SURFACE LONGWAVE LOSS OF THE KOLSASS REACH BY TWO THIRDS AT 21:30–23:00 UT — and it is absent in the old-forcing runs, so the two lineages reach the same pool deficit by different routes.** Script `wrf3dpbl-diag/evening17_cloud_lw_check.py`. **Measured (Kolsass i-Box radiometer 70322 vs the Kolsass cell):** observed longwave down 322 → 304 W m⁻² from 19:30 to 01 UT, a clear sky all evening; X12m's GLW equals it within ±5 W m⁻² in the clear windows (20:30–21:00, 23:30–01:00) and is **+44/+59 at 19:30/20:00 and +62…+64 at 21:30–23:00** — the surface net longwave loss at 22 UT is 21 W m⁻² in the model against 58 observed. The cloud is a stratus deck at 1.9–2.5 km AGL (2.5–3 km ASL, above the ridges), 0.1–0.3 g kg⁻¹, over 25–60 % of the Hall–Jenbach floor cells between 19:30 and 23:00, none after 23:30. **Lineage split (GLW − obs at 21:30–23:00):** X12m +62, X12p +63, X15 +63, ICON-terrain twin +62 at 21:30 then +3…+5 (and +63 at 23:30); **X10b +3…+7, EVE1 +2…+7, EVE1M +7…+21** — the old-forcing runs are cloud-free and match the radiometer all evening. **Source:** the ICON native-level met_em carries RH 89–98 % at 2.5–3 km ASL over Kolsass at 19–23 UT (the 36-level pressure ladder smoothed it to 79–89 %); X12m saturates that layer (RH 97–101 %) and Thompson condenses it; 23 % of all domain columns have cloud at 2–3.5 km at 22 UT against 13 % in X10 (domain-mean GLW +6 W m⁻²). Whether ICON itself carried a cloud there is not checked (met_em has no cloud fields; the raw GRIB may). The observed shortwave also says the real afternoon at Kolsass was cloudier than the model's (obs/model 278/456 at 16 UT, 159/282 at 17 UT, model right at 14 UT) while the model put its afternoon cloud over the lower valley (SWDOWN 380 vs 719 at 14 UT, reach B vs A) — the along-valley heating contrast of the afternoon is at least partly a cloud-placement error. **Not a defect:** land-use classes 31–33 (27 % of the Kolsass-reach floor cells) are the project's CORINE-to-USGS urban extension with urban parameters in `NoahmpTable.TBL`; soil moisture (0.28 vs 0.30) and vegetation fraction are alike in the two reaches. **Reading:** X12m's 22–23 UT cooling stall and the second up-valley pressure pulse (~13:15 entry) have a forcing-side cause: the native forcing's moist layer aloft becomes a longwave lid over the wide reach at exactly the hour the reach must lose its afternoon warmth. This does not explain the old lineage, which is cloud-free and never collapses — the mature-pool invariance (2026-09-07/08) hides two different evening errors. **Consequences:** (1) the native forcing is kept (its evening gain of ~0.5 K and the right collapse hour stand), but the 17th's evening in the X12/X15 lineage cannot be judged on 21:30–23:00 cooling until the cloud is dealt with; (2) cheapest test: the same 19:00 restart with the 2.5–3 km moisture of the forcing capped below saturation (or the ladder forcing's humidity above 2 km) — if the reach then keeps cooling through 22–23 UT and the wind stays down, the second pulse is the cloud's; (3) check the raw ICON GRIB for cloud at 2.5–3 km at 20–22 UT before blaming icon2wrf; (4) the observed afternoon cloud at Kolsass (16–17 UT) is the next thing to compare, since it sets the 19:30 state.
**2026-09-10, ~13:15 — THE EVENING WIND AT KOLSASS IS HELD BY THE VALLEY-SCALE ALONG-VALLEY PRESSURE GRADIENT, AND THAT GRADIENT IS THE DAYTIME ONE DECAYING TOO SLOWLY: the wide Hall–Jenbach reach stays 1–2 K warmer than the reach beyond Strass through the evening, so the up-valley pressure force is still +3…+5·10⁻⁴ m s⁻² at 22 UT (in all seven evening runs) where the ICON forcing has ~0 by 21 UT and the lidar is calm.** Scripts `wrf3dpbl-diag/evening_momentum_budget.py` (Kolsass column, fixed heights), `valley_transect.py` (Innsbruck→Kufstein transect, Hall–Jenbach PGF per run, ICON met_em), `evening17_reach_fig.py` (figure `plot_output/evening_budget/evening17_reach_pressure.png`, reach means), `reach_theta_budget.py` (WRFlux θ budget per reach, EVE archives).
**Measured (X12m b-segment, 17 July 19:30→01:00, frames + meanout).** (1) The **local** Kolsass momentum budget on fixed heights does not attribute the 22 UT re-acceleration: pressure gradient and along-valley advection are each ±3·10⁻³ m s⁻² and cancel, and the local ∂p/∂s changes by a factor 2–4 between 1-, 2- and 3-cell stencils (2Δx pressure noise) — a local budget at one column is the wrong tool here (E48). (2) The **valley-scale** gradient (p at 850 m ASL, Hall ↔ Jenbach, 23 km) is robust: +7…+9·10⁻⁴ up-valley at 19:30–20:00, +1…+2 at 20:30–21:00, **+3.4 (21:30) and +5.3 (22:00)** — a second pulse that coincides with the 21:30→22:00 tendency of +12/+10·10⁻⁴ m s⁻² (0.5 → 2.2 m/s up-valley at 50–150/150–300 m) — then −2 at 22:30 and −3…−6 (down-valley) from 23 UT on. **All seven evening runs show the 22 UT pulse** (X12m +5.35, X12p +5.41, ICON-terrain +4.48, wall-q² +3.99, X10 +3.53, EVE1 +4.67, EVE1M MYNN +4.60): a model trait, closure- and forcing-independent. **The ICON forcing itself** (native met_em, hourly): +12 at 19, +4.6 at 20, ~0 at 21, +0.9 at 22, −3.8 at 23 UT; ICON's own Kolsass wind is 0.2/1.7 m/s at 21 UT and 0.5/0.4 at 22 UT (50–150/150–300 m) — the calm the lidar shows (0.2/1.7, 0.6/1.5). (3) **Origin, hydrostatic.** First-level θ, floor cells within 2.5 km of the valley axis: Hall–Jenbach reach (km 8–34) minus Strass–Brixlegg reach (km 38–48) = +1.1…+2.0 K all afternoon (14–18 UT, p850 difference +32…+46 Pa = the daytime valley-wind forcing), **+1.4 K at 19:30, +1.7 at 20:00, +2.0 at 21:00, +1.3 at 22:00, +1.7 at 22:30, +1.5 at 23:00**, +0.9 at 23:30; p at 850 m, Strass reach minus Hall reach: +15 (19:30), +13 (20:00), +6 (20:30), +4 (21:00), +6 (21:30), **+8 (22:00)**, +1 (22:30), −1.5 (23:00), −8 (23:30). The Kolsass reach's first-level cooling **stalls at 22:00–23:00** (293.76 → 293.74 → 293.70 K) while the downstream reach keeps cooling (292.47 → 292.00 → 292.22), during the re-acceleration and with resolved ascent of +8…+17 cm/s in the Kolsass column at 21–22 UT. ICON's 0–300 m θ along the same transect is flat within ±0.5 K at 21–22 UT and its p850 transect within ±3 Pa; the HATPRO 0–100 m at Kolsass at 22 UT (291.6 K) sits with the model's *downstream* reach (291.2–292.7), not with its Kolsass reach (293.3–294.8) — the wide reach is the anomaly. (4) **θ budget per reach (EVE1/EVE1M, old forcing, the only evening archives with a populated flux set):** both reaches cool at −0.6…−1.0 K/h in 0–100 m from 20 to 21:30 UT with LW −0.33, subgrid −0.4…−0.6 and resolved advection within ±0.3 K/h, closure gap ≤ 0.3 K/h; the 22–23 UT stall of X12m cannot be decomposed — its archive's flux fields are zero (E48). (5) **After 23 UT the ~1–2 m/s down-valley drainage at Kolsass (lidar 0.1–0.6) is shared with ICON** (1.2–1.6 m/s from 00 UT): not a WRF trait.
**Reading.** The evening problem is not a nocturnal process but the decay of the daytime along-valley heating contrast: the wide reach must lose its afternoon warmth relative to the narrow reach before the up-valley pressure force dies, and in WRF it does not (still +2.2 K vs HATPRO at 19:30, the whole formation-hour deficit). The residual wind then keeps the floor coupled (the 2026-09-10 ~11:30 hypothesis), and the 22–23 UT stall of the reach's cooling with ascent over the floor is the feedback's closed loop. Consequences for the option list: (a) `w_damping`/refined levels and the 3D-APPROX twin can only act through the evening cooling of the wide reach — judge them on the reach contrast at 20–22 UT (`valley_transect.py`), not on Kolsass alone; (b) the 250 m nest must cover Hall→Strass, not the Kolsass reach only; (c) the first thing to compare with observations is the along-valley θ/pressure structure at 19–22 UT — no station downstream of Jenbach is on disk (the i-Box sites are all in the Kolsass reach) — ask for TEAMx/ZAMG stations at Jenbach, Strass, Brixlegg, Kundl (pressure + T2) for 17 July. Not measured: what heats the wide reach more by day (its HFX at 14 UT is 2× the downstream reach's, SWDOWN 750 vs 423 W m⁻² — cloud over the lower valley?) — the afternoon frames of the a-segment are hourly and have no flux set.
**2026-09-10, ~11:30 — THE POOL DEFICIT IS MADE IN THE EVENING, NOT AT NIGHT — the failed transition and the deficit growth measured on one time axis (report Sec. 3, figure `evening17_wind_theta.png`, script `wrf3dpbl-diag/evening17_wind_theta.py`, parsed from the formation-night judge table).** 17–18 July, 19:30→01:00: observed Kolsass 50–150 m falls 3.5 → 0.2 m/s by 21:00 and stays 0.1–0.6 m/s to midnight (a genuine four-hour calm), 150–300 m decays 3.4 → 1.7 → 0.7; the native-forcing run reaches the collapse at the right hour (0.6 m/s at 21:00) but does not hold it (1.9 at 22:00; 2.1–2.4 through 21:30–23:00 at 150–300 m), and the old-forcing chain never collapses (2.3 at 21:00, 3.3–3.8 aloft during the observed calm). **Cost, same window:** observed cooling −1.09 (0–100 m) and −0.96 K/h (100–300 m) vs model −0.86 and −0.68 — shortfalls of 21 % and **29 %**, so the bias grows +1.29 K at the floor and **+1.55 K in 100–300 m**, i.e. fastest in the layer that stays windy. **Decisive accounting:** the 0–100 m bias is already +3.49 K at 01 UT against +3.30 K at the 05 UT sounding hour, and the deep-night rate that follows is right (−0.35 vs −0.31 K/h observed, 2026-09-01) — **the entire mature-pool deficit is laid down before midnight**. This explains the invariance result (2026-09-07/08) rather than competing with it: none of the four eliminated levers (closure, initial state, forcing resolution, terrain) changes the evening wind. Hypothesis, labelled as such in the report: the residual ~2 m/s keeps the floor coupled to the warmer air above, so radiative cooling acts on a layer still being mixed from above, and the same flow keeps the warm wall intrusion supplied.

**2026-09-10, ~09:30 — THE TWO CRASHES ARE DIAGNOSED AND THEY ARE **DIFFERENT** FAILURES SHARING ONE MESSENGER (amends E46): the refined-level run dies of a vertical-ADVECTION Courant margin destroyed by thin layers; the length-scale run dies of a vertical-DIFFUSION number destroyed by a doubled master length scale. `SFCLAYREV produced NaN` is only the first routine that checks.** Analysis from the X13r reproduction's 1-minute frames (15 frames 16:35–16:49, `exp/X13r/temp/branko/qsqdiag_*`) plus the X14 smoke's 13:10 frame; scripts: `wrf3dpbl-diag/` (inline, see this entry).

**X13 (e_vert 89) — vertical advection.** No field is NaN ANYWHERE in the domain 54 s before the fatal step (PSFC/W/T/Q_SQ all finite at 16:49:00), so the NaN is born in the last minute, not inherited — the earlier p_sfc-propagation hypothesis is retired. Vertical Courant (mass-level w, Δt = 2 s, lowest 12 layers): **refined max 1.78 with 13 768 cells above 0.8, parent (80 levels) max 1.16 with 302** — refinement halved Δz (16.0 → 10.1 m at the crash cell) at unchanged w (domain max |w| ≈ 9 m/s in both), so the Courant field roughly doubled and the population near the limit grew ×45. The crash cell itself was at Courant 0.20–0.26 at 16:45 but carried a plume growing ×1.41/min for four minutes (w(k1) 0.90 → 1.02 → 1.69 → 2.38 m/s; u* 0.40 → 0.65; q² 0.70 → 1.22; PSFC falling 11 Pa/min); Courant 1 needs w = 5.1 m/s there, reached in ~2.2 min at that rate. Verdict: a numerically marginal configuration, first failing at its most vigorous near-surface updraft — not closure physics, not the surface layer. **Remedy: `w_damping = 1`** (WRF's targeted remedy — damps w only where the vertical Courant is violated, essentially free), fallback Δt = 1.5 s (Courant ×0.75, max 1.78 → 1.33, +33 % cost). Footprint of `w_damping` must be measured when used (count and distribution of damped points; it can clip genuine updrafts).

**X14 (`pbl3d_l_opt=2`) — vertical diffusion, and the switch was working as intended.** Ten minutes after an identical restart, the MYNN-blend scale gives l(k<12) median 34.1 m / p99 114 m against l_opt=1's 16.1 / 45.4 (×2.1 / ×2.5) and q² p99 8.24 vs 4.63 — i.e. **the carrying-capacity increase the w-spectrum called for did happen**. But the explicit vertical mixing cannot integrate it: the diffusion number KΔt/Δz² (K ≈ ½ q l) reaches **max 1.2 with 87 151 cells above the 0.5 explicit-stability limit (≈2.4 % of the lowest 12 levels), against max 0.3 and ZERO such cells for l_opt=1**. Same failure family as E28 (explicit q² diffusion, which is why `pbl3d_sq_implicit` exists — for q² only); inferred, not code-verified here, that the momentum/heat flux divergences are likewise handed to the dynamics explicitly. Δt alone is a poor remedy (dn ∝ Δt: 1.0 s → max 0.6, still over the limit; 0.75 s → 0.45, +165 % cost). **Proposed remedy (default-off, minimal, in the same family as the existing strain cap `pbl3d_sk_eps_max`): cap l where the explicit diffusion number would exceed ≈0.4** — it touches ~2.4 % of near-surface cells and leaves the median (dn 0.10) untouched, so the experiment keeps its substance at Δt = 2 s. Alternative, cleaner but larger: implicit vertical mixing for momentum/heat as `pbl3d_sq_implicit` did for q².

**Both smoke gates earned their keep**: 45 min each instead of 85–95 node-hours.

**2026-09-10, ~08:30 — X15 VERDICT: THE WALL-q² CONDITION WORKS MECHANICALLY AND THE POOL GETS SLIGHTLY *WARMER* — the conveyor hypothesis is refuted for the part it could test; the u* floor now gates the rest.** Segments b–d completed overnight (8581900/8583103/8583311; judges X15b/c/d). **The switch did what it was built to do** (verified at 04 UT, Kolsass cell + Inn-floor mask HGT<700 m): valley-floor median q² 0.0228 → 0.0780 m² s⁻² (×3.4), fraction of floor cells above 0.05 rose 39 → 58 %, and at Kolsass q² went from exactly 0 to 0.0063 = 6.5·(0.03)² to three digits — the predicted B₁^{2/3}u*² value at a cell pinned to `sfclay_ust_min`. **The pool responded the wrong way**: θ bias vs HATPRO (0–100/100–300/300–600 m) X15 vs twin X12m — 20:30 +2.90/+1.22/+0.12 vs +2.54/+1.19/+0.11; 01:00 +3.74/+2.27/+1.13 vs +3.49/+2.21/+1.13; **05 UT +3.48/+2.75/+1.80 vs +3.30/+2.65/+1.78** — systematically 0.2–0.35 K warmer at the floor, unchanged aloft. Reading: added wall turbulence mixes the (warmer) air above down onto the floor faster than it couples the air to the cooling surface; restoring wall q² does not cool the pool. **What the test could NOT reach:** at the pool core the model's u* is pinned at the artificial floor 0.03 m s⁻¹ (observed 0.07–0.16), so the condition can only deliver 0.006 there against an observed 0.13 — 20× low. The two levers were tested separately (EVE2: native caps = clean null; X15: wall-q² = null/slightly worse) but **the u* floor GATES the wall-q² source, so the combination is not the sum of two nulls** — next cheap experiment: X15 physics with `sfclay_ust_min=0.001` (EVE2 measured the relaxed caps harmless), which lets q² climb toward the sonic plateau at the pool core. Fifth pool lever (after closure, IC, forcing levels, terrain) now measured out in its tested range.

**2026-09-10, ~08:30 — X14 SMOKE FAILED WITH THE X13 SIGNATURE: `SFCLAYREV produced NaN` (hfx NaN, ust/chs finite, br = zol = 0) — the second unrelated switch to trigger the same crash, which promotes A24 from "the refined-level run's crash" to a LATENT INSTABILITY the configuration hits whenever near-surface mixing is increased.** X14smoke (8581406, `pbl3d_l_opt=2`) died after 6:23 wall at simulated 13:13:52 (≈14 min in), ranks 144/145, cells i,j = 150,118 and 152,115 with **wspd 31.5 and 7.2 m s⁻¹** and ust 1.38 — the 31 m/s says the resolved flow blew up locally first, i.e. the surface-layer NaN is the symptom, not the source (same conclusion the X13 analysis reached from the p_sfc chain). Consequences: (1) the length-scale experiment is BLOCKED until the mechanism is known; (2) the X13r reproduction's 1-minute frames (on disk, unanalysed, `exp/x13_diag/`) now serve BOTH runs — whichever field goes NaN first is the shared answer; (3) the smoke gate earned its keep twice (45 min wasted, not 85 node-hours). Recorded as E46 in KNOWN_ISSUES with the shared fingerprint.

**2026-09-09, ~23:40 — AUTO-WAKE (fresh context): X14's GATING SMOKE DIED (job 8581406, `pbl3d_l_opt=2`, 5 × 128, 13→14 UT) AT 2025-07-17 13:13:52 — 416 steps in — WITH THE A24 FINGERPRINT ON TWO NEIGHBOURING CREST CELLS; PHYSICS CRASH, CHAIN HELD, `pbl3d_l_opt=2` IS NOT RE-RUN AS IS, NOTHING RETUNED.** Measured: rank 144, i=150 j=118 (`znt 0.002`, barren): `ust 1.380, hfx NaN, chs 0.0460, br 0, zol 0, wspd 31.54 m/s, tsk 289.3, t1 281.5`; rank 145, i=152 j=115 (`znt 1.09`, forest): `ust 1.312, hfx NaN, br 0, zol 0, wspd 7.22, tsk 292.9, t1 283.5` — same step, so the NaN was already a ≥ 2-cell patch (the first printed cell is not the source; E43 applies). The twin X12ma (option 1, same grid/layout/wrfinput) ran this hour and the next five clean; the namelist differs in the one key. **Verdict:** a 31.5 m s⁻¹ wind at the first level over bare ground in 14 min is a blown-up flow, not weather; the option changed the flow's stability, not its forcing. **Inferred from `module_pbl3d_my.F:492–559` (arrays unread — python denied):** option 2 lengthens l far beyond "MYNN-style blending": α₁ = 0.8 hard-coded vs α = 0.1 of the production `MY82` set (l₀ 8× longer), the buoyancy cap 0.53 q/N is bypassed in unstable air (l_b = 10¹⁰ m), and in stable air l_b = 1.0 q/N(1 + √(q_c/(l₀N))) ≥ 1.9× the option-1 cap; only the strain cap l ≤ 0.72 q/|S| survives. With forward-Euler tendencies (`module_pbl3d.F:525–603`) the closure's own vertical mixing must keep K Δt/Δz² < ½ — K > 64 m² s⁻¹ is unstable at Δz 16 m, Δt 2 s — and S_M l q reaches that with l ≈ 300 m, q ≈ 3 m s⁻¹ in a convective crest layer. **Working hypothesis, recorded as A25:** X14 (longer l) and X13 (thinner Δz: 13.5 → 8.5 m, criterion 2.5× tighter) hit the same explicit-diffusion limit from two sides, in the same cell class — barren crest beside a forest cell with u* > 1 — at the hottest hour; the twins that kept the production l and Δz both live. **Why held, not fixed:** the event rule (2026-08-31); and the two candidate remedies — an option-2 variant that keeps the production α and the buoyancy cap (Registry-free but a rebuild, E16, and a new switch rather than a silent edit) or an implicit vertical solve for the closure tendencies — would each be applied blind before the 13:10 arrays (3:52 pre-crash) have said whether the diffusion number or the wind oscillation is there. **Value of the event:** the gate worked (E44's complement — a first-ever option dies inside its 1 h smoke, 85 node-h saved); the X14 gates (morning profiles, 11 UT mixed-layer depth, spectrum fraction, 18th scorecard) remain unanswered, and the carrying-capacity deficit keeps its ranking. **Staged (`exp/x14_diag/STAGED_COMMANDS.sh`):** squeue; cancel the dead dependents 8581407/8581408/8581409; `run_diag.sh` → `x14smoke_crash_cell.log` (both cells + X13's cell + twin, k = 1–8 state, Q_SQ/L_MASTER, diffusion-number estimate, domain hot spots; the 13:00 L_MASTER ratio to the twin is the option's pure effect at an identical state); then A24's `run_x13r.sh`. X15's state is unverified (its run dir had only the setup files at 16:05). Trap recorded: E47.

**2026-09-09, ~23:05 — AUTO-WAKE (fresh context): X13r REPRODUCED THE X13a CRASH EXACTLY — same step (2025-07-17 16:49:54), same cell (i=165, j=73), same detector values to four digits (`ust 0.6834, hfx NaN, chs 0.02388, br 0, zol 0, wspd 13.88`); the 15 one-minute frames exist; the array verdict is staged, not made (python denied in the session). Chain stays held, nothing retuned.** Measured: job 8580933 (5 × 128, restart 16:00) ran 22:32–22:56 CEST, 1497 steps at 0.953 s/step (0.851 in X13a; the 1-min stream write, ~4 s each, accounts for the rest), MPI_ABORT from rank 85 like X13a; no other rank reports anything. Bit-for-bit reproduction across a restart on the same layout confirms E14's premise (the blow-up is a deterministic state of the model, not a decomposition artefact) and makes the 1-min record trustworthy: `exp/X13r/temp/branko/qsqdiag_d01_16:35…16:49` (15 × 1.5 GB, all requested fields set — PSFC QSFC MOL RMOL BR ZOL REGIME + W T P PH QVAPOR U V MU + Q_SQ L_MASTER and the four tier diagnostics; the four `W A R N I N G`s are duplicates of T2/Q2/U10/V10 already on the stream) plus nine 5-min history frames 16:05–16:45 in `wrf_output/8580933`. **Correction to the ~14:15 entry:** the crash cell was *not* under the domain-maximum vertical Courant number — the 16:00/16:30 scan (`x13a_crash_cell.log`) gives the cell's column maximum as 0.37 / 0.27 while the domain maximum 1.72 / 1.79 sits at (k 5–6, j 193–194, i 546–549), ~400 cells away; at the cell W(k=1) is a steady +1.7 / +1.2 m/s updraft in ~10 m layers. So candidate (a) — vertical-advection blow-up — is not supported by the state 20 min before the crash and needs the last frames to survive; candidate (b) — the closure in the ~10 m layer — and (c) — the surface layer at a 7 m first level over `znt 0.002 m` (LU 19 barren, SNOWC 0: not snow, the earlier "snow/ice" reading is withdrawn) beside a 1.09 m forest cell — are open. **Staged (`exp/x13_diag/STAGED_COMMANDS.sh`, unrun):** `run_x13r.sh` moves the 1-min frames into the archive (`submit_wrf.slurm` archives history and mean files only — new trap E46) and runs `x13r_frames.py`: per-minute cell state, k = 1–5 dynamics and Courant, closure tiers, 3 × 3 stencils, domain NaN/hot-spot scan, a 15-row time series with the verdict key (a/b/c). Remedies remain proposals: Δt 1.5 s or `w_damping = 1` for (a); a new A-issue for (b); nothing for (c) without the extended detector print (12-min relink, E16 — X14/X15 chains are linking `wrf.exe`, so not now). X12/X14/X15 untouched.

**2026-09-09, ~22:55 — PARKED IDEA (Elias; no action now): a static, full-3D-terrain length scale combined harmonically with the master length scale.** Proposal: pre-compute once, from the terrain and the level heights, `d`, the nearest distance from each cell to the terrain surface in any direction (the wall distance that Spalart–Allmaras and k-ω SST take as input; LES wall damping uses `min(C_s Δ, κ d)`), store it as a state field and form `1/l = 1/l_master + 1/(κ d)` (opt-in; `d = ∞` reproduces the current `l` bit for bit). **Assessment from the code, not yet measured:** the production length scale (`pbl3d_l_opt = 1`, `module_pbl3d_my.F` `Calc_l_master_algebra`) is already the harmonic mean `l = l0 κz/(κz + l0)` of the wall term `κz` (z = height above the local ground) and the asymptotic scale `l0` (α × q-weighted centroid height of the turbulence), followed by the flow caps `l ≤ 0.53 q/N` and `l ≤ 2·6/b₁ · q/|S|`; the only geometry in it is the vertical distance to the terrain of the same column, so the proposal amounts to replacing `z` by `d` in the wall term. A harmonic mean can only shorten `l`, so it binds only where the flow caps leave `l` long. `d` differs from `z` by cos(slope) on a uniform slope and by the horizontal distance to a sidewall only in the columns at the concave foot of the valley walls (≥ 250 m at dx = 500 m). Estimated footprint: night (cold pool, `l` = 3–15 m from the buoyancy/strain caps): < 5 % — an eddy of a few metres sees no terrain but the ground, which `κz` already handles, so **the nocturnal q² deficit (0.16 of MYNN; the missing wall turbulence of the sonic referee, ~20:00 entry) is untouched, and the sign is wrong for it — a static geometric bound can only remove mixing, never create turbulence**. Day: −7 % at 300 m above a 30° slope (`l0` ≈ 100 m: 55 → 51 m), up to about −35 % at 300 m AGL in the floor column beside a sidewall (55 → 35 m), < 30 % at 500 m AGL in the valley centre — confined to one or two columns hugging each sidewall, in the regime where the run resolves 85–91 % of the kinetic energy. Two consistency caveats: the honest version is anisotropic (horizontal terrain distance bounds horizontal eddies, `κz` vertical ones), and a second length for the horizontal fluxes reopens the production/dissipation mismatch the single master length scale (`db3b9176c`) closed; and static geometry is an upper bound on eddy size, never a driver. **Where static topography has first-order content the closure lacks is as a *source*, not a bound:** the sub-500 m terrain (DEM variance within a cell) makes form-drag turbulence the resolved terrain never sees; a TOFD-style q² source (Beljaars et al. 2004 form) has the right sign for the nocturnal slopes — a separate proposal, behind the observational hold. **Cheap check before any code (half a day, no rebuild):** compute `d` from `HGT` and `PH+PHB` of the archived X7 frames, form the modified `l` offline and compare it with the stored `L_MASTER` at 04:00 and 10:00 in slope × height bins (`compare_mynn.py lscale` bins); the fraction of cells where `l` changes by > 10 % decides whether the idea earns a Registry field and a rebuild. Expectation: near zero at night, a few per cent of daytime cells.

Expectation honest: bounded by the model's own u*; a null where u* is floored is itself the answer that the missing turbulence is non-local (intermittency/submeso), pointing at the stochastic-source lever.

**2026-09-08, ~11:45 — THE LITERATURE/SCHEME-COMPARISON FINDINGS AND THEIR RUN IMPLICATIONS, RECORDED (they existed only as un-versioned PDFs in `concept/`).** Three notes now committed as sources at `branko/realcase/project/concept/`: `branko_juliano_schemes.tex` (2026-09-01: the approximate vs full closure, common engine, safeguard stack), `pbl_assumptions_table.tex` (which 1D assumption each scheme drops), `literature_vs_3dfull.tex` (2026-09-07: our configuration and every measured result in two tables with source pointers, held against the grey-zone/terrain literature). The six implications for consequent runs, from its "What follows": (1) **the convective partition result is unproven as turbulence** — coarse-grained LES expects ~¾ of mixed-layer TKE subgrid at Δx/(z_i+h_c)≈0.6, we resolve ~9/10, the signature of grid-set convection (Zhou/Efstathiou/Ching); one midday WRFlux frame gives the w-spectrum, cell spacing vs 6–8Δx and the decision; (2) **the nocturnal q² magnitude must be judged against the i-Box sonics, not MYNN** — observed u* 0.07–0.16 m/s implies wall q² 0.03–0.17 m²/s², *between* the closures; (3) **the length scale before the constants** — the literature (Arthur, Eghdami, Haupt) makes l the first-order lever in both regimes, in opposite directions; `pbl3d_l_opt=2` is built and unrun and is the candidate for the morning-growth/wall-q² deficits (A15–A17) ahead of S_q and any constant; (4) **the surface layer is where literature and our diagnosis meet** (Kaimal's bound, Lehner's 8–28 %, Ward's decoupling = our 0–100 m problem); the Eghdami 3D surface option is the one unrun switch that addresses it; (5) **the horizontal production credit needs its own measurement** — after the pairing fix the six horizontal terms carry ~5–10 % by inference, σ-dependent, no literature value exists: tabulate the nine shear-production shares by regime before any paper states a horizontal-production result; (6) **two agreements safe to state**: the closure signal lives in the wind, not θ (Shin & Dudhia; Goger), and the closure's length scale is z_i-free. Also in its "never measured" list (= known unknowns for interpretation): spectra/effective resolution, nocturnal resolved partition, post-pairing production shares, solved-tensor anisotropy vs i-Box, `pbl3d_l_opt=2`, `limiter_opt=2`, Eghdami surface, any constants but MY82.

The never-quiet floor was the 10-m skin of a premature circulation swap: weak up-valley dies early, drainage/intrusion arrives, the calm interregnum is destroyed. (3) No NNW layer exists at any height over Kolsass at any hour (wdir 48-102 deg, steady; no down-valley onset above 50 m by 22 UT — the tower's 20:30 down-valley film was < 50 m deep). The model's Kolsass floor intrusion is confirmed artifact. (4) At the Radfeld cluster's north-slope site (Kramsach Oberberg 755 m, WLS100S34) a REAL 11.5-12.3 m/s NNW flow sat at 150-300 m AGL at 16-17 UT (R2-filtered; data gone after 17:30) — the regional northerly genuinely descends down-valley, plausibly through the Brandenberg tributary mouth, while reality keeps it off the Kolsass floor. The model brings it down the wrong wall.**

The intrusion is what keeps the floor from ever going quiet.**
Feedback structure: a stratifying valley blocks such an intrusion (sheltering); the model's warmer, better-mixed valley lets it in, which keeps the valley warm - the third face of the same refusal to decouple. (1) The exchange-collapse curve: the observed transition passes through bulk Ri 0.1 -> 0.5-0.9 as the flow lulls and the conductance dies 5-10x (|ChU| 3-8 -> 0.8-2.9 mm/s, even flipping to upward flux at 21:30); the MODEL NEVER LEAVES Ri 0.02-0.08 at the windy cells - a stable-tail cutoff would never trigger there, so that switch is downgraded from primary lever. Reading: both atmospheres run the same solar/shading-clocked transition sequence -- and the model DOES attempt the lull at exactly the observed time (its own minimum is also 20:30) but bottoms out 12x too high because its floor wind only dips to 2.6 m/s instead of 0.7. **The failure is amplitude, not phase**: the transition dynamics and timing are right; the collapse depth is not. Also note the observed 19:30 burst at unchanged wind and dT (H -5.6 -> -25.7 W/m2) -- stable-BL intermittency, which no exchange formula in either model reproduces deterministically. Figure: `plot_output/diagnostics/exchange_collapse_20250717.png/.pdf` (panel a: conductance vs time; panel b: the (Ri, conductance) trajectory with the cutoff-governed region shaded; `wrf3dpbl-diag/plot_exchange_collapse.py`). Gate: calm-cell fraction and its T2/skin cooling vs EVE1, the 20:30 lull/reversal at Kolsass, HFX toward the observed -3..-9 W/m2, and no NaN (the caps' original motivation was measured under the U3 albedo era; ping 8556715 + judge 8556716 armed).**

**2026-09-01, ~23:55 — RETRACTION of the skin–soil conductance claim (23:30 entry), on Elias's challenge (one site, one surface type, questionable plate): the plate-free SEB closure shows the OBSERVED surface also draws ≈ 49 W m⁻² from ground+storage at night (Rnet −53…−58 from the 4-component radiometer, EC heat flux −3…−9 ⇒ residual −48.7…−49.3), the same magnitude as the model's `GRDFLX` −44…−53. The corrected skin budget (good instruments only, 21–22 UT): both surfaces run the same ground supply and near-identical LW_in; the model's skin balances 1.5–2 K warmer because it receives 3–6× more heat from the air (−HFX +16…+35 vs observed +3…+9 W m⁻²) — the mechanically coupled, too-windy floor handing heat downward. **The two-lock picture collapses to one measured, air-side lock: the surface exchange that will not die at moderate Ri, on a floor kept moving by the resolved slab flow (which feeds back on the warm pool).** This leg is the multi-site one (all seven i-Box stations, floor and slopes, QC-filtered), answering the single-site objection. Fix candidates now: (1) the sfclay stable-exchange cutoff switch (default-off, needs Elias's soundness review), (2) above-z1 vertical refinement (structural; breaks restart compatibility).
